18 / Reproduce and inspect

Frozen evidence, validation and source history

The selected four-family bundle contains all four checkpoints, frozen body/runtime models, references, controller schemas, training source snapshots, logs, plots, matched evaluation summaries, all trial rollouts and videos. Its manifest records SHA256 hashes and source jobs. The older jab bundle remains available as historical evidence.

At this milestone, 133 project tests and four subtests passed. The Torch-dependent module was skipped in the preview environment; its nine learning tests passed separately with the pinned physics environment. Ruff and shell syntax checks passed. The packaging guide records its additional schema checks and real release preflight. Final learned and paired-baseline videos are fully decoded; contact, recovered-guard and baseline-fall stills are visually reviewed. Media validation records accompany the bundle.

The bundle README provides exact evaluation commands. New experiments use the current server's tested V100 launcher:

sbatch scripts/train_punch_5ms_volta.sbatch \
  --reference outputs/scalar-four-reference-v1/jab/jab.npz \
  --num-envs 128 --updates 200 --save-every 25

This command starts a new experiment, not a reproduction of the accepted checkpoint's full training lineage. Frozen training arguments and initialization records preserve that lineage.

Independent audit commands are in the bundle README. The GPU guide covers allocation, environment and EGL mapping. Do not run the checkpoint with different gains, body semantics or clocks and call it the same evaluated controller.

The compact HTML uses relative workspace links. A separate standalone HTML embeds the selected images and videos for offline viewing; raw artifact and source-code links still require the project workspace. Both editions contain the full text of the 26 historical Markdown reports below. No external fonts, analytics or CDN assets are needed.

The complete historical report archive

These are the original reports, reproduced in full. Their dates and claims belong to their respective stage; the main narrative above reconciles the current state.

00 · Scope and implementation plan

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Scope and implementation plan

Date: 2026-10-10. All development and persistent generated files belong to human-punching. ../robust_sav, ../mjlab, the PDFs, and ../DuoBox are read-only inputs. No protagonist weights or old output datasets are required.

Deliverables

  1. Audit the actual source revisions and dependency locks against the Task 09 plan, including the change from joint-torque disturbance to a human adversary.
  2. Read the four existing, hash-pinned DuoBox selections: jab, cross, left hook, right hook. Retain both arms, spine, legs, and root, rather than just the attacking glove.
  3. Build a visible articulated human triangle mesh with a measured neutral crown-to-sole height of 1.80 m; preserve source bone proportions and local joint rotations. Keep its morphology/provenance explicit.
  4. Export MuJoCo MJCF, meshes, numerical trajectories and provenance; render four videos with two camera views, real-time and slow replay.
  5. Verify source integrity, forward kinematics, height, quaternion validity, clip-local derivatives, and output media. Document limitations and the next integration steps.

Chosen initial implementation

The same native MuJoCo version used by robust_sav is 3.11.0. This preview needs neither PyTorch nor a training checkpoint. The installed sibling mjlab source is newer than the revision pinned by robust_sav; combining their current requirements directly is incompatible. See the dependency report.

The supplied fitted SMPL-X files contain parameters but no mesh topology or neutral model. To make a reproducible preview without requiring a licensed model download, this milestone authors a procedural, segmented human mesh on the raw 21-joint DuoBox rig. This follows the plan's initial articulated-surface approach but substitutes the body asset. It is not an SMPL-X reconstruction and does not claim subject-specific shape fidelity. Exact uniform height scaling preserves the source proportions, relative joint motion, root displacement and timing; it does not solve biomechanical constrained IK.

The preview writes free-root and ball-joint configurations and calls MuJoCo forward kinematics. It is kinematic motion replay, not a learned or dynamically stabilized human controller. Targeting the G1, TTC adaptation, collision calibration, imitation training and the two-player learner remain later milestones.

Execution environment

The connected Supercloud login hosts expose xeon-g6-volta, xeon-p8, and related partitions. mit_normal_gpu and L40S were not listed by sinfo -a. Do not substitute another GPU type silently. Render this lightweight preview with native MuJoCo's OSMesa CPU backend; provide a separate Slurm worker using the requested mit_general / mit_normal_gpu / l40s:1 resources for the target cluster.

Sandboxed shared-filesystem directory creation stalled across reconnects; direct authorized execution restored writes. Installation and all generated artifacts stay under this project. A failed temporary runtime installation used /tmp and was abandoned when that temporary filesystem filled; it is not part of the project runtime.

01 · Task 09 compatibility and implementation assessment

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Task 09 compatibility and implementation assessment

Audit date: 10 October 2026. All inspection of ../../robust_sav and ../../mjlab was read-only. This project must keep all new environments, code, downloads, caches, outputs and reports under human-punching.

Decision

The proposed human opponent is feasible as a staged research implementation. Motion retargeting and MuJoCo rendering are a sensible first deliverable. The complete Task 09 plan is not a drop-in extension of the accepted Task 08 application: it needs a physical human tracker, a two-entity simulation adapter, a constrained planner interface, and a new training/replay schema. No existing checkpoint is needed to construct and inspect the isolated human preview.

Use MuJoCo 3.11.0, the same engine version recorded by Task 08. For subsequent mjlab integration, use the Task 08 source commit, not the sibling mjlab checkout's current HEAD. Keep the preview environment small; adding SMPLSim, Mink and WBC is unnecessary to render an initial articulated human reference. A kinematic replay is a reference/debugging artifact, not evidence that a torque-controlled person can balance, execute the punches or satisfy adversarial timing constraints.

The user has narrowed this iteration to four source punch families on a 1.80 m human mesh, without G1 targeting. That is a subset of milestone 09A and the kinematic portion of 09B. The full target/TTC, physical imitation and adversarial training stages remain future work.

Audited local sources

  • RobustSafeArrival-Vision5.pdf: finite-game theory, Task 08 implementation/closure, and accepted simulator and observation contracts.
  • Task09_Human_Adversary_Implementation_Plan.pdf: proposed architecture and gates, dated 10 October 2026.
  • robust_sav HEAD: f4672e3ab403308520beb6c6476a4027096764bf.
  • mjlab working-tree HEAD: 033ae22a2c7a30a25a6fa77b16c113ed88dd1b55.
  • Task 08 mjlab pin, independently repeated in src/boxing/standing/schemas.py, src/boxing/stance/plant.py, src/boxing/snapshots/restoration.py and configs/boxing/external_inputs.json: 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa.

The pinned mjlab commit is present in the sibling repository's Git object database. Its files can be read or exported without checking out or changing that repository. The version string mjlab==1.6.0 alone does not identify the accepted source: both inspected commits declare that version with different dependencies.

Dependency comparison

Component Accepted robust_sav environment Pinned mjlab 8ee51fbc Sibling HEAD 033ae22a Consequence
Python >=3.10,<3.14 >=3.10,<3.14 >=3.10,<3.15 Python 3.11 is in the common range.
PyTorch Package requires 2.8.0; lock records 2.8.0+cu128 >=2.7.0; CUDA 12.8 extra >=2.14.0; CUDA 13.0 extra Current HEAD conflicts with robust_sav's exact Torch pin.
MuJoCo 3.11.0 ~=3.11.0 ~=3.11.0 Same engine family; pin the patch version for reproduction.
MuJoCo-Warp 3.11.0 ~=3.11.0 ~=3.11.0 Suitable eventual shared GPU backend.
Warp 1.14.0 >=1.14.0 >=1.14.0 Preserve 1.14.0 for initial integration.
RSL-RL 5.5.0 ==5.5.0 ==5.5.1 Another reason to avoid installing sibling HEAD.
NumPy 2.5.3 in standing lock Unbounded declaration Unbounded declaration WBC introduces a conflicting upper bound; see below.
OpenCV opencv-python-headless==4.11.0.86 Not a core dependency Not a core dependency Added by the perception overlay; not needed for basic replay.

Evidence: robust_sav package, standing environment lock, perception overlay, external source pin, and git -C ../../mjlab show 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa:pyproject.toml.

The standing lock is an installed-environment inventory, rather than a universal platform-independent solver guarantee. Its scientific packages and historical CUDA build should not be silently updated in the accepted project. This assessment did not recreate or execute Task 08 training; it compares the checked-in source and dependency records.

WBC is an optional later dependency, with real reconciliation work

The plan names WBC-mjlab revision 689f0836. Its official package declaration, retrieved during this audit, requires mjlab>=1.6.0 and numpy<2.5. That conflicts with the accepted robust_sav lock's NumPy 2.5.3. The comment attributes the bound to a mediapy problem; this audit has not independently reproduced that problem.

Its official lock resolves mjlab 1.6.0, MuJoCo/MuJoCo-Warp 3.11.0, Warp 1.14.0, RSL-RL 5.4.2, and platform/extra-dependent Torch entries including 2.11.0+cu128 and 2.12.1. These are not the accepted Task 08 dependency versions. The RSL-RL version is a lock resolution, not an explicit direct WBC requirement.

Therefore do not apply WBC's lock to robust_sav or assume a common package version proves API compatibility. A future isolated tracker environment should constrain the exact accepted mjlab source, Torch 2.8.0+cu128, MuJoCo/MuJoCo-Warp 3.11.0, Warp 1.14.0 and RSL-RL 5.5.0, then resolve a suitable NumPy below 2.5 and test WBC's extension and training APIs against that combination. This is a candidate compatibility route, not a tested dependency lock. Training and integration may initially use separate environments with versioned motion/checkpoint handoff. Native mjlab tracking remains the smaller-dependency fallback.

SMPLSim/Mink are offline construction tools, not requirements that must enter the accepted G1 runtime. Their additional dependencies and model licenses can stay in separate conversion environments if needed. SMPL-X model weights and source motion access are separate from open-source exporter code; an open exporter does not imply that the body-model files are present or freely redistributable.

What transfers from the plan, and what needs adjustment

The plan's separation into a reference builder, a physical tracker and a boundary-only planner is sound. It prevents the high-level adversary from modifying an outward punch after commitment and gives physical tracking a separately testable contract. It also correctly identifies full-cycle source motion, both wrists, torso, legs and recovery as necessary data. An outward-only glove curve cannot reconstruct this information.

The first mesh need not be a seamless SMPL-X skin. The plan itself proposes articulated rigid mesh segments first. A procedural 1.80 m articulated mesh can exercise the motion-to-MJCF mapping and produce inspectable videos without restricted body-model assets. It must be described as that fallback, not as an SMPL-X reconstruction. Standing height refers to the neutral model; a bent-knee boxing posture naturally has a lower head.

Native MuJoCo skins are visualization-only, with collision handled separately. A successful native render does not establish support in the MuJoCo-Warp camera path. Prefer ordinary articulated mesh geoms with simple collision proxies for the first shared-scene model; test geometry occlusion and red-glove masks in the actual Task 08 GPU renderer before adopting any seamless skin. See the MuJoCo skin specification and MuJoCo-Warp project.

SMPL axis-angle rotations, source skeleton joint orientations and MuJoCo scalar hinge positions are different representations. The final exporter must explicitly declare joint ordering, axes, rest frames and rotation conventions, and validate forward kinematics. A proposed 63-hinge human is not automatically supplied by importing SMPL-X or SMPLSim. Reference velocities must be derived within each clip; quaternion interpolation/sign handling cannot be replaced by component-wise interpolation.

For this preview, the important acceptance evidence is source provenance, declared timing/units/frame conversion, the fixed 1.80 m target morphology, four visible and correctly handed punch motions, and successful MJCF compilation/rendering. It does not require optimizing reach to a G1 head, freezing a head target, matching a requested TTC or demonstrating physical guard recovery. Source data availability and actual delivered replay fidelity are documented separately in the implementation report.

The current Task 08 application is not already a human-adversary game

The Task 09 PDF cites several older paths. In this checkout the rendering integration lives under src/boxing/perception/, not the proposed src/boxing/vision/plant.py.

Current source Relevant finding Future integration requirement
src/boxing/stance/plant.py Pins mjlab source; physics 0.005 s, decimation 4, control 0.02 s; the application requires allocated CUDA. Preserve those rates and the accepted G1 plant contract.
src/boxing/training/plant.py Stance-energy and state operations use whole-model qpos/qvel; previous action comes from the environment action manager. Gather G1 state/actions by entity mapping and scatter reset state only to the intended entity.
src/boxing/perception/plant.py sync_fists synchronizes scheduled fist centers with renderer geometry. Use hand-attached physical glove poses consistently for rendering, contacts and safety labels.
src/boxing/perception/rendered_plant.py Builds the Task 08 camera extension and verifies 0.08 m glove radii. Include human-body occlusion and explicit collision filtering while preserving camera/latency semantics initially.
src/boxing/training/residual_learning.py Rejects enabled disturbances with Residual learning requires null disturbance; critics require two 29-vectors. New human planner/action/replay adapter; do not repurpose the 29 zero torque-disturbance channels without a new schema.
src/rsav/learner.py Generic learner supports configurable bounded adversary modules and an adversary optimizer. Reuse the SAV objective/update ideas, with correct legal-action and decision-boundary handling.

The accepted actor has 948 inputs, critic observation 2,011, and Q input 2,069 = 2,011 + 29 + 29. The final 29-vector is currently a zero disturbance. A human plan index is neither that vector nor a joint torque disturbance. Retaining the protagonist actor's input schema is useful for warm starting, but the new opponent's appearance, occlusion and sequence horizon still require reevaluation and likely fine-tuning.

The accepted document also explicitly says delay/pending packets are simulator state absent from its critic, leaving the retained critic partially observed. A new centralized critic should include enough human state, committed plan, phase, sequence remainder, time, reference and sensor-buffer state to justify any Markov claim. Human and G1 action histories and reset procedures must be separate.

Two additional game-design details
  1. A discrete planner does not fit the existing continuous adversary gradient unchanged. Learner.update_adversary differentiates Q through a bounded adversary output. An integer clip selector/argmin breaks that path. For the first small finite action bank, explicitly evaluate/minimize over legal plans at boundaries, or introduce a documented categorical/planner learner. Continuous bounded plan deformation can use a differentiable actor later, but requires renewed feasibility checks.
  2. Decision masking must apply to targets as well as rollout actions. Between legal boundaries the transition uses the committed plan, with no new planner choice. Replay and target evaluation must retain that held plan rather than compute a fresh minimizer at every physics/control tick. A per-tick expanded state with commitment/phase is the simplest starting point. A boundary-only transition representation would instead need correct duration-dependent discounting and intermediate first-event handling.

The general theory is not intrinsically restricted to torque disturbances: the manuscript formulates x_next = F(x, u, d) with an admissible adversary action set. A human planner can instantiate d if the expanded state/transition and legality rules are specified. However, its exact finite stationary deterministic-game assumptions do not directly certify a continuous, noisy, partially observed neural-policy simulation. Restricted legal plans define the robustness class being measured; invalid attacks must be accounted for separately rather than counted as protagonist wins or losses without a declared rule.

Simulator and compute scope

A native MuJoCo 3.11.0 kinematic video uses the accepted engine and MJCF representation, but does not run the accepted mjlab/MuJoCo-Warp batched physics and camera pipeline. That distinction must remain explicit in the preview README and evidence. The current Task 08 application's CUDA requirement is not a reason to change its source or claim CPU preview equivalence. GPU import, physical stepping and camera checks belong to the later integration validation.

Follow the existing Slurm pattern: run GPU work inside an explicit allocation, honor scheduler-assigned CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, set EGL for rendering, and keep caches/output project-local. The requested target is mit_normal_gpu with one L40S. The root-session cluster discovery for this run reported a different exposed Slurm installation without that partition/GPU type; no successful L40S execution should be claimed unless an actual allocation and device inventory verify it. A job script can preserve the requested target while local CPU rendering produces preliminary visual evidence.

Proposed next stages after the preview

  1. Reference quality: acquire/verify complete source recordings, annotate full cycles and splits, compare source/target wrists and feet, then decide whether SMPL-X morphology and Mink IK improve the preview enough to justify their extra assets/dependencies.
  2. Physical human: define biomechanically plausible limits/inertias/effort bounds and collision proxies; implement one clip tracker without root assistance; measure balance, feet, wrist orientation and full recovery before scaling to four families.
  3. Target/TTC and sequences: add validated immutable plans, continuous-time sampling, physically measured moving-guard completion and all ordered family-pair transitions. The plan's numeric gates are proposed targets, not achieved results.
  4. Two entities: implement the adapter in this project first, preserve G1-specific state/action indexing, replace scheduled fists with actual gloves, and evaluate a frozen/random valid human-plan distribution.
  5. Adversary: version the critic/replay/action schemas, implement the legal boundary-only selector and matched fixed-random comparison, then consider expanded planner freedom.

No changes to robust_sav are authorized by this assessment. The eventual plug-in contract can be developed in human-punching and proposed as a concrete integration patch only when the user requests that integration.

02 · DuoBox motion source and retargeting audit

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

DuoBox motion source and retargeting audit

Date: 2026-10-10. This report concerns the input motion adapter. All source reads are from the sibling DuoBox and robust_sav trees; all development is inside human-punching.

Available source and selected events

The supplied DuoBox/fbx_export_joints recordings contain scalar NumPy dictionaries. For the selected recordings, ma_position and liu_position are arrays shaped (frames, 21, 3); the corresponding rotation arrays are (frames, 21, 3, 3). Both actors are present in each file. The attacking actor for these four selected events is ma; pairing order must not determine actor identity.

The selections are inherited exactly from robust_sav/configs/boxing/punch_crops.json, including file hashes, inclusive source frame bounds and anatomical hand. That configuration explicitly marks these as machine-selected candidates pending visual and human label review. This project preserves that status rather than promoting the labels to validated ground truth.

Preview name Source filename under fbx_export_joints Inclusive frames Samples Elapsed motion Anatomical hand / wrist joint
jab 000_ma_jab_liu_defense_joints.npy 370–518 149 1.2333 s left / 8
cross 003_ma_cross_liu_defense_joints.npy 223–367 145 1.2000 s right / 12
left_hook 006_ma_leadhook_liu_shanbi_joints.npy 401–563 163 1.3500 s left / 8
right_hook 007_ma_rearhook_liu_shanbi_joints.npy 463–620 158 1.3083 s right / 12

The frame convention is 120 Hz, inherited from the existing robust_sav source audit. No timestamps or per-file rate metadata are stored in these NumPy dictionaries, so this is a source-code convention, not an independently calibrated clock. Source positions are interpreted in metres based on the inherited convention and measured human-scale limbs.

The crops include the machine-annotated outward motion and recovery to a candidate guard reacquisition. Optional padding_s=0.25 adds 30 real recorded frames at each end, producing 209, 205, 223 and 218 samples respectively. Padding never invents a pose; it is clipped to recording bounds. The final pose is not forcibly matched to the initial pose, so concatenating the clip into an endless seamless loop is not claimed.

Coordinate and rotation checks

Hips is joint 0. Positions use a right-handed world with +z up. In the skeleton's neutral local frame, +y is up, +x is anatomical left and +z is forward. The hierarchy is:

Hips
├── Spine → Spine1 → Neck → Head
│           ├── LeftShoulder → LeftArm → LeftForeArm → LeftHand
│           └── RightShoulder → RightArm → RightForeArm → RightHand
├── LeftUpLeg → LeftLeg → LeftFoot → LeftToeBase
└── RightUpLeg → RightLeg → RightFoot → RightToeBase

Only the root's rotation is directly a world rotation. Every child's stored rotation is parent-relative. Column-vector composition is therefore

R_world[root] = R_source[root]
R_world[j] = R_world[parent[j]] @ R_source[j]
p_world[j] = p_world[parent[j]] + R_world[parent[j]] @ rest_offset[j]

The adapter recovers each constant rest offset as the component-wise median of R_world[parent].T @ (p_child - p_parent) across the selected frames. Treating the child matrices as world rotations instead produces maximum reconstruction errors of 1.64–1.76 m on these crops. Correct composition reconstructs the supplied positions to submicrometre precision:

Punch Maximum reconstructed joint error Maximum rest-offset deviation
jab 2.865 × 10⁻⁷ m 3.151 × 10⁻⁷ m
cross 2.902 × 10⁻⁷ m 3.500 × 10⁻⁷ m
left_hook 2.913 × 10⁻⁷ m 3.260 × 10⁻⁷ m
right_hook 3.281 × 10⁻⁷ m 2.911 × 10⁻⁷ m

Stored matrices have small export noise: maximum entrywise orthogonality error is approximately 1.36 × 10⁻⁶. Conversion to normalized quaternions projects this noise onto proper rotations. The source adapter retains the recorded matrices and exposes numerical diagnostics so later replay tolerances can distinguish export noise from frame-convention errors.

For actor ma, measured neutral segment lengths are approximately 0.500378 m thigh, 0.421891 m shin, 0.303919 m upper arm and 0.251426 m forearm. Summing neutral offsets places the head joint at local y=0.623897 m relative to the pelvis, feet at y=−0.922269 m and toes at y=−0.987408 m. These are joint locations, not scalp/sole vertices; exact 1.80 m body height must be measured from the generated body surface. The mesh builder applies one uniform scale to the body, source offsets and root displacement after accounting for scalp and sole geometry. It does not independently shrink arms, lower a fist, or aim at the G1.

Data contract and integrity

motion.py exports JOINT_NAMES, PARENTS, FPS, CROP_SPECS, MotionClip, load_crop, compose_global_rotations and forward_kinematics. MotionClip exposes original positions, local and global rotation matrices, recovered rest offsets, source frame numbers, relative sample times and provenance metadata. active_hand_index is the 21-joint skeleton index (8 or 12), whereas the inherited configuration also has a separate two-hand-array index (0 or 1).

The raw files are pickle-backed, so the loader verifies the SHA256 of the exact source bytes against the inherited allowlist before enabling NumPy pickle loading. Loading uses those same verified bytes; replacement files fail before deserialization. Derived numeric archives can be loaded with allow_pickle=False. The adapter does not import robust_sav, Torch, SMPL or any dataset preprocessing/training stack.

Punch Verified SHA256
jab 7ed040b3b727baa108b5090872fab864d684ff1152031a746da07b6b260e17a3
cross 4295973d6348916cd820d8e3de1ff091af6eaf25b40b5d4c81ad4f2e6282f39f
left_hook 790fb445dd9dd002756f9902f29730e69a40de8259a8c6add7bf4bd3f48de23b
right_hook 86e378bfbd31779fa65ceac9d4d26d3d9bc0c3aedca74eebe0b027f9b4dfcfb3

Validation performed

Six tests in tests/test_motion.py passed using the project environment. They verify nontrivial parent rotation/child translation behavior, hierarchy and hand indices, input validation, rejection of modified input before pickle loading, reconstruction of all four real source crops, and exact preservation of the original frames under optional context padding. Source-dependent tests skip clearly when the external DuoBox directory is unavailable; they do not claim validation on absent data.

PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_motion.py -v

This validates a motion source and its geometric replay contract. It does not validate punch style labels, biomechanical joint limits, contact forces, dynamically stable tracking, perception realism, target adaptation or adversarial policy learning. Those remain separate integration stages.

03 · Implementation and results

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Implementation and results

Date: 2026-10-10. This report covers the isolated motion-retargeting preview, not physical adversary training.

Delivered implementation

src/human_punching/motion.py loads the four exact, hash-pinned raw DuoBox selections inherited from robust_sav. It includes both arms, all 21 source joints, the root trajectory and 120 Hz timing. The default preview adds 0.25 seconds of existing recorded context on either side. It does not fabricate guard poses or close the sequence into a loop. Family and outward-completion annotations are inherited machine-selected candidates, still subject to human review.

src/human_punching/human.py generates a plain light-blue mannequin from triangle meshes. It has a featureless oval head, smooth torso/limbs and one solid mesh per foot. Facial features, hair, clothing details, muscle overlays and separate visible toes have been removed. The left glove is blue; the right is red. Both are flattened rounded blocks with a pale orientation mark; all glove geometry rotates rigidly with its corresponding Hand body. The source wrist rotations are preserved; see the glove audit. These colors are review aids, not the accepted Task 08 perception appearance contract. The body is a segmented rigid surface, as permitted for the initial mesh milestone in the Task 09 plan. It is not SMPL-X and is not a reconstruction of the capture participant. No external human model or trained weights are required.

The target is scaled so its neutral T-pose crown-to-sole mesh extent is exactly 1.800000 m. This measures actual compiled mesh vertices, including the featureless head and solid feet. A bent-knee boxing posture is naturally shorter. Mesh counts and the updated normalization scale are reported below. The source skeleton still includes its toe joints to preserve the recorded joint schema, but those joints have no visible geometry.

src/human_punching/retarget.py transfers the complete motion to the generated MJCF. For each joint, the source parent-local rotation becomes a MuJoCo ball-joint quaternion. Parent-local bone offsets and world root displacement use the same constant scale as the mesh. The first root-forward direction defines one world yaw alignment for the entire clip. A fixed planar translation centers the initial root. A fixed vertical translation places the lowest mesh vertex over the complete clip at 2 mm above the z=0 reference plane. The rendered floor is at z=-3 mm. There is no per-frame floor clamp, root stabilization or hand-only target correction.

This is a faithful scaled replay on a compatible rig, not constrained IK onto a differently proportioned anatomical model. It preserves the source proportions and joint motion. Solving a constrained retargeting problem becomes necessary if those proportions, DOFs, joint limits, contacts or physical goals change.

src/human_punching/render.py renders the actual MJCF and poses with native MuJoCo 3.11.0. Each video has an oblique view and a side view, a real-time pass and a half-speed pass. A short yellow trail marks the active wrist. Text labels and the progress line are added after rendering; the body, geometry and camera views come from MuJoCo. No image-generation tool was used. The camera remains fixed within each clip.

Output contract

outputs/preview/model/human.xml references relative OBJ assets under model/meshes. It loads directly in MuJoCo 3.11.0. human_metadata.json records exact scale, hierarchy, target offsets, mesh counts and morphology limitations.

Each punch directory contains:

  • <punch>.mp4: 1280 × 720, H.264, 30 fps; real-time then half-speed replay, with a 0.3-second hold after each pass.
  • <punch>.png: still at the inherited outward-completion source frame.
  • trajectory.npz: 120 Hz samples with time_s, original source_frames, qpos, qvel, body_names, body_pos_w, body_quat_w, body_lin_vel_w and body_ang_vel_w.
  • manifest.json: source hashes/crop bounds, orientation/origin/scale/floor transforms, FK errors, bounds, wrist speed and media metadata.

The root manifest.json records runtime versions, the full joint layout, and SHA256 hashes for the XML and every referenced OBJ. It also contains the four motion manifests. overview.png is the four-video visual index.

The rig has one free root and 20 ball joints: 87 qpos coordinates and 66 velocity coordinates. Quaternions are scalar-first wxyz; body arrays follow the explicit 21-name list and exclude the world body. qvel uses MuJoCo's tangent-space convention via mj_differentiatePos. Body linear velocities use clip-local finite differences; body angular velocities use relative rotations in the world frame. Quaternion signs remain continuous within each clip.

These arrays are not WBC's scalar-hinge joint_pos format or an accepted robust_sav checkpoint. The next exporter must adapt to the final physical human's DOFs and recompute body arrays from that exact model. No derivatives are taken across different clips.

Compute and reproducibility

The default outputs/preview artifacts were generated using CPU OSMesa, with no learned controller. Subsequent native GPU rendering on this server’s Volta partition is documented in the GPU server guide, with separate job-specific outputs. outputs/compute_inventory.txt records available Slurm partitions; this connected cluster does not expose the requested mit_normal_gpu / L40S resources. scripts/render_l40s.sbatch preserves the requested target account, partition and GPU selection, checks the actual GPU name, and uses EGL. That script has not been executed on an L40S here.

This is the same native MuJoCo engine version and MJCF asset format used by robust_sav. It is not an execution test of the batched mjlab/MuJoCo-Warp physics or raycast camera implementation. Rigid mesh surfaces were chosen in part to avoid depending on native-only deformable skins, but actual GPU visibility/occlusion must still be checked.

Run bash scripts/render_cpu.sh from this project to regenerate the outputs. requirements-preview.lock records the tested Python 3.12 package environment. The README includes isolated installation and the target-cluster Slurm command. All persistent project files, environments, caches and outputs are inside human-punching.

Validation and limitations

The test suite covers real source hashes and whole-body reconstruction, rejection of corrupt inputs before pickle decoding, recorded-context padding, exact compiled mesh height, the full joint layout, scaled offset preservation, MuJoCo FK for all four families, quaternion continuity, root motion, the constant floor offset and clip-local tangent-space derivatives. Additional glove tests rotate each wrist independently about all three axes and compare the full hand/glove orientation to the recorded source on every frame. The measured source-to-MuJoCo FK errors are reported below. Test output is saved in outputs/tests.txt.

The XML deliberately has no actuators, no gravity and no collision participation. Its nonzero inertias are structural placeholders. Rendering calls mj_forward on recorded configurations, not a physical tracking policy. The result therefore establishes visual and kinematic fidelity only. Segmented surfaces may show seams or overlap, the source feet may slide or hover, and there is no biomechanical joint-limit or collision guarantee. Disablement is explicit in both model metadata and tests.

Future Task 09 work needs validated masses/inertias/joint limits/effort bounds, contact proxies and a physical tracker; a fixed glove attachment consistent with rendered and safety geometry; full-cycle annotations and transition validation; target/TTC adaptation; actual GPU rendering checks; and finally the expanded human/game state and legal boundary-only planner. The compatibility report details why the existing disturbance vector cannot simply be replaced with a punch ID.

Measured final artifacts

Simplified mesh revision: 29 mesh assets, 9,562 vertices, 19,008 triangles. Neutral standing height: 1.800000 m; uniform scale: 1.00431662.

Punch Source frames (with context) Source duration MP4 duration Maximum FK error
jab 340–548 (209) 1.733 s 5.867 s 2.91e-07 m
right hook 433–650 (218) 1.808 s 6.067 s 2.99e-07 m
left hook 371–593 (223) 1.850 s 6.200 s 3.27e-07 m
cross 193–397 (205) 1.700 s 5.767 s 3.61e-07 m

Generation took 101.8 seconds. All four MP4s passed full FFmpeg decoding, resolution/frame-count checks, and trajectory checks. All 30 XML/OBJ hashes match. Evidence: outputs/media_validation.json.

Glove rotation was checked across both hands in all 855 source frames and with independent 90° rotations about every wrist axis. No hand-to-glove rotation error was found; the flattened glove geometry and pale patch make the existing rotation visible. Detailed results are in outputs/glove_rotation_audit.json and report 04.

Validation: 22 passed, 4 subtests passed in 14.34s. Both source repositories remain unchanged.

The earlier detailed mesh/videos are retained under outputs/archive/preview-v1-detailed; the default outputs/preview now contains only the simplified mannequin.

Overview · Jab · Right hook · Left hook · Cross

04 · Glove rotation audit and simplified appearance

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Glove rotation audit and simplified appearance

Date: 2026-10-10

The glove transforms already followed the recorded hand rotations correctly. The previous rounded gloves made that rotation difficult to read visually. The revised model uses a plain light-blue mannequin and flatter, rounded gloves with a pale back-of-hand patch to make their orientation visible. The recorded wrist rotations are preserved.

What was checked

The audit replayed every saved pose from all four preview clips, including the existing 0.25 s source padding at each end: 855 frames and both hands. It compared actual MuJoCo data.xmat hand rotations to the full parent-composed DuoBox rotations after the same initial heading alignment. It then measured each rendered glove component's rotation and position relative to its hand. Checking joint positions alone would miss a dropped terminal wrist rotation; these checks inspect orientation explicitly.

The original previews gave:

Clip Padded frames Active glove maximum rotation from first frame Active wrist local rotation from first frame
Jab 209 88.00° 31.02°
Cross 205 92.25° 16.59°
Left hook 223 178.96° 23.33°
Right hook 218 159.36° 28.71°

These angles describe the largest orientation difference from the first frame, not accumulated rotation or just forearm pronation. The glove and hand have identical orientation changes. The raw local wrist rotations also vary; they are not identity placeholders.

Across both hands and every clip in the original previews:

  • Maximum hand orientation disagreement with the composed source: 3.9 × 10⁻¹² degrees.
  • Maximum change in any glove component's orientation relative to its hand: 3.1 × 10⁻¹⁴ degrees.
  • Maximum change in any glove component's position relative to its hand: 4.5 × 10⁻¹⁶ m.

These are floating-point noise. The meshes are attached directly to LeftHand and RightHand, and the hand ball-joint quaternions are populated from the corresponding source local rotations. MuJoCo recenters and rotates imported mesh assets internally, so the audit compares each geom's fixed compiled transform relative to the hand, rather than incorrectly requiring the geom and hand matrices to be identical.

Appearance changes

The updated mannequin removes eyes, hair, nose, mouth, ears, individual chest details, clothing, and separate toe surfaces. Its head is smooth and its feet are simple solid shapes. The internal source skeleton retains its original 21 joints, including toe joints, to preserve the motion/export contract; there are no visible separate toe meshes.

Each glove now has a flatter rounded shape, a cuff, and a pale patch on its back. The patch and glove share the same hand body, making roll visible without adding artificial wrist motion. Left and right gloves retain their blue and red colors for tracking the two hands.

This checks agreement with the available recorded wrist frames. The dataset does not include a detailed finger skeleton or measured glove surface, so this is not an independent calibration of a particular physical glove's orientation relative to the actor's anatomy.

Reproduction and regression coverage

From human-punching:

.venv/bin/python -B scripts/audit_glove_rotation.py
.venv/bin/python -B -m pytest tests/test_gloves.py -q

The audit reads existing preview MJCF, saved trajectories and the hash-verified DuoBox crops. It prints per-hand numerical results and fails if source orientation or rigid glove attachment exceeds the tolerances. It includes every named glove component, including the new orientation patch.

The 10 regression cases cover:

  • Independent 90° rotations about all three wrist axes, for each hand, while the forearm remains fixed. Actual transformed mesh vertices must follow the expected rigid rotation around the wrist.
  • Every padded source frame in all four clips, for both hands, including the rendered glove orientation and center after MuJoCo's mesh compilation transforms.

The six synthetic wrist tests do not require DuoBox. The four source integration tests skip when that dataset is unavailable. All tests write temporary models under the project's configured pytest cache.

All development and generated files are confined to human-punching. robust_sav is a read-only input.

Regenerated mannequin verification

All 22 project tests passed (including the 10 glove cases). All four revised videos passed complete decoding and asset-hash verification. The saved post-render audit is outputs/glove_rotation_audit.json.

Maximum hand/source rotation error: 3.88e-12 degrees. Maximum hand-relative glove orientation drift: 3.1e-14 degrees. Maximum hand-relative glove position drift: 4.04e-16 m.

05 · Using GPUs on this server

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Using GPUs on this server

Date: 2026-10-10. Scope: the isolated human-punching project.

The GPU partition on the connected server is xeon-g6-volta. Request a GPU with --gres=gpu:volta:1, following the supplied example_slurm.sh. The earlier mit_normal_gpu / L40S script targets a different cluster and does not apply here. A single allocated GPU is sufficient to render all four punch previews. The complete four-punch job was tested successfully on an allocated NVIDIA Tesla V100: 23 seconds of batch time, with 15.67 seconds inside the preview application.

Resources verified on this server

These values were read from sinfo, scontrol show partition xeon-g6-volta, and sacctmgr during this task. They describe scheduler configuration, not GPUs that are immediately free.

Setting Observed value Meaning for this project
Slurm cluster txe1 The connected cluster
GPU partition xeon-g6-volta Use this partition for batch jobs
GPU resource type gpu:volta Preserve this type in submission and job-step requests
GPUs per compute node 2 An eight-GPU request cannot fit on one node
Partition inventory 172 nodes / 344 Volta GPUs Shared cluster inventory; not this user's allowance
User/account association kyun / default_group The supplied example uses the default account
Association limit GrpTRES=node=4 Consistent with up to four GPU nodes for this association
Allowed QoS high,normal The script relies on the site's default selection
Partition wall-time limit 4-04:00:00 Preview jobs should request much less
Example environment module anaconda/Python-ML-2025a Python 3.10.14; not needed by the verified project environment

The user's stated eight GPUs is consistent with the observed four-node allowance and two GPUs per node. This is an interpretation of the current limits, not a reservation or a guarantee that eight GPUs can start immediately. Other jobs using the association consume its allowance. The normal QoS query showed no additional explicit GPU limit.

To inspect current resources and limits yourself:

sinfo --format='%P|%a|%l|%D|%G'
scontrol show partition xeon-g6-volta
sacctmgr -n -P show assoc where user="$USER" \
  format=Cluster,Account,User,Partition,QOS,GrpTRES,MaxTRES,MaxJobs,MaxSubmitJobs
sacctmgr -n -P show qos \
  format=Name,MaxTRESPerUser,MaxTRESPerJob,GrpTRES,MaxJobsPerUser,MaxSubmitJobsPerUser

--gres=gpu:volta:1 requests one GPU per allocated node. Slurm sets CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES for the allocation; GPU numbering inside a constrained job can differ from host numbering. Preserve the scheduler's assignment. Slurm GRES documentation

Render all four punches on one GPU

The existing .venv is ready to use. For a fresh checkout, first follow the README setup inside this project. Do not use a training environment from a sibling repository.

cd /home/gridsan/kyun/Azizan_Lab/HumanBox/human-punching
job_id="$(bash scripts/submit_gpu.sh)"
echo "$job_id"
squeue -j "$job_id"

submit_gpu.sh creates the log directory and submits render_volta.sbatch from the correct project directory. The worker requests one node, one task, one Volta GPU, 4 CPUs, 12 GiB RAM, and 10 minutes. It uses the existing .venv, establishes EGL rendering, then generates jab, right hook, left hook, and cross in one process. It needs neither a display server nor an Anaconda module.

Preview options can be passed after the wrapper name:

bash scripts/submit_gpu.sh --height 1.80
bash scripts/submit_gpu.sh --punches jab

The worker fixes the backend to egl and chooses a unique output directory for each job. Do not supply --no-render to this rendering worker: its completion check requires rendered media.

For job JOB_ID, the output is:

outputs/slurm/human-punch-volta-JOB_ID.log
outputs/gpu-JOB_ID/
  nvidia-smi.csv       allocated hardware information
  gpu_runtime.json    CUDA/EGL mapping and actual rendering evidence
  egl_device.env      verified EGL device selection for the preview process
  complete.json       written only after successful rendering
  preview/
    manifest.json
    overview.png
    model/
    jab/jab.mp4
    right_hook/right_hook.mp4
    left_hook/left_hook.mp4
    cross/cross.mp4

Trajectories, still images, and per-punch manifests accompany the videos. Job-specific CUDA, shader, temporary and XDG caches stay under .cache/gpu/JOB_ID. The accepted CPU previews at outputs/preview remain available.

Monitor or stop a job

Use the ID printed by submit_gpu.sh:

squeue -j "$job_id" -o '%.18i %.9P %.25j %.8T %.10M %.6D %R'
sacct -j "$job_id" --format=JobID,JobName,State,ExitCode,Elapsed,AllocTRES
cat "outputs/slurm/human-punch-volta-${job_id}.log"
cat "outputs/gpu-${job_id}/complete.json"

A finished successful job has State=COMPLETED, ExitCode=0:0, and a complete.json file. A missing job in squeue can simply mean it finished; inspect sacct and the artifacts. Logs appear when the worker starts. To cancel a job you no longer need:

scancel "$job_id"

A queued job may wait for resources, priority, or the association's node allowance. Inspect the final squeue column and scontrol show job "$job_id". Do not change GPU visibility or request unallocated devices to address queue delays.

Optional: one punch per array task

The default one-GPU job is enough for the current preview. To run the four punches as independently scheduled jobs:

array_id="$(bash scripts/submit_gpu.sh --array)"
squeue -r -j "$array_id"
sacct -j "$array_id" --format=JobID,State,ExitCode,Elapsed,AllocTRES

This submits --array=0-3%4: at most four tasks can run at once, each requesting one GPU. IDs map to 0=jab, 1=right_hook, 2=left_hook, and 3=cross. Logs use human-punch-volta-ARRAY_ID_TASK_ID.log; outputs use outputs/gpu-ARRAY_ID_TASK_ID/preview. The worker adds the selected punch argument, so each task renders exactly one punch. There is no combined overview.png because each task produces only one clip.

The array path is for independent work. It does not distribute one render across multiple GPUs. The four current clips do not need all eight GPUs. Future eight-GPU workloads must fit the site's two-GPU-per-node topology and node allowance, and implement separate processes or distributed computation explicitly.

Verify the allocated GPU is actually rendering

The worker runs gpu_preflight.py before generating videos. The preflight:

  1. Requires a Slurm GPU allocation and preserves CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES.
  2. Uses the CUDA driver API to identify logical device 0, including its physical PCI address and UUID.
  3. Matches that physical address to an EGL device; CUDA logical indices and EGL indices are distinct.
  4. Sets MUJOCO_EGL_DEVICE_ID, creates a real MuJoCo render context, and confirms that its EGL display uses the selected device.
  5. Checks the actual OpenGL vendor/renderer and rejects software rendering; renders a nonconstant 160 × 120 test image.
  6. Records the evidence in gpu_runtime.json and exports the verified selection through egl_device.env.

A successful nvidia-smi command alone is not evidence that MuJoCo used the GPU. Inspect gpu_runtime.json: status must be passed, egl_display_matches_selected_device must be true, and opengl must identify NVIDIA hardware. A mapping or context failure stops the worker before preview rendering and records the error in the JSON report.

Troubleshooting

Symptom Action
Invalid partition for mit_normal_gpu Use scripts/submit_gpu.sh and the current xeon-g6-volta worker. The L40S script belongs to another cluster.
Missing project .venv Complete the README setup in human-punching, then resubmit.
CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES missing / no CUDA GPU Use sbatch through the wrapper. Running the worker directly on the login host does not allocate a GPU.
EGL device mismatch or initialization error Read gpu_runtime.json and the job log; preserve scheduler visibility and use the supplied preflight. Do not guess an EGL index.
llvmpipe, softpipe, or another software renderer Treat the job as a failed GPU validation. The preflight rejects this case.
TIMEOUT Inspect the log for the stage that stalled. For a deliberately longer workload, adjust the worker's --time request within the partition limit.
OUT_OF_MEMORY Inspect sacct and the failing stage before increasing the worker's --mem request; host RAM and GPU memory are separate resources.
complete.json is missing Check sacct, the Slurm log and gpu_runtime.json. Completion is recorded only after the preview manifest contains rendered media.

What GPU support means for this milestone

The current preview renders the articulated light-blue mannequin with native MuJoCo 3.11.0. Its headless GPU backend is EGL, selected before importing MuJoCo. There is no desktop display or interactive viewer in a batch job. MuJoCo's Python package provides explicit OpenGL contexts for offscreen rendering. MuJoCo Python documentation

Retargeting, mj_forward, text overlays, and video encoding still include CPU work. This milestone replays recorded poses; it does not train a policy or test mjlab/MuJoCo-Warp GPU physics. Successfully rendering on a Volta GPU does not establish that the future training stack, its CUDA wheels, or the Warp camera pipeline support this hardware.

More allocated GPUs do not make the current single-process preview use them automatically. Run the four previews on one GPU unless there is enough independent work to justify parallel jobs. For future parameter sweeps, a Slurm array can limit concurrent tasks with a % suffix; each task still needs its own GPU allocation and separate output directory. Slurm job-array documentation

Environment and project boundaries

All project environments, logs, caches, models and videos belong under human-punching. The sibling robust_sav, mjlab and DuoBox inputs remain read-only. Slurm scripts must resolve the project directory from their submission configuration: Slurm may execute a spool copy of the script, so its runtime BASH_SOURCE is not a reliable way to find this repository.

The existing project .venv works on the compute node unchanged: Python 3.12.3, MuJoCo 3.11.0 and NumPy 2.5.3 were imported successfully. There is no need to load anaconda/Python-ML-2025a for this preview. The example module provides Python 3.10.14; switching to it would use a different interpreter from the tested environment. requirements-preview.lock records the Python 3.12 environment. The worker enters the project directory and uses .venv/bin/python explicitly, preserving the isolated dependencies.

Verified GPU probe

Initial probe Slurm job 5884571 completed with exit code 0 in 6 seconds on node d-12-12-1. It used the existing project environment and reported:

Check Result
Allocated GPU NVIDIA Tesla V100-PCIE-32GB
GPU memory 32,768 MiB
Driver 580.159.04
Python 3.12.3
MuJoCo 3.11.0
NumPy 2.5.3
EGL/OpenGL vendor NVIDIA Corporation
EGL/OpenGL renderer Tesla V100
Render test Valid 64 × 64 image

The scheduler supplied a GPU UUID in CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, rather than an integer, and SLURM_JOB_GPUS=0. nvidia-smi exposed only the allocated GPU at index 0. This is why the rendering setup must resolve the allocation to the corresponding EGL device instead of copying CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES directly into an integer-only EGL setting. Do not hard-code this job's UUID or assume the next allocation uses the same physical device.

Verified full four-punch run

Slurm job 5884573 completed with state COMPLETED, exit code 0:0, and 23 seconds of batch wall time on d-12-12-1. The preview application generated all four clips in 15.67 seconds. This includes retargeting, GPU rendering and video creation; it is not a controlled GPU-versus-CPU benchmark.

The production preflight passed on the allocated Tesla V100-PCIE-32GB, compute capability 7.0. Its CUDA PCI address matched the EGL device through EGL_NV_device_cuda, and the active EGL display matched that selected device. The rendered preflight image was 160 × 120 RGB with finite, nonconstant pixels. The OpenGL renderer was Tesla V100-PCIE-32GB/PCIe/SSE2, vendor NVIDIA Corporation.

Evidence: Slurm log, GPU preflight report, completion record, and preview manifest.

Rendered outputs: overview, jab, right hook, left hook, and cross.

This validates the current mannequin and punch replay in native MuJoCo EGL on this server's GPU. It does not validate physical adversary training, multi-GPU training, or the mjlab/MuJoCo-Warp camera and simulation pipeline.

Verified four-task array

Array 5884574 completed all four tasks with state COMPLETED and exit code 0:0. The scheduler placed two one-GPU tasks on each of two nodes, using four GPUs in total. Every task passed the CUDA-to-EGL physical-device check and generated its selected punch video.
