All four processes selected their CUDA logical device 0, while two required EGL device 1. This confirms why assigning EGL index 0 unconditionally would be incorrect on this server. The JSON reports include the per-node GPU UUIDs and the active-display verification.

The Slurm accounting record contains allocation details, exit codes and elapsed times for the probe, single job and array tasks. This test verifies four GPUs across two nodes; an eight-GPU run was not needed or performed.

All eight generated MP4s across the single-job and array runs passed full decoding and metadata checks: H.264, 1280 × 720, 30 fps, with the expected frame counts. Their saved trajectories passed comparison against the accepted CPU references at a 1e-12 tolerance. The combined media and trajectory validation report records per-video checks and SHA256 hashes, alongside GPU UUIDs and PCI addresses.

Standing-G1 targeting preview

The newer head-targeted preview uses the same tested Slurm allocation and EGL-device preflight:

bash scripts/submit_g1_gpu.sh

It runs human_punching.target_cli through scripts/target_g1_volta.sbatch, requesting one Volta GPU, 4 CPUs, 12 GiB RAM and 15 minutes. Logs are outputs/slurm/human-g1-target-JOBID.log; the completed scene, trajectories and four videos are under outputs/g1-gpu-JOBID/preview. This workflow includes CPU arm IK followed by native GPU rendering. It does not train a policy or run Warp physics. See the targeting report.

Physical simulation follow-up

The physics runtime report now verifies the exact pinned training stack on these V100s, including actual MuJoCo-Warp physics, cameras, and a controlled jab. Use scripts/setup_physics.sh, scripts/physics_probe_volta.sbatch, and scripts/warp_tracking_volta.sbatch for that environment. Native physical videos use scripts/physical_pilot_volta.sbatch; their GPU allocation accelerates rendering while the native solver runs on the CPU. The Warp worker explicitly runs GPU dynamics.

06 · Task 09: limitations, revised phases, and implementation gaps

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Task 09: limitations, revised phases, and implementation gaps

Assessment date: 10 October 2026. This is a proposed roadmap, not a claim that the later phases have been implemented or validated.

This report preserves the early preview-stage assessment. For current physical results see report 13; for the original SMPLSim/SOMA body plan, implemented prototype, and revised training milestones see report 15. Simple blue appearance does not determine the choice of physical rig.

All implementation belongs in human-punching. robust_sav, mjlab, and DuoBox remain read-only inputs. In particular, the Task 09 PDF's suggested src/boxing/human/ additions should initially become modules under this project's src/human_punching/; changing the accepted robust_sav requires a separate explicit instruction.

1. Recommendation

The plan is feasible as a staged research project, but it is considerably larger than “plug a human policy into robust_sav.” Its separation into reference construction, a low-level human tracker, and a constrained adversarial planner is worth keeping. Its named software stack and all its proposed numerical thresholds should not be treated as prerequisites or established results.

My recommended first physical milestone is one 1.80 m blue mannequin executing one head-targeted jab, at one feasible TTC, returning to guard under bounded actuation in the actual mjlab environment. Before expensive tracker training, run a cheap combined G1/human scene test to expose camera, entity indexing, runtime, and GPU compatibility problems. Then expand to four families, short sequences, and adversarial selection.

There is also a useful intermediate result: a planner can choose between committed kinematic human reference motions and form a two-player game without learning a torque-level human controller. That measures G1 robustness to the declared motion bank. It does not demonstrate human balance, effort feasibility, or physical impact response. Keep this as a debugging baseline and possible narrower research milestone; retain the physical tracker for the full Task 09 objective. A controller is necessary for physical execution, but it need not be learned if a bounded feedback controller actually passes the same tests.

The most important change to the PDF is ordering: discover shared-scene and runtime problems early, rather than after imitation training. This follows Vision5's Task 07b matched-state checks and Task 08 P1 interface/pilot work before P3 training. Optional extensions should have their own phase, as the optional ZED SDK work does in Task 08.

2. What we have actually established

Area Verified state What it does not establish
Human visualization Featureless light-blue 1.800000 m mannequin; four DuoBox punches; full hand/glove rotation checked across 855 frames. An anatomical or dynamically validated human model.
Motion transfer Full 21-joint source hierarchy; original 120 Hz convention; maximum FK error about 3.61e-7 m. Constrained retargeting to different proportions, foot support, or achievable motor commands. The rig preserves source proportions.
Human dynamics Free root plus 20 ball joints; 87 qpos and 66 qvel coordinates. There are no actuators, gravity/contact participation are disabled, and inertias are placeholders.
Data Four hash-verified candidate crops, one per family, all using actor ma, with recorded context padding. Reviewed guard-to-guard labels, independent source splits, broad style coverage, or generalization.
Rendering/compute Native MuJoCo 3.11.0 CPU replay and NVIDIA EGL replay; one full GPU job and a four-task array passed. mjlab/MuJoCo-Warp physics, its camera pipeline, a tracker, or SAV training on this server.
G1 integration Current source, dependency records, and contracts inspected. An executed combined scene or restored accepted G1 policy. The trained bundles/reset artifacts are not supplied in this checkout.

Evidence: motion audit, implementation/results, glove audit, and GPU guide. The visual preview portion is complete; the physical asset portion of the PDF's milestone 09A is not.

This review uses robust_sav commit f4672e3ab403308520beb6c6476a4027096764bf. Its accepted mjlab source is 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa, whereas the sibling working-tree HEAD is 033ae22a2c7a30a25a6fa77b16c113ed88dd1b55. Vision5's final Task 08 closure includes the verified warm continuations; this assessment does not reopen that accepted task. The accepted simulation results still do not constitute hardware validation or a formal safety certificate.

3. Main limitations of the Task 09 plan

3.1 The physics and tracking problem is the largest research uncertainty

A mesh exporter does not provide a balanced boxer. A person punching toward the shorter G1 must coordinate pelvis, knees, torso, feet, and both arms. Lowering a recorded wrist or speeding up a clip can produce unreachable, slipping, or torque-infeasible motion. The current constant whole-clip floor offset only makes a replay clear the floor; it does not solve contact constraints.

The PDF correctly mentions masses, limits, PD gains, and unassisted tracking, but these require substantial new work. Begin with a free, unactuated root, suitable floor contacts, plausible inertias and bounded joint actuation. A fixed pelvis or external root wrench may help debugging, but cannot count as an accepted freely standing physical boxer. Joint limits and torque bounds define a simulation surrogate, not validated human biomechanics by themselves.

3.2 Target adaptation and TTC are coupled to physical feasibility

The proposed 0.50/0.65/0.80 s TTCs are pilot candidates. We have not established that all four families can hit all proposed head locations at these times and then recover. Time compression increases required velocity and acceleration; broad independent sampling of target and TTC will include infeasible combinations.

Build a small admissible bank conditioned on starting guard, placement, family, and TTC. Reject unavailable combinations explicitly. At launch freeze the world target and outward plan; the low-level controller may correct its own tracking error, but must not chase a subsequently moving head. Check reference contact time, actual glove contact with the frozen diagnostic head, and contact with the moving G1 as three distinct measurements. The PDF's §4 distinction should be retained.

3.3 The initial data is much smaller than the proposed training/evaluation program

The four current events are enough to debug a retargeter and overfit a tracking pilot. They do not support claims about arbitrary punches, people, guards, or style. Their inherited labels still require review. Return to a plausible final frame is not proof of measured physical guard recovery.

Inspect additional full recordings already available in DuoBox, annotate both hands and feet, and split by recording before cropping, retiming, or augmentation. Use person-disjoint tests only where the data permits. Repeating a clip with many simulator seeds provides execution trials, not new held-out source motion.

3.4 The proposed first planner can accidentally have no choices

PDF §8 lets the curriculum specify family, TTC, and count. Our current bank has one example per family. With those variables fixed, a selector over that bank is effectively deterministic.

Before calling an experiment adversarial selection, provide multiple legal within-family variants for at least some supported contexts: different reviewed source cycles or validated stance/trajectory variants. Alternatively, explicitly grant the adversary family or TTC choice and document the changed game. Do not silently turn a user-prescribed sequence into a freely chosen one. Record legal-set size and coverage; singleton contexts carry no adversarial choice.

3.5 Existing application code is stricter than the plan's plug-in language suggests

The PDF audited an older robust_sav revision and cites vision/plant.py; the current production path is perception/. There are hard model-size, source-provenance, runtime-location, appearance, and checkpoint checks. These are useful preservation rules for Task 08, but a new combined scene cannot satisfy all of them unchanged.

Moreover, the current production boxing learner rejects enabled disturbances and hardcodes zero disturbance in updates. Its replay stores no adversary action. The generic rsav library has adversarial-learning components, but those are not already wired into the accepted captured GPU boxing path. Integration therefore includes a new runtime/scene adapter and a versioned learning path, not just a new policy class.

3.6 A discrete, occasional decision is different from per-tick torque disturbance

A punch-plan ID is not a 29-dimensional G1 torque vector. The generic continuous adversary gradient cannot optimize a discrete argmin unchanged. For a small bank, evaluate the learned Q function for every legal candidate at a boundary and select its minimizer; a separate planner network is optional.

Commitment must apply to Bellman targets as well as rollout actions. Between boundaries the admissible planner action is the already committed plan. Minimizing over fresh punches at every target step would train against a different opponent, even if the rendered rollout looks committed. Section 7 below specifies the missing contract.

3.7 A human changes perception even if the actor still has 948 inputs

Body occlusion, guard height, glove shape, and placement alter the input distribution. Task 08's active RGB pipeline expects both gloves red; the preview's blue-left/red-right colors are review aids. The inherited appearance function also recolors all geometry before the active renderer restores only G1 materials, so the blue human needs an explicit appearance rule.

Preserve the accepted camera mount, raster, clocks, delay, and detector first. Test actual initial-guard visibility and subsequent occlusion in the production camera path. A 1.80 m person's gloves may be hard to see with the downward-facing torso camera. Search admissible placements/guards before changing camera pitch. If a camera change is required, version it and reevaluate the protagonist; it is not a free compatibility fix.

3.8 The theory permits a human adversary, but does not automatically certify this simulator

Vision5's abstract adversary in x_next = F(x, u, d) need not be joint torque. A legal committed human plan can instantiate d. The state must include the human and its controller, current plan/phase, remaining prescribed sequence, timing, and relevant history.

However, the exact theory assumes a finite stationary deterministic game. Continuous dynamics, noisy observations, neural approximation, and a restricted motion bank do not inherit that guarantee. The accepted critic is explicitly partially observed because delay/queue/fault state is absent. Adding human state alone does not repair that. Either expose sufficient privileged state to a new critic or clearly retain an approximation; a recurrent model can help estimation but is not itself proof of full observability. Report robustness to the declared valid opponent class.

4. What to keep, simplify, or defer

Plan item Recommendation Reason
Full-cycle motion, both wrists, rigid glove attachment Keep. These define the actual attack and recovery. The current glove orientation audit is useful evidence to retain.
Immutable launch plan, simulator-owned legality/count, no teleporting, invalid-attack accounting Keep. Removing these lets the adversary exploit implementation loopholes.
SMPL-X/SMPLSim as mandatory first dependencies Make optional. The approved stylized surface is sufficient. Use another body/exporter only if it solves a demonstrated physical-model problem; restricted model assets otherwise add work.
Exactly 63 scalar hinges Do not prescribe the count yet. Choose controllable anatomical DOFs and explicit axes/limits. Preserve useful torso/shoulder/forearm/wrist motion; neither the current unconstrained ball rig nor a proposed hinge count is automatically suitable.
WBC-mjlab as the default Try pinned native mjlab tracking first. It already has tracking rewards, reference machinery, and a PPO runner. WBC remains an option if a concrete capability offsets its dependency/API adaptation cost.
Mink, GMR, PyRoki, SOMA23 Treat as alternatives, not a required stack. Use one offline solver when constrained retargeting needs it. A pretrained policy from another body/framework is not a verified warm start.
Photorealism, seamless skin, face/finger/toe detail Omit. Unnecessary for this objective and inconsistent with the requested simple appearance. Simple foot contact geometry still matters physically.
Multiple heights/shapes and unrestricted footwork Defer. Each increases reach, dynamics, visibility, and validation coverage. Start with one body and restricted placement.
All 16 ordered pairs and arbitrary lists before any useful game Stage. Start with one punch, then one repeat and one mixed pair. All 16 pairs remain necessary before claiming complete four-family transition support.
Continuous plan deformation and joint-level adversarial fine-tuning Defer. They can break timing, balance, style, and commitment. A frozen tracker plus finite bank is interpretable first.
Human–G1 impact dynamics Separate later experiment. A floor-contacting physical human can threaten G1 through the accepted analytical hazards without introducing collision impulses or body-charge attacks.
Three training seeds and a large release matrix during every pilot Defer to release comparisons. Debug with bounded experiments; preserve independent seeds and uncertainty reporting for substantive claims.
CUDA graph optimization, distributed RL, using all eight GPUs Defer. Establish correct updates and one-GPU throughput first. Independent jobs can use arrays without distributed learning.

The PDF itself already labels several of these as optional/later. They are not defects in that document; the revision makes those boundaries operational and removes them from the first milestone's critical path.

5. Concrete gaps against the current software

Paths below refer to the inspected source snapshot, not proposed implementation locations.

Component Current fact / source evidence Required work in human-punching
Dependency identity Both mjlab commits call themselves 1.6.0, but sibling HEAD requires Torch >=2.14/RSL-RL 5.5.1; Task 08 pins Torch 2.8/RSL-RL 5.5.0. Audit Own a separately locked environment using the exact accepted source revision. Do not install sibling HEAD by default.
Optional WBC The previously inspected 689f0836 declaration requires NumPy <2.5; Task 08's inventory is 2.5.3. WBC APIs against the accepted pin are untested. Audit and upstream links Resolve/test separately or avoid it. A separate training environment still needs a verified checkpoint/inference handoff.
Human asset human.py uses ball joints, placeholder inertia, disabled gravity/contact, and no actuator block. New physical model/configuration, supported DOF map, bounded action law, contact proxies, and a controller. Reuse the simple visible surface where practical.
Reference schema retarget.py exports qpos/qvel and named body arrays. Native tracking expects joint_pos/joint_vel and positional body indices. Export from the final model with named mappings, correct root separation, rotation conventions and clip-local derivatives.
Reference clocks/transitions At the accepted mjlab pin, src/mjlab/tasks/tracking/mdp/commands.py advances one stored frame per environment step and writes state on clip resampling. Custom continuous-time command; direct 120 Hz playback at 50 Hz would be 2.4 times too slow. Restrict state writes to declared reset/RSI, never between executed punches.
Runtime ownership standing/runtime.py requires Python/mjlab under the robust_sav root; stance/plant.py additionally expects its cache-owned interpreter. New project-owned runtime/provenance checks. Matching package versions alone will not let an unchanged old factory run from this environment.
G1 plant dimensions stance/plant.py, lines 228–229, rejects whole-model dimensions other than 36/35/29. New adapter with explicit G1 and human qpos/qvel/actuator maps and separate histories.
Scene audit perception/scene.py, lines 110–129, permits only two extra massless glove bodies/geoms and unchanged dynamic dimensions. New combined-model audit preserving G1 entity parameters/invariants; do not bypass the old audit and claim it passed.
State/reset/energy training/plant.py operates on whole-model state in G1 energy/reset paths. G1-only gather/scatter and partial-reset tests; human resets must not overwrite G1 and vice versa.
Glove poses/appearance renderer.py restores G1 materials and makes both fists red; depth.py uses a red-only foreground. Scheduled fists are currently separate mocap objects. Actual hand-attached transforms; explicit blue-body materials; sensor-compatible glove appearance and geom-ID mapping. Preserve analytical proxy definitions.
Production learner training/learner.py, lines 30–75 and 152–153, uses/requires null disturbance. New game-aware actor/critic updates. Begin with readable uncaptured tensor code; optimize only after semantic tests.
Production replay training/replay.py, lines 15–25, has u_applied but no human action/commitment fields. New replay schema with plan/decision state, successor legal choices, sequence state and true pre-reset successors.
Termination training/collector.py combines safety, punch return, and stance energy. Sequence-gated arrival, deadlines, first-event preservation, and independent human-invalid outcomes.
Critic/checkpoints delayed_acquisition.py declares partial observability; runtime_contract.py and residual_checkpoint.py validate semantics as well as dimensions. Version Task 09 schemas; explicit protagonist weights/normalizer migration; new critics rather than pretending the old Q schema fits.
Accepted G1 artifacts Source is present; trained nominal/residual bundles and reset/stance artifacts are absent here. Restore the exact accepted assets into project-owned locations before policy comparisons. Source-only work and isolated-human pilots can proceed before that.

6. Revised phases

These phases separate interface checks, controlled pilots, training, and optional extensions. Each phase should produce its own short report, configuration/version manifest, and measurable exit evidence. The current preview remains reproducible throughout.

P0 — Visual reference baseline: completed within its stated scope

Objective: inspect four full-body motions on the approved 1.80 m mannequin.

Delivered: motion loader, source hashes, articulated meshes, trajectories, videos, glove-rotation audit, and tested native GPU rendering.

Exit evidence: correct height, FK, source timing and hand attachment. This closes the preview, not dynamic feasibility, reviewed full-cycle labels, or a playable adversary.

P1 — Runtime and shared-scene feasibility

Objective: establish that the chosen stack and combined scene can run before investing in tracking RL.

  1. Create a separate training environment inside this project using the accepted mjlab commit and compatible CUDA 12 builds.
  2. On one allocated GPU, exercise Torch forward/backward, Warp kernels, contact stepping, and the actual RGB/depth/ID camera path. Measure memory and throughput at a small batch before scaling.
  3. Build an isolated combined-scene harness with explicit entity maps, using a scripted human for this test. Check G1-only state/energy/reset/action handling and version the new scene/runtime audit. Indexing tests can use fixtures; numerical agreement with accepted stance energy requires the archived stance chart/P artifact and remains pending until that asset is restored.
  4. Render both gloves and the blue body through the Task 08 camera contract; verify occlusion and detector behavior. Restore G1 model assets if needed for compilation; trained policies are not required for matched-pose checks.

Deliverables: candidate dependency lock, GPU evidence, two-entity scene/pose renders, entity mapping, and measured resource baseline.

Gate: real Warp stepping and production-style camera output succeed; human state cannot contaminate G1 indexing; missing accepted assets are explicitly identified. A native EGL video alone does not pass. Risk: moderate engineering, with a hard hardware/runtime dependency.

P2 — Final human model and committed target references

Objective: define a physically meaningful body and a small reachable head-targeted motion bank.

  1. Keep one 1.80 m stylized body. Fix joint axes/order, limits, mass/inertia, floor contacts, actuator bounds and the hand-to-glove transform. Validate basic standing/actuator response with gravity enabled.
  2. Review full-cycle source annotations and establish recording-level splits. Start with a single jab to debug the mapping, then cover the four families without claiming generalization from four crops.
  3. Retarget to the final model. Use offline constrained IK only where necessary; preserve support feet and whole-body style. Select one feasible head placement and source-like TTC before enlarging the bank.
  4. Implement immutable plan_punch and continuous-time sample_ref, contact-time measurement, phase labels and explicit infeasibility reasons. Exercise them in the kinematic combined-scene harness.

Deliverables: physical MJCF/configuration, versioned reference bank, target/TTC feasibility table, and diagnostic videos.

Gate: correct joint/body mapping, compatible starts, no hidden root resets, complete recovery reference, coherent derivatives, correct frozen-head contact, and identical reference arrays in paired post-launch G1-motion tests. These are geometric/reference gates; physical tracking still belongs to P3. Risk: moderate to high, chiefly target/support feasibility.

P3 — One physical punch, then four-family tracking

Objective: demonstrate a freely simulated human executing and recovering under bounded feedback.

  1. Benchmark a bounded PD tracking baseline. If it cannot balance/track reliably, build a motion-conditioned residual controller using the pinned native mjlab tracking facilities; use WBC only after demonstrating a concrete advantage.
  2. Replace the default motion command with a time-aware, fixed-world command. Match training and integration observation order, reference lookahead, normalization, action scales, PD law, and clocks.
  3. Train one jab at the admitted target/TTC. Use reference-state initialization for learning, but evaluate from actual guard starts. Expand to all four families and the small feasible target/TTC bank only after the jab works.
  4. Evaluate with no external root assistance and no state overwrites during execution. Freeze accepted controller weights, normalizers, actuation settings and schema.

Deliverables: standalone controller/checkpoint, inference adapter, full configuration bundle, and a physical execution report.

Gate: measured guard-to-guard completion, balance, foot slip, joint/effort limits, glove pose and physical TTC errors meet thresholds fixed after pilots and before held-out tests. Count misses/falls/timeouts. A visually plausible animation does not pass. Risk: highest research uncertainty and likely the largest training cost.

P4 — Sequences and a fixed-opponent G1 baseline

Objective: run valid physical human attacks continuously against G1 before optimizing adversarial selection.

  1. Integrate the frozen tracker into the P1 adapter. Keep human–floor dynamics; initially disable human–G1 collision impulses while using the accepted two-glove analytical hazards. Explicitly forbid unintended body-charge/interpenetration behavior through placement/legality checks.
  2. Implement physical guard completion, deadlines and sequence scheduling. Start with N=1, then a compatible repeated pair and a mixed pair. Reject unsupported transitions; do not reset into the next reference pose.
  3. Restore the accepted nominal/residual G1 bundles and required snapshot/stance artifacts. Evaluate a frozen G1 against fixed or random valid human plans; then fine-tune the residual if needed, preserving its actor interface and frozen nominal controller.
  4. Preserve capture/delay/history semantics and physical first-event timing. Require final sequence completion for arrival. Record human validity separately, including completion rollouts after early G1 failures where needed.

Deliverables: integrated fixed-opponent environment, restricted sequence API, matched G1 baseline, and validity/visibility/termination evidence.

Gate: no extra attacks, no teleporting, no homing, correct final-sequence arrival, and all attempts accounted for. Expand to all 16 ordered pairs and balanced N=1,2,3 before advertising the PDF's full sequence support. Pairwise success alone does not prove arbitrary long-list success. Risk: high integration effort; requires the absent G1 artifacts.

P5 — Constrained adversarial plan selection

Objective: demonstrate whether choosing difficult legal punches improves adversarial training over the matched random baseline.

  1. Populate multiple valid alternatives within the allowed context. Keep family/TTC/count curriculum-controlled initially, as in the PDF, and report coverage where alternatives are unavailable.
  2. Implement a finite selector over legal plans at guard boundaries. Freeze the tracker; define which information the planner sees and the order of G1/planner decisions at launch.
  3. Introduce the new critic, replay and update schema described below. Test held-plan targets against a small enumerated example before long GPU training.
  4. Alternate against frozen opponent snapshots. Compare uniform/fixed-random plans, current adversarial selection and historical selectors under matched held-out recipes. Initially reuse the same small sequence subset rather than wait for every optional extension.

Deliverables: versioned two-player learner and inference bundle, fixed-random versus adversarial comparison, and complete validity/coverage accounting.

Gate: the implemented choices and targets match the declared game; adversarial results are evaluated with uncertainty, matched conditions, and no selective removal of invalid attempts. A valid implementation may find no improvement; that is a reportable scientific result, not a reason to redefine legality after seeing test outcomes. Risk: high semantic integration risk, even though finite selection itself is small.

P6 — Optional coverage and realism extensions

After the initial physical game is interpretable, expand source diversity, all transitions if not already completed, longer sequences, morphology/reach, footwork, continuous deformation, and eventually joint-level adversarial updates or impact dynamics. Revalidate each expansion. Detailed skin/face rendering is not a planned requirement.

Deliverable/gate: separately versioned experiments with renewed feasibility, camera, timing, and validity evidence. This phase must not block the first five.

Relationship to the PDF's 09A–09E milestones
Original milestone Revised placement
09A asset/data P0 supplies the visual subset; P2 supplies reviewed data and the physical asset.
09B references P2, with early combined-scene diagnostics enabled by P1.
09C imitation P3, using a selected tracking implementation rather than mandating WBC.
09D sequences/integration Integration risk discovery moves to P1; validated execution and G1 baselines remain P4.
09E robust game P5 for a declared restricted bank/sequence scope; broader coverage belongs to P6.

The narrower kinematic game can reuse P1/P2 and the P4/P5 interface/learning work while omitting physical-tracker claims. It is not evidence that P3 has passed.

7. Proposed plug-in and learning contract

Keep the human/reference provider independent of the G1 learner. These are proposed interfaces, not existing APIs.

Interface Essential content
HumanModelSpec Model/hash, morphology, joint/body/site/actuator names, coordinate maps, collision policy, glove attachment/proxies, action law, physics/control cadence.
MotionBank / PunchPlan Source split/provenance, family/hand, compatible starting state/guard, frozen target/world transform, reference/time law, phases, TTC, next guard, deadlines, validation, immutable ID/hash.
plan_punch / sample_ref Select/adapt a legal reference from actual initial state; explicit rejection if unavailable; sample only the committed plan and elapsed time after launch.
tracker_step / guard_status Actual human feedback and prior applied action; matched observation/normalization; measured body-relative recovery and dwell. No live G1 homing input.
HumanOpponentAdapter Reset/inference clocks, independent entity state/history, actual glove poses, legal boundary mask, sequence status, and distinct invalid reasons.
GameTransition Realized actor/critic observations, G1 applied action, selected/held plan, decision eligibility, successor legal set or reproducible context, sequence/time state, first-event labels, validity, true pre-reset successor.

For the first game, retain a per-control-tick expanded state, which fits the existing learning cadence more naturally than a new variable-duration representation. At a continuing successor state, the planner's feasible set is all admitted plans if a new decision is permitted; otherwise it is the singleton containing the committed plan. Use this rule in collection, critic targets, and actor optimization. Terminal SAV handling and the accepted off-control-tick event semantics must remain explicit; do not replace them with a generic PPO reward for G1. PPO is a candidate for human imitation, not a replacement for the protagonist's SAV objective.

A finite minimization through learned Q does not require differentiating through a discrete selector or through MuJoCo physics. If a boundary-only/SMDP representation is introduced later, specify duration-dependent discounting and intermediate failure/arrival events; ordinary fixed-step targets are not sufficient.

Define planner information and move order explicitly. Vision5 allows an adversarial response depending on state and protagonist action; deciding the plan before that action is a different information pattern. Freeze that choice in the experiment contract. The family/TTC/count schedule, legal mask, and sequence completion must be environment-owned, not outputs the adversary can redefine.

The new critic will need human mechanical/controller state, plan/time/sequence state, and the relevant existing G1/sensor history. Include latent delay/buffer/fault state if claiming a fully observed simulator critic; declare any retained omission. Compute new dimensions from the final schema. Do not force the plan into the old 29 zero channels. Preserve the actor's 948-feature order where possible, but make checkpoint migration explicit: new runtime identity, checked actor weights and normalizer, newly initialized/retrained critics.

8. Validation changes I would make

Preserve the Task 08 baseline geometry and timing first. The analytical glove radius is 0.08 m, head radius 0.06 m, and extra safety margin 0.02 m: frozen-head physical-contact TTC uses 0.14 m separation, while the safety boundary is 0.16 m. Keep both gloves as hazards through guard, outward motion, return, and waiting. A decorative glove mesh needs a declared relation to these proxies; an arbitrary center offset or larger visible shape must not silently change labels. See Vision5 Task 08(f) and the current runtime contract.

Calibrate the PDF's numerical gates rather than promise them. The proposed 2 cm glove RMSE, 20 ms P95 physical TTC error, 3 cm/15 degree guard tolerances, 0.15 s dwell, 1.5 s return bound, and 95%/99% completion targets are useful pilot hypotheses. Check source variability and controller performance, choose tolerances before final tests, and report any relaxation with its effect on admitted coverage. Do not make a hard case “pass” by silently substituting an easier TTC.

Separate timing resolution from root-finding tolerance. A 1 ms offline reference-contact tolerance is possible for a declared interpolant, but the source interval is approximately 8.33 ms and the accepted physics step is 5 ms. It does not establish 1 ms physical accuracy. Record the crossing bracket or an explicitly defined interpolation estimate. Control is 20 ms; physical event checks remain at 5 ms. Misses must remain in completion statistics even when finite TTC-error percentiles are also reported conditional on contact.

Treat reliability statistically. A 99% empirical completion target is not a confidence guarantee. For example, with zero failures in n independent trials, the one-sided 95% exact binomial lower bound is 0.05 ** (1/n): about 97.05% at n=100, and at least 99% only at n=299. Correlated crops/retimings do not satisfy independence automatically. Use per-family/TTC/sequence counts and uncertainty; the PDF's trial counts are a pilot floor, not certification.

Keep all attempted attacks visible in the accounting. Report admission rejections, valid safe arrivals, valid G1 failures, human falls, misses, timing violations, forbidden motion, return timeouts, and censored attempts. Runtime invalidity must not become a protagonist win or a source of adversary reward. Define whether/when transitions are released to training, especially when final return validates an earlier attack only retrospectively. Preserve early G1 first-event labels and separately test remaining human recovery rather than equating an immediate reset with successful return.

Use matched comparisons. Compare random and adversarial selection through the same frozen tracker and motion bank, with matched family/TTC/count, source split, placement, G1 starts, camera settings, and exogenous random streams. Report legal-plan coverage alongside outcomes so selective rejection cannot make one method appear stronger. Use bounded one-seed pilots first; independent trained seeds and a frozen held-out evaluation set belong in the release comparison. Camera changes, longer horizons, and different body models should be separately identified experimental factors.

9. Dependency and compute decision

Start with the accepted source and package family: mjlab 8ee51fbc, Torch 2.8.0+cu128, MuJoCo/MuJoCo-Warp 3.11.0, Warp 1.14.0, RSL-RL 5.5.0. Preserve the preview environment and create a separate candidate training lock. The dependency assessment records the WBC/NumPy conflict and why package version alone does not identify mjlab. No new training lock or WBC integration is claimed by this report.

The connected cluster exposes V100 32 GiB GPUs on xeon-g6-volta, not the initially mentioned L40S partition. The observed four-node allowance and two GPUs per node are consistent with the stated eight-GPU capacity; that is not an immediate reservation. Native rendering was verified on four GPUs across two nodes. See the tested submission guide.

V100 is not inherently excluded by the candidate stack. PyTorch's official 2.8 packaging announcement includes Volta support for its CUDA 12.8 build. PyTorch architecture announcement

Warp 1.14's documentation lists a CUDA 12.9 standard wheel, minimum compute capability 5.2 for CUDA 12 builds, and minimum 7.5 for CUDA 13 builds. The tested V100 is capability 7.0 with driver 580.159.04; this supports trying the CUDA 12 route, not the CUDA 13 alternative. This is a compatibility inference, not a completed Torch/Warp training test. Warp 1.14 compatibility, installation/build variants

Use one GPU for initial P1/P3 pilots, then arrays for independent seeds, families, or evaluation batches. Eight GPUs do not automatically accelerate one trainer. Measure contact simulation, camera acquisition, replay memory and learning throughput before selecting environment counts or wall-time requests. The combined body and 672 × 376 sensor workload make extrapolation from a short EGL video particularly unreliable.

10. Immediate next deliverable

Implement P1: a pinned-stack GPU probe and a small G1/human scene harness inside human-punching, together with a short review of complete source cycles. The review can proceed in parallel with infrastructure work. Success would give us actual Warp/camera evidence and a concrete adapter contract before committing to an exporter or imitation framework.

The remaining critical path is then: physical body/reference feasibility → one reliable punch → restricted sequences and fixed-opponent G1 baseline → a finite constrained game. More realistic rendering and a larger software stack do not remove any of those steps.

Sources and evidence boundaries

  • RobustSafeArrival-Vision5.pdf: finite-game assumptions; Task 07b measurement/closed-loop phases; Task 08 P1/P2/P3 structure, camera/hazard contracts, and final warm-continuation closure.
  • Task09_Human_Adversary_Implementation_Plan.pdf: §§2–3 assets/data, §4 targeting/TTC, §5 tracker, §6 sequences, §§7–8 integration/game, §9 proposed gates, §10 milestones.
  • Current local source links in §5 and project reports 01–05 distinguish implemented behavior from proposals. Native tracking behavior was inspected with git show at the exact accepted mjlab commit, not inferred from sibling HEAD.
  • External GPU compatibility sources were rechecked for this assessment. WBC dependency findings come from report 01's recorded inspection of revision 689f0836; its extension APIs have not been executed here.
  • This task produced documentation only. No new training, physical-human validation, combined-scene execution, or change to robust_sav is represented as completed.
07 · Head-targeted punches toward a standing G1

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Head-targeted punches toward a standing G1

Date: 10 October 2026. All implementation and outputs are in human-punching; robust_sav and mjlab were read only.

Result and answer to the method question

The experiment works without machine learning. The jab, cross, left hook and right hook have been adapted toward a stationary G1's head and rendered together with the robot in one native MuJoCo 3.11.0 scene. Each clip includes an oblique and side view, normal-speed and half-speed playback, and initial-guard/closest-approach stills.

Overview · Jab video · Cross video · Left hook video · Right hook video

This is motion retargeting implemented with smooth trajectory deformation and inverse kinematics (IK). These are complementary descriptions of the same work:

Method Role here
Motion retargeting The overall task: preserve recognizable recorded punches while changing their spatial goal to G1's head.
Forward kinematics (FK) Compute where the body, wrists and actual glove geometry end up for a candidate set of joint rotations; verify the solved motion. FK alone does not find the required rotations.
Inverse kinematics Adjust the striking arm so its glove follows the deformed target path. Segment lengths remain fixed.
Trajectory optimization A possible next tool for jointly imposing smoothness, joint/velocity limits, foot support and obstacle clearance across time. A large coupled optimizer was unnecessary for this fixed-target preview.
Machine learning Not needed for the current geometry problem. A learned tracker could later execute references under gravity and feedback; that is a different milestone. A learned controller is not mandatory if another controller passes the required physical tests.

The current solver itself performs small deterministic least-squares optimizations. “No trajectory optimizer needed” therefore means no full sequence/dynamics optimization problem was required, not that the IK is optimization-free.

Scene and standing robot

The scene uses the existing 1.80 m blue mannequin and the real G1 XML/STL assets from the sibling mjlab repository. The G1 asset subtree matches the exact mjlab revision pinned by robust_sav: 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa. Files and the repository license are copied into the generated output; source hashes are recorded.

G1 faces the human, with pelvis XY at (0.85, 0.20) m and yaw pi. It uses mjlab's configured KNEES_BENT_KEYFRAME joint angles, with visible soles aligned to z=0. This is a held standing configuration, not inference from a learned standing policy or the unavailable settled qpos_star artifact. Its head-proxy center is (0.8539635, 0.20, 1.231490745) m. That lower height explains why the original human punches need to be redirected downward.

The combined model has 123 qpos coordinates and 101 velocity coordinates: human 87/66, G1 36/35. Explicit entity maps preserve the human layout. Every G1 coordinate stays fixed throughout all four clips. Gravity, contacts and actuators are disabled; playback updates recorded/solved poses with mj_forward. It does not simulate balance, impact, or the G1 avoidance policy. Native MuJoCo rendering in this scene does not establish compatibility with the Task 08 Warp physics/camera application.

The portable scene is scene/scene.xml. A separate MuJoCo viewer must select its standing keyframe; loading default qpos alone does not apply the robot's bent-joint stance. The saved trajectory archives supply the human animation and complete combined-model poses.

How the adaptation works

  1. Load the same hash-verified DuoBox clips, retain the original 120 Hz timing, and generate the existing full-body replay.
  2. Find the attack's forward-reach anchor within the annotated attack interval. For a hook, the useful passing/contact region can precede the inherited outward-completion landmark; the right hook is an example.
  3. Deform the active glove-center path toward the front of the head for straight punches and toward the corresponding side for hooks. A quintic envelope fades the correction in and out. Hooks use a shorter raised-attack window so their low wind-up is not translated into an unreachable pose.
  4. Use a smooth radial clearance adjustment around the fixed head target. The head is captured once; there is no live target tracking during the motion.
  5. Solve Jacobian IK for the active shoulder, upper arm, forearm and wrist ball joints. Position tracking is primary, with source glove orientation and source arm posture as soft objectives. The previous frame's correction initializes the next solve.
  6. Recompute FK, body states and velocities from the final joint rotations. Verify the actual glove geometry rather than substituting the wrist origin for its center.

MuJoCo supplies the geometric Jacobian and quaternion-aware configuration integration used in this implementation. MuJoCo 3.11 API

The source root, trunk, legs, inactive arm and timing remain exactly unchanged. Initial and final source poses are also unchanged. The gloves remain rigidly attached to their hands. This preserves the current source motion structure, but does not certify anatomical joint limits or mechanically feasible speeds. Maximum individual arm-joint changes relative to the source reach approximately 88 degrees, so these are substantive pose adaptations, not tiny corrections.

Head geometry and why the preview stops just short

The 0.06 m spherical G1 head proxy is smaller than parts of its visible head mesh. The rounded block gloves also differ from an 0.08 m spherical glove. A small positive sphere-to-sphere clearance therefore did not prevent the visible geometry from intersecting in the first trials.

The final CLI uses 0.044 m extra clearance beyond the nominal 0.06 + 0.08 m center envelope. This was chosen against the actual visible geometry and produces a small visible gap. It is a preview-specific geometric setting, not a change to Task 08's head/glove radii or safety margin. The lower-level aim_clip function retains its 0.015 m proxy-targeting default; the scene CLI explicitly supplies the larger visible-head setting.

The authoritative non-overlap check is independent of MuJoCo's convex collision distance: for each sampled pose, transform both meshes' actual vertices to world coordinates and project them onto the axis from the head-mesh center to the glove-mesh center. Compute

gap = min(glove_vertices · axis) - max(head_vertices · axis)

A positive gap means the meshes occupy disjoint half-spaces at that pose. The table reports the minimum of this conservative separation bound over every frame. This checks the active glove against the visible head; it does not establish full-body clearance or continuous swept collision avoidance between samples.

A supplemental libccd distance check gives closest visible-head gaps of approximately 3.6, 5.0, 6.0 and 6.5 mm for jab, cross, left hook and right hook. The native CCD diagnostic returned inconsistent mesh distances in this experiment, including zero for some clearly separated configurations, so it is not used alone as acceptance evidence. The exported per-frame vertex-projection bound is reproducible without choosing a collision-distance backend.

These are punches aimed to near-contact with the visible head, not recorded Task 08 spherical-proxy hits. The final glove centers remain outside the accepted 0.14 m physical-contact and 0.16 m safety envelopes. No requested TTC was imposed, and no hit, safe-arrival, or adversarial-performance claim is made from these videos.

Measured final results

All distances below are millimetres. “Anchor IK error” measures deviation from the requested deformed glove path at its selected attack anchor; it is not distance to the head. The maximum path error includes all frames. The last column is the maximum world glove-orientation change from the source, in degrees.

Punch Anchor IK error (mm) Maximum path error (mm) Minimum visible-head separation bound (mm) Glove orientation change (deg)
jab 0.265 0.599 2.967 1.54
cross 0.293 0.566 4.682 1.46
left hook 0.408 0.436 5.026 0.73
right hook 0.333 8.018 5.934 2.87

Across all 855 poses, G1's head motion and G1 qpos changes were exactly zero. The nonselected human coordinates and endpoint poses match the source exactly. Recomputed combined-scene body FK matches the exported human body arrays exactly. The right hook has a larger transient path error than the other clips, but remains below 1 cm over this experiment and reaches its attack anchor accurately.

All four 1280 × 720, 30 fps MP4s passed full FFmpeg decoding and frame-count checks. Final Slurm job 5884609 completed successfully on one Tesla V100 in 23 seconds of batch time; the application took approximately 21.2 seconds. The existing GPU preflight confirmed actual NVIDIA EGL rendering on the allocated device. Earlier geometry trials remain in separate job output directories.

Evidence: final manifest, pose/media validation, GPU runtime, Slurm accounting, and test results.

The full test suite passed: 31 tests and 4 subtests, including new scene-isolation/asset-portability checks and real four-clip target-reaching tests. Ruff and shell syntax checks also passed. The final CLI clearance and all final exported poses were additionally checked through the artifact validation above.

Reproduction and implementation

cd human-punching
bash scripts/render_g1_cpu.sh
# Or, using the tested server GPU setup:
bash scripts/submit_g1_gpu.sh

CPU output defaults to outputs/g1-targeting. GPU jobs write outputs/g1-gpu-JOBID/preview, with their own runtime and completion records. The final reviewed GPU output has been copied to the stable outputs/g1-targeting directory; the original GPU job artifacts remain separate. Generated assets/videos stay excluded from Git.

For geometry-only export or another placement:

bash scripts/render_g1_cpu.sh --no-render --output outputs/g1-targeting-check
bash scripts/render_g1_cpu.sh --robot-distance 0.85 --robot-lateral 0.20 --clearance 0.044

A changed placement/height/clearance requires a new reach and mesh-separation review; the current solver is not a general feasibility-certified planner.

File Responsibility
g1_scene.py Copy/prefix assets, construct the shared scene, set standing pose, expose entity maps and head geometry.
targeting.py Smooth path deformation, active-arm IK, and updated trajectory export.
target_cli.py Orchestrate the four punches, hold G1 state, measure geometry, export manifests and trajectories.
target_render.py Render the shared scene with two views and both playback speeds.
target_g1_volta.sbatch Run the project-owned CPU IK/native EGL workflow in a Slurm GPU allocation.

Remaining limitations and the next useful refinement

The source's foot hovering/sliding remains visible because this experiment preserves its root and lower body. The arm rig has unrestricted ball joints and placeholder inertias; no full-body/self-collision, joint-limit, torque, balance, or return-to-guard dynamics validation has been performed. Exact endpoint preservation is a geometric property, not evidence of physical recovery. Punch-family annotations retain their existing candidate status.

For more natural motion across a larger target range, the next step is constrained kinematic retargeting: support-foot constraints, appropriate shoulder/elbow/wrist limits, smooth changes across the entire trajectory, and coordinated torso/knee motion where an arm-only solution is insufficient. Offline trajectory optimization is a reasonable tool for that. For physical execution, add dynamics and a feedback tracker separately. Neither requires introducing machine learning merely to aim these four recorded punches at this stationary target.

08 · Choosing a physical human for the punching adversary

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Choosing a physical human for the punching adversary

This research informed the first physical implementation. References below to the existing nonphysical model describe the original preview; see the implemented pilot and results for the current physical code.

For the final training-body decision, see the newer Task 09 plan reconciliation. The 34-scalar-DOF model below is an unimplemented proposal; the code still uses the diagnostic ball-joint body. The original PDF separately proposed SMPL-X/SMPLSim with SOMA23 as a fallback.

Date: 11 October 2026. Research and design assessment; implementation measurements belong in the accompanying experiment reports. This document does not claim that the proposed controller or all proposed model reductions have already passed physical tests.

Recommendation

Keep the existing blue mannequin and add a simple rigid-body dynamics model underneath it. Use primitive collision/inertia shapes, explicit segment masses, a free unactuated pelvis, bounded joint torques, and foot–floor contact. Keep rendering meshes separate from physical shapes. There is no immediate reason to acquire SMPL-X assets, simulate muscles, reproduce fingers/toes, or replace our motion data pipeline.

For an immediate diagnostic, retaining the existing 20 ball joints preserves the tested 87-coordinate pose layout and avoids confusing a controller failure with a retargeting failure. Call this a 60-actuated-DOF surrogate human, not an anatomical human. It is a valid articulated dynamics abstraction, but its knees and elbows have extra freedom. Before large-scale training, either implement explicit quaternion-aware mjlab control/observation adapters or reduce the skeleton to scalar anatomical axes and retarget again. Do not assume the current ball-joint trajectory files fit the stock tracking task.

The simpler alternative is also legitimate: a kinematic adversary with a constrained library of feasible-looking punch paths. That is enough to test G1's camera, avoidance, launch commitment, and family/target selection. It cannot establish human balance, torque feasibility, reaction to contact, or physically executable recovery. The choice depends on what the experiment is meant to claim; the kinematic baseline need not be discarded when physical tracking is added.

What the literature and released models actually do

Reference Relevant design What to borrow / what not to assume
DeepMind Control Suite CMU humanoid MuJoCo model using capsules, spheres, simple feet, bounded hinge motors and joint ranges. It includes more spine/hand detail than this punching task needs. Borrow the separation of a simple physical body from motion-control objectives. Do not copy joint axes or numerical ranges without transforming from its different skeleton frames. Released MJCF
DeepMimic Its paper's humanoid is 1.62 m, 45 kg and 34 DOF. Learned control tracks reference motions using joint PD targets; the study demonstrates martial arts as well as locomotion/acrobatic skills. A simplified body can produce recognizable physical human motion. A neural policy is a controller choice; it does not eliminate the physics model. Paper
DeepMimic released assets Spherical torso/hip/shoulder/ankle joints, hinge knees/elbows, fixed wrists, primitive body geometry, explicit masses and torque limits. A useful engineering scale reference, but its fixed wrists are a poor fit to our orientation-sensitive gloves. Character definition
SMPLSim Generates SMPL/SMPL-H/SMPL-X-compatible humanoid simulator models and supports MuJoCo; provides a small training framework. Useful if shape-controlled bodies or existing SMPL-based trackers become central. It is not required to put dynamics under our procedural mannequin. Official repository
PHC Reference-driven physical imitation and recovery; published setup uses Isaac Gym and separately obtained SMPL models. Its README explicitly notes that a single primitive can work without the full mixture architecture. A future learned-tracking reference. Its checkpoint and training stack are not drop-in components for our DuoBox skeleton and pinned mjlab environment. Official implementation
mjlab tracking The local pinned code already contains a reference-motion command, tracking rewards, terminations and RL infrastructure. Start from the stack already used by G1, after checking the action/observation representation and GPU runtime. Reusing task concepts is easier than assuming a new human asset is automatically compatible. Pinned tracking source

These examples support simple rigid-body models. They do not validate a particular invented mass, stiffness or torque limit as a measurement of a human boxer.

Which physical parameters are unavoidable, and which can be simplified?

Quantity Kinematic playback Dynamic human Practical simplification
Bone lengths / joint transforms Required Required Keep the tested DuoBox proportions scaled to 1.80 m.
Mass / center of mass / inertia Do not affect prescribed motion Required Use simple volume shapes and explicit segment masses; let MuJoCo compute consistent inertias.
Joint limits Optional for rendering, valuable for credible targets Required for the intended constrained surrogate Use conservative engineering limits and audit references; improve anatomical detail later.
Collision / friction Optional for a visual preview Required for supporting feet and any intended physical contact Boxes for soles, capsules for limbs, simple torso/head shapes.
Actuation None Required for active tracking Bounded torque motors with feedback control; no muscle model necessary.
Detailed skin / face / finger mechanics Unnecessary Unnecessary Preserve the simple blue surface and rigid gloves.
Learned control Unnecessary Optional Try feedback/inverse-dynamics or trajectory optimization first; add learning if those cannot meet the performance target.

MuJoCo can infer body inertias from geometry with assigned mass or density. Its compiler can restrict inertia inference to selected geom groups, so decorative meshes need not contribute twice. Ball joints have three rotational DOFs; their built-in range limits only the total rotation angle from the reference pose. A ball-joint limit therefore does not model separate elbow flexion, twist and lateral-bend ranges. MuJoCo XML reference

An XML that merely compiles is insufficient: positive mass/inertia, proper unit scale, plausible mass distribution, contact behavior, torque accounting, and integration stability must be checked. In particular, the original preview's placeholder inertias and zero gravity are not an acceptable dynamics baseline.

Concrete model construction plan

A. Immediate physics diagnostic with the existing skeleton

Retain all 21 body names and the 20 quaternion joints. Keep the free pelvis unactuated. Add physical primitives in a separate geom group, remove placeholder inertias, assign one mass budget per body, and verify the compiled total and inertia tensors. Feet need flat support patches; the current decorative ellipsoidal feet are not a good sole-contact definition.

Use one explicit total mass such as 75 kg including gloves as an engineering scenario, with an audited body-mass table. There is no need to estimate an unknown capture actor's actual mass. Distribute the budget toward the torso and thighs, give hands/gloves nonzero mass, and avoid allocating substantial mass to invisible schema-only toe segments. Do not present volume-derived distribution as a biomechanical calibration. A later sensitivity study can vary total mass, mass distribution, friction and strength once a nominal controller works.

For ball-joint control, use a three-component orientation error in a consistent tangent frame, angular-velocity error, and three orthogonal torque motors per joint. Keep saturation after any feedforward term; log actual actuator torques. MuJoCo ball motors use gear-axis directions in the child frame, and scalar-joint actuatorfrcrange is ignored for ball joints. Per-actuator force limits and, if desired, a joint torque-vector norm cap must therefore be explicit. MuJoCo actuator/joint reference

This diagnostic can answer whether the motion is approximately trackable, whether the feet support the body, whether the code is using the right frames, and which joints saturate. It cannot establish anatomical knee/elbow behavior. A root weld, fixed pelvis, prescribed root motion, or stabilizing external wrench must be marked as assisted and kept out of unassisted success statistics.

B. Reduced model if scalar mjlab actions or anatomical constraints are needed

A reasonable proposed target is 34 actuated scalar DOFs, plus the six-DOF free root:

Region Proposed DOFs Treatment
Two trunk segments 6 Three rotations per segment.
Neck 2 Pitch/yaw; head rigidly follows its neck segment.
Shoulders 6 Three per side; clavicle transforms fixed initially.
Elbows 2 Flexion hinge per side.
Forearm rotation 2 Pronation/supination per side.
Wrists 4 Two per side; forearm rotation plus wrist motion retain full glove-orientation control.
Hips 6 Three per side.
Knees 2 Flexion hinge per side.
Ankles 4 Pitch/roll per side.
Fingers/toes 0 Rigid fist/foot; schema markers may remain as sites.
Total 34 41 qpos / 40 qvel, including the free root.

This is a project proposal, not a supplied SMPLSim preset. Adding axes is easy; determining their reference frames and validating their limits requires care. Fit the source motion onto the reduced model using constrained IK with hand orientation, foot support and temporal smoothness. Recompute every exported body pose and velocity from the exact final model. Simply converting source axis-angle values into hinge coordinates would be incorrect.

A useful initial range-design workflow is: define anatomical axis directions from the bind skeleton; set conservative independent envelopes; inspect neutral/guard poses and each recorded cycle; identify violations; improve retargeting or record a deliberate model change. Avoid setting each bound only to the maximum of the four training clips and calling that an independently validated anatomical limit. Large rotations around shoulders and forearms are also sensitive to the chosen Euler factorization, so explicit sequence/frame documentation is necessary.

C. Effort and gain starting points

DeepMimic's released asset/controller files provide the following reference settings for its own character:

Joint group Torque limit in character file (N m) Kp / Kd in controller file
Shoulder 100 400 / 40
Elbow 60 300 / 30
Hip 200 500 / 50
Knee 150 500 / 50
Ankle 90 400 / 40

Sources: character limits, PD controller configuration. These are useful scale comparisons, not measured human strength and not validated gains for our 1.80 m mannequin, masses or timestep. They should not be copied wholesale or used to infer wrist capability from fixed-wrist examples.

Choose conservative pilot values, freeze them in metadata, and tune with isolated step responses and gravity-loaded standing before attempting full-speed punching. With PD control, proportional gains have torque/radian units and derivative gains torque/(radian/second); numerical settings depend on inertia, integration method, step size and actuator implementation. Report torque saturation and tracking error rather than silently increasing strength until the reference can be followed. If a reference is too aggressive, slow or re-optimize it and record the timing change.

D. Standing initialization and balance diagnosis

Start from a quiet guard sample, with joint velocities set to zero, and ramp the target gradually before advancing the punch. Place the root using the physical sole geometry, not the minimum decorative mesh vertex over the full clip. The current whole-clip vertical shift can leave one or both feet hovering at the initial pose. Solve a small pose-adjustment problem for both soles near z=0 and a nearly upright pelvis/trunk; do not correct only root height when the two feet require different corrections.

Measure the compiled subtree center of mass and project it onto the floor. For an initial quasi-static guard, place that projection inside the convex hull of the active sole contact patches with a positive margin. This is a useful necessary static-balance check, not a guarantee during a fast punch: angular momentum, acceleration, contact force feasibility and friction matter dynamically. Keep sufficient knee bend to avoid singular straight-leg corrections, but avoid a deep crouch that consumes the available knee/hip effort before the punch starts. Verify both intended stance feet actually bear load after settling.

Use separate diagnostics: free fall without motors, supported joint step responses, a free-root quiet-guard hold, then a slow jab. A small timestep (for example 1–2 ms as a pilot setting) can expose whether a 5 ms rollout failure is integration stiffness or a controller/reference problem; the final production timestep must be tested explicitly. Do not combine very small schema-only segment inertias with large derivative gains. Remove/fuse unused toe dynamics in a future reduced model; if preserving them for compatibility, document their small positive inertia and appropriately weak control.

Gravity or inverse-dynamics feedforward can improve posture tracking, but dropping the root entries of a full inverse-dynamics force vector does not make the remaining torques dynamically feasible. A contact-consistent whole-body controller must solve for allowed joint effort and admissible contact forces with zero root actuation. If plain PD tips over, first inspect COM/support, reference foot motion, momentum and torque saturation; a larger gain or hidden root force is not evidence that the problem is solved.

The actual software gap

Our current human model has a free root and 20 ball joints, no actuators, zero gravity and disabled contacts. The targeting solver preserves original lower-body motion and does not constrain support, anatomical joint limits or effort. These are local implementation facts, not missing capabilities of MuJoCo itself; see preview report and standing-G1 experiment.

The mjlab revision pinned by robust_sav expands ball-joint qpos and qvel widths in EntityIndexing, but the stock joint targets, per-joint default state, encoder bias, position limits and PD/action machinery are designed around scalar robot joints. A native MuJoCo or MuJoCo-Warp step of the ball-joint model is not evidence that stock mjlab tracking works unchanged. The motion loader expects joint_pos and joint_vel; our preview stores full qpos and tangent qvel with a different layout. Reference: pinned Entity implementation, Entity data.

The native rendering jobs already passed on the available V100 GPUs. Training-stack import, a batched dynamics/contact rollout, and the production camera path remain separate acceptance checks; the GPU experiment report should record their actual results. Neither SMPLSim nor a different human mesh resolves a CUDA/runtime incompatibility.

What should count as progress

  1. Model checks: compile; inspect total mass and principal inertias; verify neutral height and all joint/actuator maps; verify feet collide with the floor and decorative meshes do not add mass/contact unexpectedly.
  2. Static standing: compare assisted and free-root runs; measure pelvis/head height, foot slip, contact forces and torque saturation for several seconds. A supported standing success is not free standing.
  3. One slow jab: initialize consistently, follow a full guard–strike–return reference, and measure glove error and recovery under gravity. Repeat at nominal timing only after the slow case is understood.
  4. Constrained references: improve foot support and joint envelopes, then retest without changing acceptance definitions. Inspect actual hand orientation as well as glove center.
  5. Four families and sequences: preserve continuous state between punches, and evaluate return-to-guard rather than resetting the body at every boundary.
  6. Controller decision: retain model-based tracking if it meets the gate; train a motion-conditioned residual or imitation controller if balance/recovery/generalization remains inadequate. Learning should solve a measured deficiency.

For G1 avoidance the accepted 0.14 m glove/head center contact condition and 0.16 m safety threshold remain the task geometry. A larger visible mesh does not authorize changing those thresholds. Human–floor dynamics and task head-contact detection are separate contracts; if impact response is not being studied, terminate on the task event and avoid claiming that a visible mesh collision defines the event. The detailed PDF/code audit should resolve equality and termination semantics independently of this model choice.

Asset and dependency decision

No third-party body-model asset is needed for the proposed pilot. Our procedural surface and existing raw DuoBox skeleton already supply the geometry and reference motion; this report researched external implementations without incorporating their code or body assets.

The CMU humanoid source is in the Apache-2.0-licensed dm_control repository, and DeepMimic is MIT-licensed. SMPLSim's code is BSD-3-Clause; its source-code license does not grant rights to separate SMPL/SMPL-X model files. PHC's setup explicitly directs users to obtain those separately. If any external code/assets are adopted later, retain their notices and record exact revisions. dm_control license, DeepMimic license, SMPLSim license, SMPL-X project and model-download instructions.

All implementation stays in human-punching. The sibling robust_sav and mjlab trees were read only. No Git add, commit or push is part of this work.

09 · Physical human runtime: isolated GPU validation

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Physical human runtime: isolated GPU validation

Date: 2026-10-11. The GPU runtime probe and an improved full-speed physical jab against fixed G1 both passed on allocated Tesla V100 GPUs. The strongest controlled result is job 5891019, described in the final section.

Environment boundary

The new .venv-physics is separate from the existing preview .venv. The accepted mjlab revision 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa was exported using git archive into .cache/deps/mjlab-8ee51fbc; the sibling repository was only read. Nothing was staged, committed or pushed. All scripts, package locks, job outputs and intended caches belong to human-punching.

The core versions match the accepted robust_sav simulation stack: Python 3.12.3, Torch 2.8.0+cu128, MuJoCo 3.11.0, MuJoCo-Warp 3.11.0, Warp 1.14.0, RSL-RL 5.5.0 and NumPy 2.5.3. Supporting dependencies were resolved independently and frozen in requirements-physics.lock; this is not a byte-for-byte reconstruction of every historical robust_sav dependency. uv pip check passed for all 126 installed packages.

Reproduce from this directory:

bash scripts/setup_physics.sh
mkdir -p outputs/slurm
sbatch --parsable scripts/physics_probe_volta.sbatch \
  --physical-model outputs/physics-pilot/model/human_physical.xml

The setup script reads the accepted git object from sibling mjlab, creates the isolated environment, installs the complete lock, and checks compatibility. The project itself is installed editable so new local source is visible. Do not use the sibling mjlab HEAD: its newer Torch requirement is incompatible with this pin.

What is tested

The GPU probe records independent outcomes:

  1. A Torch MLP performs five CUDA forward/backward/Adam updates with finite gradients and decreasing loss.
  2. A standalone Warp CUDA kernel computes a known numerical result.
  3. mjlab.sim.Simulation steps 16 independent falling-sphere worlds for 1.2 seconds with gravity and floor contact; all must settle near their 0.10 m radius with low velocity.
  4. The fixture camera produces RGB, optical-axis depth and typed geometry IDs using mujoco_warp.create_render_context/refit_bvh/render, the same underlying API used by Task 08.
  5. The existing mannequin/G1 mesh scene is loaded into two Warp worlds and rendered through that same camera API. Besides an observer camera, it uses the actual Task 08 raster (672 × 376), fovy 54°, torso mount (0.08, 0, 0.45) m and 15° downward pitch, adapting only the body prefix to g1/torso_link. Glove visibility and correct RGB channel ordering are checked.
  6. The actual physical human runs four worlds for 100 steps at 2 ms. One world has zero control, three receive a 0.5 Nm ball-joint motor pulse. The test verifies finite dynamics, a nonzero response to the motor, normalized joint quaternions and RGB/depth/ID rendering. It is a transmission/runtime check, not a standing or tracking criterion.

The worker requests one V100, six CPUs, 24 GiB host RAM and 30 minutes for initial JIT compilation. There is no OpenGL/EGL fallback in this probe: cameras use the Warp renderer. Outputs appear under outputs/physics-probe-JOBID/, with structured runtime.json, RGB PNGs and RGB/depth/ID NPZ arrays. A stage failure leaves evidence and makes the job fail even if other stages succeed.

Verified final result

Slurm job 5890856 completed with COMPLETED, exit 0:0, on d-8-4-1. The structured result, Slurm log and accounting record preserve the evidence.

Check Measured result

Warp CUDA kernel All 512 elements match the expected result
Batched floor contact 16 worlds; 240 steps; final sphere height 0.099633 m; max speed 8.74e-6

The Task 08 near-contact image is dominated by the glove: 93.0% of pixels show the striking glove, median raw depth is 0.0204 m, and only 4.76% of pixels fall within Task 08's accepted optical-depth range [0.1, 3] m. This is a concrete near-field occlusion case, not a renderer failure. The left glove is blue in the preview, whereas the current production detector expects red gloves. Therefore this raw-camera pass does not validate HSV classification, delayed visual observations or protagonist performance. Pixel metrics

Initial runs and corrections

Job 5890789 began on d-8-5-1, using a Tesla V100-PCIE-32GB. Torch's actual architecture list contains sm_70; the MLP passed all five optimization steps (loss 1.0700 → 0.9883). Warp 1.14.0 initialized with CUDA Toolkit 12.9, recognized the same V100 and successfully executed the numerical kernel. The first script revision then used an obsolete metadata accessor, wp.context; that bookkeeping error was corrected to the public wp.get_cuda_toolkit_version() API. Job 5890808 passed all numeric stages. Visual review then caught swapped R/B channels in the diagnostic unpacker; this was corrected to the renderer’s packed RGB order (bits 16, 8, 0) and a red-object assertion was added. Job 5890851 passed that correction; job 5890856 additionally verified the Task 08 torso camera. Earlier images remain historical diagnostics and should not be used as final color evidence.

These measurements are more specific than broad CUDA-version assumptions. The current official PyTorch 2.8 release notes describe removal of Maxwell/Pascal from CUDA 12.8/12.9 builds, while this installed Linux build explicitly retains Volta. Warp's runtime reports its own toolkit version, which differs from Torch's; a separate system CUDA toolkit installation is unnecessary for these wheel-based tests.

How the physical human uses this API

A free root plus 20 ball joints has 87 position coordinates and 66 velocity coordinates. Three torque motors per ball joint provide 60 control channels. This is a different action representation from the scalar-joint IdealPDActuator wrappers used by standard mjlab robot tasks.

The low-level mjlab simulation accepts compiled MuJoCo models directly and exposes batched sim.data.qpos, sim.data.qvel and sim.data.ctrl; a quaternion-aware PD/controller can write torques there. This avoids pretending that 4 quaternion coordinates are 3 scalar joint positions. Controllers must preserve Torch/Warp CUDA stream ordering, keep the free root unactuated, respect torque limits, and log falls and assistance explicitly. The direct ball-joint motor/stepping gate has now passed for the physical model; stable closed-loop standing and punching remain separate criteria.

Limits of the evidence

Passing these tests establishes that the installed training math, batched dynamics and rendering primitives execute on this server. It does not establish a trained human, stable tracking, G1 avoidance performance, collision accuracy for all body pairs, or multi-GPU learning. The original G1 policy checkpoints are still separate artifacts needed for meaningful protagonist evaluation.

Controlled standing and jab on actual Warp dynamics

The next gate went beyond the motor smoke test. warp_tracking_probe.py ran two worlds through mjlab.sim.Simulation: a 5-second standing hold and the conditioned, re-aimed jab reference at 0.6 playback speed, followed by a 2-second recovery hold. Slurm job 5890905 passed. The model and reference are snapshotted in the output directory; the report records their hashes and both backend results.

The existing quaternion stable-PD controller with double-support balance feedforward ran on the CPU. At every step it observed the current GPU qpos/qvel, called CPU mj_forward to obtain its mass matrix and Jacobians, and uploaded only bounded motor torques. Warp performed every physical integration/contact solve. GPU positions and velocities were assigned exactly once at reset; no pose replay, root wrench, external root force or later state correction was applied. Foot contact counts and penetration were read from the actual Warp contact buffers. This deliberately synchronous CPU-controller/GPU-physics arrangement is a transparent compatibility experiment, not a high-throughput training implementation.
