The native comparison used the same model, conditioned reference, controller and timestep. Across the jab, the maximum root-position difference was 0.118 mm; the largest component of joint rotation error between the two backends was 0.00217 rad. These are measured differences, not a claim of identical numerical integration. Warp states are float32; native MuJoCo uses float64. GPU output site coordinates were recomputed at each saved state, while the native helper saves the previous step's kinematics, explaining a small timing offset in site-level metrics.

The same upright gate as the native pilot was used: pelvis height above 70% of its initial value and pelvis up-axis component above 0.70. A pass additionally requires zero root actuator force and torque norms within their limits. Glove contact is evaluated separately against the fixed analytic head center; the human-only physical model does not exchange impact impulses with a G1.

Reproduce the controlled experiment:

sbatch --parsable scripts/warp_tracking_volta.sbatch

The worker defaults to the snapshot input generated by native job 5890875. Override --model, --reference and --speed for new inputs; output always goes to outputs/warp-tracking-JOBID. The sampled trajectories preserve both actual and desired joint poses, actual hands/feet, center of mass and measured contact counts. The results support retaining this classical controller baseline before introducing a learned tracker. They cover one nominal planted jab and a standing hold; broad robustness, all punch families, target/TTC variations and continuous sequences remain separate tests.

Full-speed sensitivity exposes a tracking limit

Job 5890920 repeated the same reference at 1.0 source speed. Both native MuJoCo and Warp remained upright and within the torque bounds, but the punch missed: minimum striking-glove/head center distance was 0.162581 m, compared with 0.138935 m at 0.6 speed. Maximum torque reached its norm cap; the mean fraction of saturated joints was 0.000937, and maximum foot drift rose to 8.80 mm. Full-speed report

This distinguishes geometric reference contact from executed physical contact. The full-speed job's status: passed refers only to the explicit upright/no-root-assistance/torque-bound control gate; contact_goal_achieved: false records the failed punching objective. The slower job has contact_goal_achieved: true. At that stage the accepted physical jab was a 0.6-speed nominal case; the later full-speed refinement below supersedes that limitation for this one reference. Reference timing/trajectory and controller improvements are the next classical-control experiments; replacing the physics backend does not address this miss.

Job 5891019 passed the physical support and isolated-punch gates at full source speed, using the fixed-G1 scene and improved reference from native experiment 5890952. The reference was smoothed with a four-frame Gaussian standard deviation, re-aimed to a 0.10 m glove/head center target, and tracked with PD gain scale 1.5. Torque limits were retained. The G1 is present as fixed scene geometry with zero dynamic DOFs. Task contact is analytic; G1 impact response is disabled. In particular, aiming inside the 0.14 m proxy permits the glove to pass through that envelope without receiving a collision impulse. This tests executable targeting and recovery, not physically realistic glove/head impact or learned G1 avoidance.

10 · Task 08 contact and safety contract for the articulated human

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Task 08 contact and safety contract for the articulated human

Date: 11 October 2026. Source: RobustSafeArrival-Vision5.pdf, physical page 33, §f. All changes are in human-punching; robust_sav was read only.

The user's interpretation is correct

The accepted geometry is a 0.08 m radius glove and 0.06 m radius head, with an additional 0.02 m safety margin. Let d_g = ||p_glove,g - p_head|| in world coordinates.

Condition Accepted definition Equality
Physical proxy contact At least one glove has d_g <= 0.14 m Contact at exactly 0.14 m
Safe glove clearance Both gloves have d_g >= 0.16 m Safe clearance at exactly 0.16 m
Signed safety clearance min(d_left, d_right) - 0.16 m Zero is on the safe boundary
Safety violation without contact At least one glove is closer than 0.16 m, but neither is within 0.14 m The open 0.14–0.16 m band is unsafe without proxy contact

This last band is intentional. Contact and safe clearance are not complements. A glove at 0.15 m has violated the required safety margin without touching the physical proxy. “Safe clearance” also is not the full safe set: robot height, joint limits, self-collision, and finite state remain independent requirements. Arrival additionally requires actual punch return and the accepted stance-energy condition.

The PDF requires both gloves to remain hazards throughout approach, retraction, guard motion, and waiting. The inactive hand must not be excluded.

This already exists in robust_sav

The current canonical source is boxing/punches/glove_geometry.py. It declares the exact radii, margin, thresholds, and version task08_head06_gloves08_margin02_v1. Its wrapper supplies 0.16 m clearance and 0.14 m contact to the shared label implementation. ResidualCollector selects this wrapper and evaluates/latches events at 5 ms physics substeps. The runtime contract also includes the geometry identity.

The shared older collector defaults are 0.14 m safety and 0.12 m contact because earlier tasks used 0.06 m gloves. Those are not the Task 08 operating thresholds: the Task 08 wrapper explicitly overrides them. No change to robust_sav is needed to correct the user's values.

The head center comes from the actual robot/head_collision geometry. Its radius is checked to be 0.06 m in the training plant; the native G1 XML places that sphere at (0, 0, 0.43) m in torso_link. It is not the robot pelvis, a visual mesh centroid, or an estimated camera detection center.

Why the earlier targeted videos did not register hits

The previous standing-G1 experiment deliberately kept the visible rounded glove meshes separate from the larger visible G1 head surface. It used an extra 0.044 m preview clearance above the 0.14 m proxy envelope:

Preview Minimum glove/head center distance Task 08 proxy contact?
Jab 0.184738 m No
Cross 0.184736 m No
Left hook 0.183637 m No
Right hook 0.183755 m No

These results were appropriate as near-contact visual previews, but they did not exercise the accepted hit boundary. The visible head is larger than the 0.06 m sphere in some directions, and the block-shaped glove is not an 0.08 m sphere. Consequently, visible mesh overlap and proxy contact can disagree. The correct response is to keep the accepted 0.14/0.16 m contract and make the role of each geometry explicit; it is not necessary to redefine contact using the visible head outline.

For the next reference/controller experiments, use 0.08 m spherical glove proxies centered on explicit hand attachments and the original 0.06 m G1 head proxy. Aim the reference into the physical envelope, allowing a small deliberate penetration of that proxy in a kinematic diagnostic so solver tolerance cannot convert a planned hit into a miss. Report the measured distance and first entry, not merely the requested target. If the original visible head mesh is retained, document its mismatch with the proxy or expose an optional diagnostic overlay. Proxy correctness is not evidence that an arbitrary visual mesh is collision free.

Center attachment and wrist rotation

An articulated human replaces a scheduler-owned glove center with a rigidly attached center:

p_glove_world = p_hand_world + R_hand_world @ p_glove_in_hand

The wrist origin is not the glove center. MuJoCo's compiled mesh frame also need not equal the input mesh's authoring origin. For the existing 1.80 m preview model, the compiled glove centers relative to the hand body are approximately:

Left:  (+0.06677617, -0.00430814, +0.01077035) m
Right: (-0.06677617, -0.00430814, +0.01077035) m

These numbers describe the current generated model, not universal anatomical constants. A revised body model should store its own local attachment offsets in its manifest. Use data.site_xpos for a named attachment site or data.geom_xpos for its spherical proxy after FK; drive targeting, labels, observations, and any sensing geometry from that same center. Hand rotation must rotate the offset as well as the glove's appearance.

Three time/contact questions must stay separate

  1. Requested/reference TTC: freeze the head center at outward launch and find the first reference-glove entry into the 0.14 m envelope. The safety margin does not enter this calculation. Recompute TTC after changing the arm path, glove attachment, morphology, or timing.
  2. Physically executed TTC against that same frozen target: use the actual tracked glove trajectory against the launch-frozen head. This measures execution and timing fidelity even if G1 subsequently moves.
  3. Observed contact against G1: compare the actual glove trajectory with the actual moving head. Successful avoidance can make this a miss even when the first two TTCs are finite.

Do not continuously redirect a committed outward punch toward G1's moving head. For a standing head these definitions can coincide only if the physical tracker follows the reference; that coincidence must be measured rather than assumed.

The word “physical” in the Task 08 contact definition describes geometric envelope entry. It does not mean the existing simulator applies impact forces: the sensing scene explicitly uses contype=0 and conaffinity=0 on the two fist spheres, and its audit rejects added punch contacts. The human can have physical dynamics and foot contacts while glove/head contact remains an analytic event. Simulating striking forces is a separate optional experiment that changes the robot/human transition dynamics; it is unnecessary for preserving the current punch-avoidance objective.

Implementation in human-punching

contact.py provides the geometry without importing robust_sav or Torch:

  • evaluate_clearance(glove_centers, head_center): NumPy broadcasting over batches/frames, both gloves, per-glove signed margins, any-contact and all-gloves-clear labels. Nonfinite geometry is rejected.
  • world_attached_centers(body_positions, body_rotations, local_offsets): the explicit rigid attachment transform.
  • first_contact_time(time_s, glove_centers, head_center): first entry along piecewise-linear relative-center segments, including crossings between samples; accepts a fixed launch head or a moving head trajectory and returns infinity on a miss. The output uses the supplied absolute clock; subtract launch time for TTC.

The last routine is an offline diagnostic. Interpolated centers approximate the inter-frame motion; they are not a continuous articulated-trajectory certificate or a replacement for actual substep event evaluation. The geometry utility does not itself alter the earlier targeting exports, add collision response, or implement the remaining safe-arrival predicates.

Validation: 10 tests passed in test_contact.py, covering both equality boundaries, the unsafe/non-contact band, inactive-hand hazards, moving versus frozen targets, rotated attachment offsets, inter-frame sphere crossings, tangent contact, initial contact, misses, and invalid input. Run from human-punching:

.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_contact.py -q
11 · Contact-directed G1 punch references

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Contact-directed G1 punch references

Date: 11 October 2026. This experiment corrects the objective of the earlier visual near-contact preview while preserving the accepted Task 08 radii. No changes were made to robust_sav.

Result

All four recorded punch families now enter the 0.14 m glove/head proxy-contact envelope against the standing G1. The target center separation is 0.135 m, giving the IK solver 5 mm of nominal overlap tolerance. This does not change the 0.08 m glove radius, 0.06 m head radius, or 0.16 m safety boundary. Both gloves are measured throughout every frame.

Reference Closest active-glove center distance First contact from clip start Sampled frames in contact Maximum IK path error
Jab 0.135798 m 0.762704 s 13 0.565 mm
Cross 0.135854 m 0.627309 s 25 0.564 mm
Left hook 0.134628 m 0.849237 s 4 0.437 mm
Right hook 0.134932 m 0.732864 s 3 10.227 mm

The inactive glove remains outside the contact envelope in all four base references; its closest distances are approximately 0.564–0.619 m. All active contacts also violate the 0.16 m clearance requirement, as expected. These labels describe the glove/head geometry; they do not certify the G1's other safe-arrival conditions.

The contact time uses a head frozen at clip start and first sphere entry along piecewise-linear interpolation of the exported center path. Clip start is a diagnostic commitment time, not a newly validated launch-phase annotation. The source timing is retained; no requested TTC is imposed. The saved 120 Hz samples also contain actual proxy-contact frames, so acceptance does not depend solely on inter-frame interpolation.

Finite geometric variants

The bank contains 12 references: the four families, each with approach-direction yaw offsets of −6°, 0°, and +6°. The yaw rotates the target normal around the frozen head while keeping its target distance at 0.135 m. It does not rotate the robot or change its placement. All 12 variants pass sampled proxy-contact checks and have finite exported states.

This provides a small within-family choice for later experiments. “Accepted” here means geometrically reaches the contact envelope, not dynamically feasible, anatomically validated, or approved for a trained adversary. These references retain source lower-body motion and unrestricted arm ball joints. Physical feasibility, support-foot constraints, bounded actuation, and recovery are separate acceptance gates.

Implementation and preserved behavior

aim_clip now accepts target_mode="proxy_contact", contact_center_distance_m=0.135, and an optional approach yaw. Its existing visual_clearance default remains unchanged, so the older near-contact rendering commands retain their previous behavior. Smooth path deformation, source-orientation regularization, exact guard-endpoint preservation, and unchanged nonselected joints remain in effect.

The solver still allows the original source root, legs and torso to move as recorded. It changes only the active arm's four ball joints. The right hook has a larger transient path error than the other families; its closest-approach contact still passes. This stage should not be represented as a full-body constrained trajectory optimizer.

contact_target_cli.py reuses the existing blue-human model, source trajectories, G1 scene, and frozen target; it does not require regenerating assets or installing learning libraries. It exports:

  • human_trajectory.npz: the human's 87-coordinate reference, corresponding velocities/body states, both glove centers, distances and contact/safety labels.
  • trajectory.npz: the same reference scattered into the combined G1/human scene, with every G1 coordinate held fixed.
  • manifest.json: geometry identity, measured first contact, both-glove minima, attachment offsets, solver errors, and hashes.

The shared geometry implementation and exact equality conventions are documented in 10-contact-contract.md.

Evidence and reproduction

Generated bank: outputs/contact-targeting. Independent combined-scene checks: validation.json. Each reference's compiled combined-scene glove centers agree with the human-only export to better than 1e-10 m; all G1 coordinates are unchanged and all numeric state arrays are finite.

From human-punching:

# Export four base references plus two yaw variants per family.
.venv/bin/python -m human_punching.contact_target_cli --approach-yaws-deg -6 0 6

# Render the four base references; use EGL inside an allocated GPU job.
.venv/bin/python -m human_punching.contact_target_cli \
  --render --backend egl --output outputs/contact-targeting-rendered

# CPU rendering is also available with --backend osmesa.
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_contact.py tests/test_targeting.py -q

The contact/targeting suite passes 20 tests, including all previous targeting tests, four real-clip contact-mode checks, invalid targets, and the contact contract regressions. Ruff passes for all modified source and test files.

The rendered block glove and original visible head can visibly overlap at proxy contact because they do not share the analytic spheres' boundaries. No impact force or balance claim follows from these kinematic references. The subsequent physical-model experiments should retain the explicit proxy centers/radii, use physical foot/body dynamics where needed, and keep analytic contact-event detection separate from any optional impact-response experiment.

Completed GPU render

Slurm job 5890904 completed with exit code 0:0 in 15 seconds on a Tesla V100 (d-8-4-1). The preflight confirmed native NVIDIA EGL on the allocated GPU; all four videos fully decoded without errors.

Overview · Jab · Cross · Left hook · Right hook

Evidence: GPU runtime, media validation, and Slurm accounting. Submit from human-punching with sbatch scripts/contact_targets_volta.sbatch. These videos deliberately retain the original kinematic lower-body motion; visible hovering remains and is not evidence of floor-supported execution.

12 · Finite reference bank and full-cycle commitment

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Finite reference bank and full-cycle commitment

Date: 11 October 2026. This report covers an isolated human-punching reference adapter and, in the final section, a separate continuous physical two-jab proof. Neither runs the G1 policy or trains an adversary.

Implemented contract

cycle_bank.py loads the 12 geometrically accepted references described in 11-reference-contact.md. It checks the model XML hash, each human-trajectory hash, joint/state dimensions, normalized quaternions, monotonic clocks, finite states, and accepted contact metadata. Each bank entry exposes family, source duration, frozen target center, payload hash, and measured diagnostic contact time.

A BoundaryCyclePlayer enforces the following sequence:

State Allowed action Result
boundary Select a legal variant and provide matching current position/velocity Freeze the variant and launch head center; enter active.
active Advance the committed reference at the configured control timestep Sample the same reference throughout the cycle. New plans are rejected.
waiting_guard Use an external transition/controller; acknowledge a measured guard matching the next reference Return to boundary. No automatic jump or next-punch launch occurs.

The launch plan is an immutable dataclass. Its head snapshot is a tuple copied from the caller, so subsequently changing the caller's head array cannot redirect the punch. The advance method has no live head-target argument. A completed reference clock is explicitly different from physical punch completion: the terminal sample carries requires_guard_controller=True.

At a boundary, plan masks can filter family, maximum source duration, and a window of measured diagnostic contact times. They also check that the launch head matches the pretargeted reference head. At present all references target the same standing G1 placement. A different head location produces an empty mask and requires retargeting; the adapter does not silently shift a precomputed trajectory or begin homing. All masks are false while active or waiting for guard acknowledgment.

The timing-window filter does not synthesize a requested TTC. It filters the existing first-contact measurements from clip start. Source durations and timing are retained, including guard padding whose phase annotation remains provisional.

Control-clock sampling

The default control interval is 0.02 s. Positions interpolate between saved poses using MuJoCo's tangent-space differentiation and integration, including shortest-arc quaternion rotation. Exported reference velocities are linearly interpolated as feedforward values; they are approximate desired velocities, not an exact derivative certificate for the pose interpolant.

At the first control tick at or after the source endpoint, the adapter returns the final source pose and marks the cycle complete. World time remains on the control clock; the reference elapsed time is capped at the clip duration:

Family Source duration Completion control time at 50 Hz
Jab 1.733333 s 1.74 s
Cross 1.700000 s 1.70 s
Left hook 1.850000 s 1.86 s
Right hook 1.808333 s 1.82 s

No final pose is teleported to another clip. Sampling poses is not physical stepping, and these control-clock results do not establish a tracking controller's recovery time.

Guard admission uses separate quantities and units

The adapter compares the supplied current state with the selected clip's initial state. guard_errors() reports root and joint errors separately; ball-joint angle errors use the norm of their rotation vectors. Every articulated joint participates in the maximum joint error.

Quantity Default admission tolerance
Root position error 0.02 m
Root orientation error 0.02 rad
Maximum articulated-joint angle error 0.02 rad
Root linear-velocity error 0.10 m/s
Root angular-velocity error 0.10 rad/s
Maximum articulated-joint angular-velocity error 0.10 rad/s
Slide-joint position/velocity error, if present 0.02 m / 0.10 m/s

These are explicit engineering defaults for avoiding discontinuous reference admission, not empirical physiological limits or a physical guard-recovery certificate. The physical controller must additionally establish appropriate foot support, balance, actuator feasibility, and attack eligibility. A caller must supply actual controller state when using the adapter with physics; substituting reference arrays would only demonstrate kinematic playback.

The guard audit found that none of the 16 base-family endpoint-to-start pairs passes admission, including repetition of the same family. Example same-family discontinuities are:

Repeated reference Root-position mismatch Root-orientation mismatch Maximum joint-angle mismatch Maximum joint angular-velocity mismatch
Jab → jab 0.04285 m 0.12919 rad 0.34774 rad 3.88956 rad/s
Cross → cross 0.19620 m 0.24400 rad 0.24564 rad 2.30065 rad/s
Left hook → left hook 0.06869 m 0.24082 rad 0.49585 rad 9.35831 rad/s
Right hook → right hook 0.02906 m 0.18991 rad 0.20540 rad 2.22744 rad/s

Preserving recorded endpoints during IK therefore did not make the clips stationary, recovered guards or freely concatenable cycles. The source crop annotations and return phases need further refinement before continuous physical sequence acceptance.

Optional bridge is explicitly kinematic

kinematic_guard_bridge() generates a quintic pose transition with normalized quaternion interpolation and zero endpoint velocities. This is useful for inspecting the gap between two guard configurations. It does not constrain support feet, avoid self-collision, respect joint/torque limits, or solve balance. Its zero terminal velocity can also differ from the next source clip's initial velocity; the ordinary guard-admission check still applies.

The bridge returns requires_physical_guard_controller=True. This bank adapter and its generic kinematic bridge do not by themselves establish physically continuous execution, and no bridge is automatically executed or admitted by the cycle player. The separate conditioned physical proof below measures execution and guard recovery explicitly.

Validation and usage

Eight tests pass in test_cycle_bank.py: immutable launch snapshot, mid-cycle plan rejection, legal masks, dimensional guard metrics, position/velocity admission, quaternion interpolation, explicit completion/guard states, payload tamper rejection, and an actual generated-bank cycle. Ruff passes. The all-family control-clock/guard measurements are recorded in cycle-bank-audit.json.

from human_punching.cycle_bank import CycleBank, BoundaryCyclePlayer

bank = CycleBank("outputs/contact-targeting/manifest.json")
player = BoundaryCyclePlayer(bank, control_dt_s=0.02)
mask = player.valid_plan_mask(measured_head_center, family="jab")
# Choose one admitted variant at the boundary, then pass actual controller state.
reference = player.start(
    chosen_variant, measured_head_center, launch_time_s,
    current_qpos=measured_human_qpos, current_qvel=measured_human_qvel,
)
# Each control tick: reference = player.advance(); send it to a separate tracker.
# On completion, run a guard controller before player.acknowledge_guard(...).

The adapter is a foundation for fixed/random finite selection and later boundary-only adversarial choice. It does not implement the RSAV learner/replay changes, Bellman commitment masks, physical sequence controller, or adversarial training.

Continuous physical two-jab proof

A separate run now demonstrates two consecutive physically executed jabs and return to guard, with one initial reset and no state overwrites between punches. It reuses the successful conditioned jab and physical scene from outputs/physical-5890912, rather than directly concatenating the unconditioned source bank discussed above.

Watch the continuous two-jab video · Still · Metrics

The human has a free, unactuated root. The same bounded quaternion PD plus contact-aware balance feedforward controller runs at 2 ms physics steps, with gain scale 1.5 and reference playback speed 0.6. G1 is fixed in the scene, the head snapshot stays fixed in world coordinates, and glove/head entry is an analytic event with no impact response. No learned policy is involved.

The sequence runs:

  1. Execute the full jab reference at 0.6 speed.
  2. Hold its final target for approximately 0.4 s.
  3. Track a 1.5 s quintic generalized-coordinate return to the initial guard.
  4. Hold the initial guard until actual measured pose, velocities, both-foot contacts, glove clearance and support limits pass continuously for at least 0.10 s. Reject the next launch after a 3 s timeout.
  5. Launch the second jab only after that measured admission, then repeat the return and guard check.

Only controller targets interpolate during the bridge. The actual state evolves through mj_step; it is not assigned to either endpoint. Guard comparisons use the separate dimensional tolerances listed above, with a stationary target guard velocity of zero. This is a dedicated same-jab sequence proof; it does not establish arbitrary family transitions or all 12 bank variants.

Measured results

Slurm job 5890969 completed with exit code 0:0 in 11 seconds on a Tesla V100 (d-8-4-1). It simulated 9.79 s / 4,895 physics steps, including both final guard recovery and the second launch at 4.898 s.

Metric Result
First jab: minimum active glove/head distance 0.110467 m
Second jab: minimum active glove/head distance 0.110762 m
First jab: measured time to contact from launch 1.143171 s
Second jab: measured time to contact from launch 1.144021 s
Both feet in contact 99.9796% of sampled physics states
Maximum sole horizontal drift 0.006698 m
Maximum floor penetration 0.001795 m
Mean fraction of joints at torque saturation 0%
Maximum joint torque norm / permitted norm 0.683108
Root actuator force and externally applied force Exactly zero

Contact was detected during both punch phases; no contact occurred during endpoint holds, return bridges, or guard waiting. The inactive glove remained outside the 0.14 m envelope. The complete sequence passed the explicit support, contact, torque, and recovered-guard gates.

At second launch, measured guard errors were 1.632 mm root position, 0.001577 rad root orientation, 0.002856 rad maximum joint angle, 0.000617 m/s root linear velocity, 0.002723 rad/s root angular velocity, and 0.008295 rad/s maximum joint angular velocity. The complete actual qpos/qvel snapshot is retained in the launch record. Across the immediately adjacent 2 ms samples at that launch, the glove centers moved only 9.29 and 9.72 micrometres; no reference-pose jump was inserted.

Evidence: full rollout, independent validation, GPU runtime, Slurm accounting. The video passed full FFmpeg decoding, all saved states are finite, and both launch/recovery snapshots independently pass the declared guard thresholds.

Implementation: sequence_cli.py, with the existing physical controller and renderer reused. test_sequence.py additionally verifies a deliberately insufficient guard timeout prevents a second launch. That regression passes; Ruff and shell syntax checks pass.

# Run from human-punching. Uses the successful conditioned physical jab artifact.
sbatch scripts/physical_sequence_volta.sbatch

# CPU physics without rendering:
.venv/bin/python -m human_punching.sequence_cli --output outputs/physical-sequence-local

The 1.143–1.144 s contact delay is measured from controller cycle start, including guard padding. It is a result of the slowed reference, not the PDF’s separately annotated outward-launch TTC or a requested TTC guarantee. This proof has one morphology, one stationary target, one punch family, and one deterministic initialization. It establishes a useful classical-control baseline, not robustness to disturbances, broader sequence feasibility, learned adversarial selection, or a G1 avoidance-policy evaluation.

13 · Physical human pilot: findings, implementation, and remaining gates

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Physical human pilot: findings, implementation, and remaining gates

11 October 2026. Development and generated artifacts are confined to human-punching. The sibling robust_sav, mjlab, and DuoBox inputs were read only. No Git staging, commit, or push was performed.

What changed

The project now has a physical human with a free pelvis, bounded torque control, gravity, and foot contact. It also has the exact Task 08 contact contract, contact-directed references, an isolated compatible GPU environment, real Warp dynamics/camera checks, and a cycle-selection interface. These are concrete preliminary components; they do not constitute a trained protagonist–adversary game.

The strongest backend result is a controlled jab at source playback speed on actual MuJoCo-Warp through pinned mjlab, with a fixed G1 in the same scene. The closest glove/head center distance was 0.112658 m, maximum foot drift 5.05 mm, and double-foot contact 99.786%. Root actuator force was zero and torque-vector bounds were respected. Native MuJoCo and Warp differed by at most 0.686 micrometres in root position. See GPU evidence and job 5891019. The CPU computes feedback torques from actual GPU states; Warp integrates physics. This validates correctness before a fully GPU-resident controller.

All four families now have an accepted isolated native-physics case. Only the jab has passed at source playback speed; the cross and hooks currently require 0.3× speed. A separate two-jab sequence physically recovers its guard before the next launch, without resetting the state.

Four-case overview · Jab · Cross · Left hook · Right hook · Two jabs

Accepted native case Source speed Minimum active glove/head distance Maximum sole drift Source job
Jab 1.0× 0.112658 m 5.05 mm 5890952
Cross 0.3× 0.137540 m 7.80 mm 5890924
Left hook 0.3× 0.116591 m 5.50 mm 5890924
Right hook 0.3× 0.113157 m 5.13 mm 5891006

The right hook uses a 12 cm forward stance offset, deeper reference aiming, and gain scale 3; the other accepted cases use gain scale 1.5. Effort limits are unchanged. These are separate conditioned cases, not a universal controller shown to generalize. Their final reference and executed-pose audits have no cone violations beyond the recorded tolerance; see review manifest.

1. The 0.14 / 0.16 m definitions transfer directly

For each glove, use its attached center, not its wrist origin. Contact occurs if either center is at most 0.14 m from the head center. Glove clearance is safe only if both are at least 0.16 m away. Thus 0.15 m is unsafe without contact. Equality belongs to contact at 0.14 and safe clearance at 0.16. These definitions are already supplied by the Task 08 wrapper in robust_sav; older generic collector defaults are not the Task 08 operating values.

The physical human does not change the thresholds. It changes who generates the center trajectory: body/hand forward kinematics now supplies it. Both gloves remain hazards during all phases. The new physical pilot records both centers at every 2 ms physics step, separately from the video sampling rate.

“Physical contact” here is an analytic sphere-envelope event, not a requirement to exchange collision impulses. The existing Task 08 sensing fists also have impact contacts disabled. Accordingly, the new human has floor contact while the G1 target is fixed and glove/G1 impact response remains off. This preserves the avoidance event without introducing a different impact-driven transition model. Visual meshes may overlap before/after proxy contact because the visible head and gloves are not those spheres.

The prior videos stopped around 0.184 m and therefore never hit this contract. The new kinematic bank has 12 contacting variants, four families with three approach directions. Contact radii never change when aiming deeper inside the envelope to tolerate tracking error. See the complete contract and contact reference results.

2. A physical human can be simple

The new model retains the plain blue body and uses primitive capsules, ellipsoids, spheres, and boxes for dynamics. It assigns segment masses summing to 75 kg and asks MuJoCo to integrate each primitive's inertia. The mass is a chosen scenario, not an estimate of the capture actor. No SMPL assets, muscles, fingers, toes with detailed surfaces, or anatomical scans are required.

The pilot keeps the source's 20 ball joints / 60 torque motors, plus an unactuated free root. Limits are isotropic quaternion-rotation cones, not independent anatomical elbow/knee limits. Torque is clamped by each joint's three-axis vector norm, with per-axis XML limits as an additional bound. Tiny toe articulations remain for archive compatibility, but their reference rotations are set to identity. The physical model uses two red gloves to match the existing red-only perception assumption; earlier blue/red preview assets are retained as historical outputs.

This is an engineering rigid-body abstraction, not a biomechanically calibrated human. Human self-collision and human/G1 body collision are currently disabled. Contact between the human and floor is enabled. Before calling a learned policy “human-like,” replace unrestricted elbow/knee ball freedom with suitable anatomical joints or enforce stronger axis constraints, audit segment properties and effort ranges, and enable/audit selected self-collisions.

If the adversary only needs to choose among bounded, precomputed glove paths, even this physical rig is optional: a kinematic trajectory-bank adversary is a legitimate simpler game. Physics is needed when claims concern executable human motion, balance, momentum, support, or strength. The evidence and primary-source comparisons are in physical-human research.

3. What improved the motion, and what failed

  • Foot/COM IK creates a deliberately planted double-support version. It fixes the physical sole positions/orientations and brings the projected COM near the support center. It is not recovery of unknown true capture floor contacts.
  • Head aiming is repeated after support correction. Planting feet changes root position; assuming the old aimed glove path survived would falsely report hits.
  • A quaternion sign-continuous smoothing option suppresses source rotation spikes. Original hook rotations reached roughly 61–68 rad/s. The altered references, speed factors, aiming depth, and stance offsets are recorded explicitly.
  • Pose-only PD fell despite a statically feasible initial guard. Joint torques needed to support gravity were missing. Bounded PD plus contact-aware inverse statics and root-error feedback holds the guard. Its estimated support forces are only a calculation; they are never applied as external forces or used to overwrite the pelvis.
  • The controller assumes double support. Its friction projection is not a full constrained dynamics optimizer or stability proof. Contact, slip, saturation, and uprightness must be measured in rollout.

The initial unchanged-speed, unchanged-target jab stayed upright in both native and Warp backends but missed at 0.162581 m. This was a tracking limitation, not a backend incompatibility. A later, smoothed, deeper-aimed native jab succeeded at source playback speed. Slower cross and both hook references also passed. Failed runs are preserved; staying upright alone is not an accepted punch.

A useful diagnosis corrected an earlier reachability suspicion: the final right-hook reference already entered the contact envelope, but the physical glove lagged by up to roughly 13 cm at the important reference pose. Shoulder tracking error and post-IK angular-speed spikes mattered. The shifted stance reduced motion demand; increased feedback gains and revised aiming produced the accepted slowed case without raising strength. Reference smoothing before IK alone does not bound the speed of the final IK output.

The pilot's engineering acceptance gates are deliberately explicit: pelvis height remains above 70% of initial height, root up component above 0.70, sole drift and lift at most 3 cm, floor penetration at most 1 cm, double-foot contact in at least 95% of samples, and mean joint saturation at most 5%, plus an actual 0.14 m contact event. These are diagnostic thresholds, not new definitions of the Robust Safe Arrival safe set. Final recovery must be checked independently.

Timestep remains a gap: a 5 ms version diverged, and a second lower-gain 5 ms trial developed large drift/fell. Do not silently replace the accepted G1 plant's timestep or claim direct 200 Hz integration. The demonstrated human controller uses 2 ms physics and a torque update at every step; integration must explicitly choose and revalidate substepping or a controller/model stable at 5 ms.

4. GPU and software compatibility

The isolated physics environment uses accepted mjlab commit 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa, Torch 2.8.0+cu128, MuJoCo/MuJoCo-Warp 3.11.0, Warp 1.14.0, and RSL-RL 5.5.0. The current sibling mjlab checkout's newer dependencies are not installed over this stack.

Actual V100 jobs passed CUDA learning operations, standalone Warp kernels, batched floor contacts, 20-ball/60-motor dynamics, combined human/G1 mesh cameras, and the Task 08 torso camera dimensions/mount. L40S/mit_normal_gpu is not exposed on this server; jobs use the tested xeon-g6-volta allocation described in the GPU guide.

Native/Warp physics supporting ball joints does not make stock mjlab joint-position actions and scalar IdealPD compatible with this quaternion archive. The current custom controller handles quaternion error and 60 motor channels explicitly. For batched learned tracking, either implement GPU-resident quaternion-aware observations/actions/PD or finish a scalar-joint rig and re-retarget. Do not call the 87-qpos archive a standard scalar motion-tracking motion file.

The camera probe also found extreme near-field occlusion: at a near-contact preview frame the striking glove occupied about 93% of the G1 image, with median depth near 2 cm. Raw RGB/depth/ID rendering works; this does not validate the existing HSV calibration, valid-depth filtering, history/delay handling, encoder, or G1 policy. Two red physical gloves address one color assumption, not the complete perception integration.

The repeated-jab proof simulated 9.79 seconds with two contacts, both guard recoveries, 6.70 mm maximum foot drift, no saturation, and no root force. Its second launch was admitted only after actual guard errors stayed within tolerance for 0.10 seconds. See sequence evidence. Time-to-contact numbers in these pilots start at the controller cycle/clip start; they are not yet the PDF’s annotated outward-launch TTC. The stationary head makes the target positions coincide, but the clocks still differ.

5. Phases from here

Phase Current evidence Remaining acceptance work
A — Geometry and runtime contract Exact radii/equalities, both-glove events, frozen-target distinction, pinned GPU stack tested Wire the accepted labels and sensor adapter into a new two-entity task without changing old contracts silently
B — Physical rig and support 75 kg primitive rig; unassisted standing; positive mass/inertia and bounded control tests Anatomical scalar joints or stronger axis constraints, self-collision, property sensitivity, timestep compatibility
C — Feasible reference bank Four contacting kinematic families / 12 directional variants; support-aware reference pipeline Broader targets/heights, explicit outward-launch annotations and requested TTC bounds, acceleration/torque-feasible timing
D — Physical tracking Native and Warp jab contact; native isolated slower cross and both hooks passed Full-speed four-family tracking, robust recovery, perturbation tests, fully GPU-resident controller if training needs throughput
E — Continuous fixed-target opponent Boundary-only bank adapter; two physically executed jabs with measured guard recovery passed Mixed-family continuous sequences, held launch-head snapshot, explicit launch/TTC clocks and empirical reachability masks
F — Two-player training Interface/gap analysis and finite selector contract Instantiate a second policy, hold its plan across substeps, add plan/phase state to critic/replay, define adversary reward/termination, train/evaluate against fixed G1 first

Machine learning is not required to define the model, aim the motion, or demonstrate the first physical jab. Use motion optimization/time scaling and classical control first. A residual/imitation tracker becomes useful for fast motion and broader robustness after feasibility gates identify what it must learn. A learned tracker will not fix an unreachable target, wrong contact centers, inconsistent clocks, or state resets masquerading as recovery.

robust_sav still owns a single-G1 plant with fixed state/observation assumptions. A physical human changes model dimensions, contact masks, observation ownership, camera visibility, timing, resets, and opponent state. A separate scene/adapter is needed before integration. Missing G1 checkpoint/settled-stance artifacts prevent validating a real protagonist here; the baked standing G1 is an explicit fixture, not an inferred trained controller.

Reproduction and artifacts

From human-punching, after the original preview and G1 fixture have been generated:

bash scripts/setup_physics.sh
