sbatch scripts/physics_probe_volta.sbatch
sbatch scripts/warp_tracking_volta.sbatch --speed .6
sbatch scripts/physical_pilot_volta.sbatch \
  --input outputs/contact-targeting --balance --reaim --with-g1 \
  --punches jab --speed 1 --smooth 4 --center-target .10 --gain-scale 1.5

physical_pilot_volta.sbatch runs native MuJoCo physics and GPU EGL rendering. warp_tracking_volta.sbatch runs actual GPU physics with a host feedback controller. These are different tests; a GPU allocation alone is not evidence that physical integration ran on the GPU.

Reports and videos retain failed attempts as well as accepted cases. Unit/integration tests cover contact boundaries, attachments, torque signs/norms, freefall, absence of root actuators/forces, fixed-G1 FK/dynamics isolation, reference timing, and guard admission. The complete regression suite passed 71 tests plus 4 subtests; Ruff passed across source, tests, and scripts. Video payloads and independent reference/rollout audits are recorded with the final review artifacts.

Final media verification: all five review MP4s decoded successfully. All report links resolved. The full-speed Warp, continuous-sequence, and final review jobs completed with exit 0:0; see validation and Slurm accounting.

14 · Choosing the human model before learning a punching policy

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Choosing the human model before learning a punching policy

12 October 2026. This assessment answers why the pilot used a procedural mannequin, what its successful motions establish, and what should change before training. No model, reference, controller, or sibling repository was changed for this assessment.

Why the first model was procedural

The first requested milestone was a 1.80 m human executing four DuoBox punches in the same simulator as G1. The procedural mannequin preserves the existing capture skeleton and quaternion layout, needs no external body assets, and makes glove attachments and source FK easy to verify. A second stage added rigid-body physics underneath that surface to isolate contact, balance, control, and runtime questions.

This was a prototype choice. It was not a conclusion that a custom body is more suitable than SOMA or SMPL for the eventual learned human policy. The original Task 09 PDF already specified SMPL-X through SMPLSim as its primary body and SOMA23 as a fallback. Our later model survey in report 08 did not evaluate SOMA specifically; that omission must not be confused with the original plan. Neither planned standard body has been implemented here. See the plan-versus-implementation reconciliation. The existing 20-ball-joint rig should remain a diagnostic baseline rather than be treated as a finished anatomical training body.

The user-requested plain blue appearance can be retained with any suitable skeleton or physical model. Removing facial detail does not require removing anatomical joint constraints.

Three separate properties

Property What it supplies What our pilot establishes
Visual mesh Surface appearance and its attachment/deformation with the skeleton A featureless procedural surface follows the capture-derived skeleton and glove attachments
Physical rig Joint articulation, mass/inertia, contact, friction, passive forces, actuation and limits A coherent 75 kg articulated surrogate, free unactuated root, bounded torque control and real floor support
Executed motion Actual state trajectory produced by the controller in that physical rig A full-speed jab and slower other punches pass the pilot's contact/support gates; two jabs recover guard without reset

None of these alone establishes biomechanical validity. A detailed mesh can be attached to unrealistic joints; a simple visual surface can cover a reasonable articulated body. Mesh realism and physical realism must be assessed separately.

How physically reasonable is the current model?

Its dynamics are real: positive masses and inertias, gravity, bounded torque motors, an unactuated floating pelvis, and foot contacts. The accepted physical trajectories are produced by integration rather than prescribed pose playback. The native/Warp comparison and no-reset sequence provide useful implementation evidence. See the physical pilot report.

However, its human validity is limited:

  • All 20 articulated joints are ball joints. Elbows and knees have extra rotation axes compared with a constrained human approximation. A spherical rotation cone does not impose independent flexion, twist and lateral-bend limits.
  • Mass distribution and torque limits are engineering choices. They are not measurements of the DuoBox actor or a calibrated boxer.
  • Human self-collision is disabled. Successful support/contact metrics do not rule out body interpenetration.
  • The pilot mainly uses planted double support and a controller assuming that support mode. It does not validate weight transfer, stepping, recovery from pushes, or broader athletic motion.
  • The cross and hooks currently pass at 0.3 of source playback speed. Full-speed failures identify limitations of the current reference/controller/model combination; they do not show that a human could not throw the original punch.
  • Head/glove contact is an analytic 0.14 m center event with no G1 impact response. It validates the accepted avoidance hazard, not striking mechanics or force transfer.

Accordingly, the accurate claim is: the accepted motions are executable in the current surrogate simulation; anatomical feasibility for a real human remains unestablished. Captured human motion is a useful reference, but reconstruction noise, skeleton changes, IK, floor conditioning and timing changes can introduce poses or derivatives that the actor did not perform.

What SOMA would and would not give us

Assuming the intended SOMA is NVIDIA's current SOMA body representation, SOMA-X supplies a standard parametric body/hand representation, shared topology and rig conventions, skinning, fitting, and conversion tools. Those features would help with body proportions, consistent motion representation and asset interoperability. A parametric surface alone does not define calibrated strength, mass distribution or anatomical physics constraints.

There is also a concrete simulation starting point: NVIDIA's ProtoMotions SOMA23 configuration and MuJoCo asset. The inspected configuration contains 23 bodies and 66 articulated scalar DOFs. The XML includes primitive physical shapes, motors, joint damping/stiffness, and selected self-collision exclusions. Scalar coordinates could simplify integration with the pinned mjlab action/tracking machinery, although its skeleton and state layout differ from our current DuoBox archive.

It is not automatically anatomically constrained. The inspected default XML gives both forearms and shins three hinge axes with broad ±180° ranges. Thus adopting SOMA23 without changes would retain extra elbow/knee freedom in a different coordinate representation. Its passive stiffness/damping, motor gear interpretation, torque bounds and collision exclusions also need auditing under our controller; numerical defaults from another training stack are not proof of human biomechanics.

ProtoMotions documents physical motion tracking and multiple simulation backends, including a Newton path based on MuJoCo Warp. This is relevant prior work, not a tested drop-in dependency for our pinned mjlab/Torch/Warp environment. We have not imported the SOMA asset, installed ProtoMotions, or validated its GPU/runtime compatibility in this project.

SMPLSim is another route to a standardized body-derived MuJoCo model. It still requires retargeting, property/constraint audits, and attention to separate body-model asset requirements. DeepMimic demonstrates learned physics control of simplified humanoids, including martial arts; realistic skin detail is not a prerequisite for that approach.

These are sources inspected on 12 October 2026. Repository main branches may change; any implementation should pin the chosen source and asset revisions.

Recommended next development phase

Before substantial policy training, choose and freeze the physical training rig. A useful first comparison is a standard SOMA/SMPL-derived simulation asset against the current diagnostic rig, judged by control compatibility and physical constraints rather than appearance. Avoid paying for detailed skin or fingers unless the task needs them.

  1. Constrain articulation. Use knee flexion hinges; constrained elbow flexion plus separately represented forearm pronation/supination; bounded shoulder/hip/trunk and ankle axes; and enough wrist freedom to preserve glove orientation. Retain a free unactuated root and simple rigid fists/feet.
  2. Audit physical properties and forces. Freeze body scale, mass distribution, inertias, motor strength, passive forces, friction and collision filters. Account for every force source, including joint springs/damping, rather than reporting only capped motor torques.
  3. Retarget and certify references on that exact rig. Include hand orientation, foot support, temporal regularity, joint envelopes and self-collision. Measure final FK and derivatives after all IK edits. Preserve the 0.08 m glove / 0.06 m head / 0.02 m safety margin contract.
  4. Train motion imitation first. Learn a reference-conditioned controller for balanced full-cycle execution and guard recovery. Observations should include actual proprioception and reference/phase/target information; actions should pass through a documented bounded actuator controller. Reference imitation and physical penalties should discourage nonhuman exploits.
  5. Add goal-conditioned punching. Vary feasible head targets and timing, validate all four families at useful speeds, and assess recovery/generalization. Only then add adversarial hit objectives against G1 and the two-player integration.

An unconstrained hit reward could exploit the current extra joint freedoms, disabled self-collision or unrealistic strength assumptions. Learning optimizes the model it is given; it does not infer missing anatomy automatically. Conversely, failing our current PD controller is not evidence that imitation learning cannot execute a properly constrained reference.

A physically constrained rigid-body human with a plain blue surface is sufficient for an initial learned boxing policy. A biomechanically predictive study of injury, muscle activation or human force capacity would require substantially different validation; those claims are not needed for the current G1 avoidance objective.

15 · Task 09 training body: original plan versus implemented prototype

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Task 09 training body: original plan versus implemented prototype

12 October 2026. Assessment against the five-page Task 09 implementation plan, current project source, generated experiment records, and the earlier project reports. Page numbers below are physical PDF pages. This is a documentation update; it does not import a new body, train a policy, or change simulation behavior.

Direct answer

We have a physical body for controller and training-development experiments. We do not yet have the validated training body proposed in Task 09, a registered human imitation-learning task, or a trained human policy.

The original PDF explicitly selects SMPL-X through SMPLSim as the primary body, with ProtoMotions SOMA23 as a fallback. Those were already in the plan. The current custom mannequin is an intermediate diagnostic implementation, not an SMPL-X or SOMA export. Earlier discussion should have made that distinction explicit.

The architecture remains reasonable. The prototype strengthens the case for completing the plan's body/control audit before substantial training. It does not establish that the proposed standard bodies are unsuitable, or that a custom model is better.

1. What the original PDF actually specifies

PDF location Specification Interpretation
Page 1, sections 1–2 DuoBox fitted SMPL-X motion; SMPLSim-generated neutral adult near 1.80 m; one morphology initially A concrete primary asset path, not simply an unspecified human mesh
Page 1, section 2 SMPLSim model export; articulated rigid mesh segments with separate simple physical proxies The plan already permits simple rendering and collision geometry; seamless skin is deferred
Page 1, section 2 Free unactuated pelvis, root plus 21 articulated joints, proposed 63 scalar hinges; all three wrist rotations retained; fingers fixed This reduction requires new exporter work. The PDF explicitly says it is not a shipped preset
Page 1, section 1 SOMA23 fallback: 23 bodies, 66 scalar DOFs, wrists Its pretrained tracker is explicitly not assumed to transfer to mjlab
Page 1, section 2 Audit masses, inertias, anatomical ranges, feet/friction, self-collision, bounded PD, passive forces and simulator rates A generated model must pass physical checks; the PDF does not claim that a mesh provides these automatically
Page 2, sections 3–4 Constrained reference adaptation, complete cycles, frozen launch target and first-contact TTC Offline IK/reference preparation is separate from actual physical tracking
Page 3, section 5 WBC-mjlab human extension, motion-conditioned PPO tracker, bounded reference-relative joint commands Train physical imitation; motion data do not contain motor actions for the new body
Pages 3–4, sections 6–8 Measured guard recovery; continuous sequences; shared G1 scene; frozen tracker with boundary-only adversarial planning Keep low-level execution and adversarial plan selection separate
Page 5, sections 9–10 09A assets/data; 09B references; 09C imitation; 09D sequences/G1; 09E game Proposed timing and success thresholds are acceptance targets, not demonstrated capabilities

The named software route is therefore SMPLSim → constrained retargeting with Mink → WBC-mjlab imitation → robust-SAV integration. Native mjlab tracking is already offered as a smaller-dependency alternative; GMR and PyRoki are optional. Importing all these frameworks is not a requirement.

2. What exists in human-punching now

The physical model builder adds dynamics to the procedural blue surface. Its 21-body DuoBox hierarchy has 20 ball joints, 60 torque motors, an unactuated free pelvis, and 87 position / 66 velocity coordinates. Its chosen total mass is 75 kg including gloves. Gravity, primitive-derived inertias, floor contact and bounded torque control are active. Appearance remains independent of physical geometry.

This body can support experiments with a learned controller once the environment and policy interfaces are implemented. It is not currently a drop-in mjlab tracking asset: the quaternion joint coordinates need custom action/observation/PD handling, whereas the stock tracking machinery is oriented toward scalar robot joints.

The local inventory contains no implemented SMPLSim or SOMA body, and neither project environment contains SMPLSim, SOMA/ProtoMotions, Mink or WBC. The dependency cache contains the pinned mjlab source. DuoBox/fbx_fit_smpl does contain fitted-motion files; those must not be confused with the separate parametric body-model files or an exported physical body. Their compatibility with a future exporter has not been established by our raw-joint motion adapter.

Three designs appear in the documentation and must not be conflated:

Design Status
PDF: SMPL-X/SMPLSim, proposed 63 scalar hinges; SOMA23 fallback with 66 Planned candidates; not imported, reduced, or validated here
Model research report: proposed 34 actuated scalar DOFs Alternative reduced anatomy proposal; not implemented or validated
Current code: 20 ball joints / 60 actuated rotational DOFs Implemented and physically exercised diagnostic surrogate

The 34-DOF proposal would distribute glove orientation across forearm rotation and two wrist axes. It is a change from the PDF's three-axis wrist prescription, not an equivalent mapping already demonstrated by code. Any reduction must explicitly preserve the glove poses needed by all four punch families.

3. What the experiments establish

The physical pilot report and review manifest provide the measured evidence:

  • A jab at source playback speed contacts the analytic head envelope in both native MuJoCo and actual MuJoCo-Warp, with no root actuation. The Warp run records about 5.05 mm maximum foot drift.
  • The cross and both hooks have passing isolated native-physics cases at 0.3× source speed. Different conditioning/gains were used; this is not one controller demonstrated across arbitrary requests.
  • Two jabs at 0.6× speed execute continuously with measured guard recovery and no intermediate state reset.
  • Successful trials use 2 ms physics/control updates. The tested 5 ms variants failed their tracking/support checks. Accepted G1 integration uses 5 ms physics, so combining the systems still requires a deliberate timing solution and reevaluation.
  • Glove/head events use the accepted 0.14 m contact and 0.16 m safe-clearance thresholds. The G1 is fixed; impact response is disabled. This validates an avoidance hazard, not punch force transfer.

The body still has extra elbow/knee freedom, engineering rather than calibrated mass/strength parameters, and disabled self-collision. The controller assumes planted double support. These limitations are especially relevant for learning, which can exploit the model's freedoms.

Current contact delays start at the controller cycle/clip start. They are not yet the PDF's annotated outward-launch TTC. None of these demonstrations establishes the PDF's statistical tracker gates, all 16 family transitions, moving-G1 performance, or an accepted adversary.

4. Does the plan still make sense?

Yes as a staged design; no as a ready-to-run recipe or evidence of a finished training body. Several important safeguards are already in the PDF. Our experiments turn them into specific implementation requirements rather than reasons to discard the design.

Keep the body candidates, but validate the articulation

SMPLSim remains a reasonable first candidate because it exports SMPL-family MuJoCo bodies and aligns with the PDF's intended data path. This capability is documented by the official SMPLSim repository. Model-file availability, exact fitted-motion schema, exporter reduction and pinned-runtime compatibility still need checking. SOMA23 remains the explicit fallback if that route is impractical.

Do not treat a standard model name or scalar joint count as proof of anatomy. The inspected SOMA23 MuJoCo asset gives forearms and shins three hinge axes with broad ±180° ranges. It also includes passive springs/damping and motor gears that require careful torque accounting. It would need a constraint/control audit here. Likewise, the PDF itself warns that SMPLSim's broad exporter limits are not human biomechanics.

Choose joint axes and limits based on the intended human approximation, then validate reach and wrist orientation. Do not require exactly 34, 63 or 66 actuated coordinates before that work. A constrained rigid-body model is sufficient for this avoidance research; muscles, skin mechanics and detailed fingers are unnecessary.

Preserve the blue appearance independently

Our procedural choice was useful for the early visual/FK and runtime milestones. The user's request for a featureless blue body is not an argument against SMPL-X, SOMA, or anatomical constraints. A standardized physical rig can retain a simple blue surface. Conversely, a more realistic surface would not repair the present joints or controller.

Prefer the runtime we have actually tested

Start learning integration from accepted mjlab commit 8ee51fbcf806a7419189f706d9e394cbeb7790fa and the tested physics environment. The current sibling mjlab checkout and the PDF's WBC lock are different dependency combinations. Native mjlab tracking is a sensible first implementation path; use WBC only if its concrete benefits justify porting and dependency work. In either path, continuous reference clocks, immutable launch plans and physical guard transitions need project-specific code.

Resolve timing before claiming shared-plant compatibility

The current 2 ms human pilot is not compatible with 5 ms G1 physics merely by placing both XML bodies in one scene. Either achieve stable control at the accepted timestep or define a common finer physics clock that preserves declared actor/sensor schedules and then revalidate G1 behavior. Separate entities in one coupled simulation do not have independent physics timesteps. The PDF already requests rate checks; those checks have now exposed a concrete failure.

Learn earlier than an exhaustive classical-control campaign

The successful classical controller remains a valuable baseline. It is not necessary to make that controller execute every family at full speed before trying imitation learning. After the body, actuation, feasible references and reset/observation contracts pass a small pilot, train one reference-conditioned jab and assess recovery. Broader fast tracking is a legitimate goal for learning. Learning cannot fix invalid anatomy, unreachable targets, arbitrary TTC demands or inconsistent reference coordinates.

The initial 0.50/0.65/0.80 s TTC grid and tight tracking gates should remain proposed targets until launch annotations and measured feasible ranges exist. The current slowed hooks neither meet those gates nor prove that the recorded human motions are impossible.

5. Revised next milestones within the original phases

Phase Next work and exit condition
09A — Finalize the training body Attempt the planned SMPLSim asset route, using SOMA23 if needed; retain the existing body as a diagnostic baseline. Freeze one 1.80 m morphology, anatomical joint maps/limits, masses/inertias, passive forces, effort caps, soles and collision rules. Verify full glove orientation, unassisted guard support and a documented timestep. Integrate one scalar-joint action/observation path on the pinned stack. No extensive multi-body comparison or detailed skin work is needed.
09B — Retarget to that exact body Transfer the existing IK/FK and contact-contract work; re-solve poses after changing the skeleton. Start with a complete jab, then four families. Annotate outward launch and guard phases, export consistent derivatives, and measure feasible target/TTC ranges. Preserve source provenance and recording-level splits.
09C — Train physical imitation Register a human mjlab task; train one jab from guard with bounded actuation, no external root force and physical return. Then expand families, placements and feasible timing. Freeze observation/action/controller manifests with checkpoints. Evaluate actual execution, not reference playback.
09D — Continuous sequences and G1 Extend the two-jab proof to mixed-family transitions. Complete shared-entity state/action/reset indexing, the real camera pipeline, and no-homing tests. Evaluate a fixed/random valid opponent before learning adversarial choices. A real G1-policy evaluation needs its accepted artifacts.
09E — Adversarial planning Freeze the accepted tracker. Choose among multiple validated plans only at legal boundaries; include committed plan/phase and opponent state in learning interfaces. Preserve the prescribed sequence/count and report invalid attacks. Broader joint-level adversarial fine-tuning remains a later experiment.

Shared-scene/runtime checks should continue during 09A–09C rather than waiting until all imitation training is finished. Existing contact definitions, glove attachments, event logging, source audit, experiment fixtures and GPU validation are reusable; body-coordinate references and controller gains must be regenerated when the rig changes.

For scope clarity, the PDF's proposed src/boxing/human/ modules belong in this project's src/human_punching/ until the user explicitly authorizes changes to robust_sav. This assessment changed documentation only, submitted no GPU jobs, and performed no Git staging, commits or pushes.

16 · Scalar-joint training body: constrained SOMA23 derivative

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Scalar-joint training body: constrained SOMA23 derivative

12 October 2026. Body asset milestone for Task 09A. This report describes the asset and compilation checks; successful learned tracking and physical guard recovery are separate acceptance gates.

Choice and source

The Task 09 PDF chooses SMPL-X through SMPLSim first and explicitly allows SOMA23 as a fallback. We checked the local workspace for standard SMPL/SMPL-X body-model filenames; none was found. DuoBox's fitted pose/joint files do not supply the separate parametric body-model assets required by the SMPLSim exporter. We therefore used the planned SOMA23 fallback instead of making those assets a prerequisite for the learning pilot.

The source is ProtoMotions SOMA23, pinned to revision 7a8417a9f55b1586b0d6e8f46578c5bc9d7ce1f1. The original XML, reference config, Apache-2.0 license, third-party notices and file hashes are retained in assets/vendor/protomotions. No ProtoMotions runtime or pretrained controller is imported. The source config identifies the body as a 23-body reduction of SOMASkeleton30; this is not an SMPL-X export and does not include SOMA's detailed skinned surface.

Per the user's updated preference, native bone lengths were kept. The modified neutral body measures 1.7664 m from the lower sole to the top of the simple head envelope. There is no forced rescaling to exactly 1.80 m. The chosen total mass is 75 kg, including gloves. This mass is an experiment parameter, not an estimate of the recorded actor's weight.

What was changed

The builder retains all 23 named bodies but reduces the source's 66 hinges to 48 scalar hinges. There is one unactuated free pelvis: nq=55, nv=54, nu=48.

Region Controlled coordinates Rationale
Spine1, Spine2, Chest Three per link, 9 total Bounded flexion, lateral bend and twist
Neck1 Three Neck2 and Head are fixed relative links; one neck joint supplies head motion
Shoulders and upper arms Three per link, 12 total Preserve shoulder-girdle motion and arm orientation
Forearms Elbow flexion followed by axial pronation, 4 total Remove nonphysical sideways elbow hinge; keep forearm roll
Wrists Three per hand, 6 total Preserve glove orientation, with a small wrist axial range
Hips Three per leg, 6 total Hip motion
Knees One flexion hinge per leg, 2 total Remove sideways bending and free axial knee rotation
Ankles Three per foot, 6 total Bounded foot orientation; toes are fixed hierarchy landmarks

Canonical body axes are +Z up, −Y forward and +X left. Unlike the original asset's mirrored left X axes, all exported hinge axes use positive XYZ directions. The neutral free-root quaternion rotates the body 90 degrees around Z, producing world +X forward and +Y left. The forearm joint order is elbow Z then pronation X, so pronation occurs about the bent forearm instead of changing its flexion plane. Right elbow flexion is positive Z; left is negative Z. Both knee flexions are positive X. Tests verify the resulting FK directions, not just joint labels.

The model uses simple light-blue ellipsoids/capsules and whole-foot boxes, with no eyes, mouth, hair or detailed toes. Red gloves follow the complete hand transform. Each glove is a radius-0.08 m sphere centered on the corresponding LeftHand_center or RightHand_center site; the small dark cuff makes wrist rotation visible. These exact sites, not wrist origins, define the glove centers for the 0.14 m head-contact contract. LeftFoot_sole and RightFoot_sole, plus four sole-corner sites per foot, expose support geometry.

The original primitive envelopes and uniform-density masses were replaced. Per-segment masses are explicitly assigned and normalized to 75 kg; MuJoCo integrates the inertia from the selected physical primitives. The builder exports all masses/inertias through the compiled MJCF. The two neutral soles differ by 0.3 mm because the original skeleton is slightly asymmetric.

Actuation and collision contract

Every motor has gear 1, symmetric torque control and force limits. The XML has zero passive spring stiffness, zero passive damping, and zero friction loss. All requested restoring/damping torque belongs to the explicit bounded controller. Joint armature is 0.02 kg m². This avoids hidden source springs adding hundreds of Nm outside the controller's declared effort budget.

The initial effort caps are 100–120 Nm per trunk hinge, 20 Nm neck, 35 Nm clavicle, 100 Nm upper arm, 60 Nm elbow/pronation, 15 Nm wrist, 200 Nm hip, 150 Nm knee and 90 Nm ankle. These are per-coordinate engineering limits, not a measured strength envelope or a vector-norm cap for each anatomical joint. The gain/effort arrays, ordered joint names, coordinate addresses and angle limits are saved in scalar_model.json. Model-based reference preparation and training must use that same ordering and body hash.

All human primitives collide with the floor. Thirteen explicit self-collision pairs cover each glove against head, chest, lower trunk, pelvis and opposite forearm; both gloves; both shins; and both feet. Adjacent anatomical envelopes are not tested against one another because they intentionally overlap. This is selected self-collision coverage, not a complete self-collision model. There is no G1 impact body in this standalone asset.

The builder defaults to 5 ms for compatibility experiments and accepts a finer timestep such as 2 ms. Compilation and 0.15-second bounded-control stepping passed at both. That short check does not establish balanced standing, jab tracking or recovery at either timestep. Those must be measured using the retargeted reference and learned/controller execution.

Reproduction and validation

From human-punching:

.venv/bin/python -m human_punching.scalar_body --output outputs/scalar-body --timestep 0.005
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_scalar_body.py -q

The generated human.xml is self-contained; no mesh files or body-model package is needed to load it. The API build_scalar_human(output, timestep=.005, mass_kg=75.) returns the same metadata written to scalar_model.json, including the standing T-pose neutral_qpos, body/site maps and controller arrays.

Six tests passed. They verify pinned-file hashes, native height and mass, positive-definite full mass matrix, positive moving-link inertias and inertia triangle inequalities, one-to-one scalar-joint/motor ordering, actual torque clipping, zero root actuation and external force, knee/elbow FK directions, hand-attached glove centers/orientations, selected self-collision declarations, floor contact and finite short physical integration at both timesteps. Ruff passed on the builder and tests.

This makes the body usable for the next learning experiment, while keeping the remaining assumptions visible. Anatomical ranges, mass distribution and strengths are approximate; shoulder anatomy is simplified; rigid soles cannot reproduce toe mechanics; only selected self collisions are enabled. A learned controller can exploit these approximations, so accepted rollout metrics and visual review remain necessary. A realistic skin surface would not resolve these physical limitations.

17 · One-jab learned imitation pilot

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

One-jab learned imitation pilot

This is a small, motion-conditioned residual-control experiment. It uses the constrained SOMA-derived scalar-joint body and one complete retargeted jab cycle in the pinned mjlab/MuJoCo Warp environment. It is not yet the Task 09 adversarial planner, a target-generalizing punching policy, or a biomechanical validation.

Controller and learning contract

src/human_punching/jab_learning.py implements a Gaussian actor and value network, generalized advantage estimation, and clipped PPO. Its physics state stays on the allocated GPU through mjlab.sim.Simulation. There are no CPU physics steps in training. Native MuJoCo is used once during initialization to evaluate reference FK and an offline support-torque estimate.

The action is a bounded joint-angle residual around the committed reference. Joint position targets are clamped to the body's anatomical ranges, and final motor commands are clamped to each joint's torque range. All motors have unit gear and actuate scalar hinges. The free root has no motor. Neither external Cartesian forces nor generalized applied forces are used. Contact is simulated through MuJoCo Warp.

The optional feedforward term estimates gravity/velocity compensation with assumed double-foot support, using reference-pose contact Jacobians. The estimated support forces are never applied to the simulator: they only produce bounded joint motor torques. --no-feedforward disables that term. This calculation is an engineering initializer, not a physically guaranteed whole-body controller.

An optional --balance-feedback extension uses reference contact Jacobians and root mass blocks to turn root position/orientation/velocity error into additional joint torques. Its matrix has only actuated-joint output rows. Root acceleration requests are clipped to ±3, and final motor commands retain the same torque limits. It remains an approximate double-support controller; actual contact forces are neither overwritten nor imposed. This option is off in the first PPO experiment, and must be assessed as a distinct controller configuration.

Observations contain root pose/velocity, joint pose/velocity, reference errors, elapsed phase, the previous action, samples 0.10 and 0.25 seconds into the reference, and both glove position errors. Reward separately measures joint, root, body, glove-position and glove-orientation tracking. A late-cycle velocity penalty encourages quiet return to guard. Actions are updated every 20 ms; bounded PD torques are recomputed every 2 ms.

Training resets sample either guard or a reference phase, with small state perturbations. Falling terminates a training episode. This improves sample coverage but does not itself prove an uninterrupted full cycle. Evaluation therefore starts from guard, performs exactly one initial state reset per world, and runs the entire cycle plus final hold without resetting after a fall. Its first world starts at the exact guard and others use reproducible perturbations. Every evaluation physics step records state, torque, free-root actuator force, applied forces, and actual foot-floor contact support.

The elapsed phase is measured from the initial guard reset. It is not the paper's outward-launch time-to-contact definition. The fixed head center is used for the existing 0.14 m contact predicate; 0.16 m clearance and return-to-guard acceptance are checked independently by jab_evaluation.py.

Reproducing a run

Run from human-punching after generating the scalar body and validated reference:

sbatch scripts/train_jab_volta.sbatch \
  --reference outputs/scalar-reference-v3/jab.npz \
  --num-envs 128 --updates 200 --horizon 64 --save-every 25

The script uses this server's available xeon-g6-volta V100 partition, not an unavailable L40S partition. It respects Slurm's assigned CUDA devices and places caches under the project. It defaults to .venv-physics, the previously validated Torch 2.8 / CUDA 12.8 and MuJoCo Warp 3.11 stack. See 05-gpu-server-guide.md for the server details.

Each output directory contains a copied model directory, reference archive, body metadata, learner source, command arguments, and controller schema. The checkpoint also includes model/reference hashes, network dimensions, gains, joint order, action scale, observation layout, and timing. training.jsonl records PPO progress; baseline and learned rollouts are separate artifacts.

Reproduce the accepted checkpoint's fixed-bank evaluation:

sbatch scripts/train_jab_volta.sbatch --mode evaluate \
  --model outputs/jab-learning-5895253/model/human.xml \
  --reference outputs/jab-learning-5895253/reference.npz \
  --body-metadata outputs/jab-learning-5895253/body_metadata.json \
  --checkpoint outputs/jab-learning-5895253/checkpoint.pt \
  --evaluation-bank outputs/scalar-evaluation-bank/guard_trials.npz \
  --recorded-worlds 32 --attack-window .5 3.3888888888888893

Omit --checkpoint in evaluation mode for the matched reference-PD baseline. The checkpoint restores its timing/scales and validates its effective gains, joint order, action/observation schema, body and reference. Changed controller settings require a separate experiment. A fresh baseline can probe 5 ms with --timestep .005; checkpoint evaluation preserves its trained 2 ms setting. Compatibility with G1's accepted 5 ms physics requires measured validation.

Verified primary result

The original PPO-200 checkpoint passes all declared pilot gates in 32/32 independently audited fixed-bank trials. Its matched zero-residual controller passes 0/32. Every learned trial stays upright, respects the tested torque/joint limits, reaches the head-contact predicate during the declared attack window, keeps sole drift within the pilot limit, and returns to a supported quiet guard. Every recorded free-root actuator force and applied generalized/Cartesian force is zero. Evaluation contains one initial reset and no subsequent state overwrite.

The nominal learned trial reaches 0.11399 m glove/head center distance, with first contact at 1.75 seconds from the initial guard clock. Maximum sole drift is 4.95 mm and final guard dwell is 1.804 seconds. Final glove errors measured in the Chest frame are 6.35/4.80 mm and 2.14/1.41 degrees, with relative speeds 0.0042/0.0121 m/s. This is actual learned feedback: the same reference and PD controller with its network residual removed falls in all 32 matched trials.

The selected checkpoint is outputs/jab-learning-5895253/checkpoint.pt. Paired full-state evaluations are outputs/jab-learning-5895279 (reference PD) and outputs/jab-learning-5895280 (learned policy). The independent bank is outputs/scalar-evaluation-bank/guard_trials.npz, SHA256 e8ae40abcdbce50b42cea94a5f4b5e0e6535a4bec03fe61cb11cdbda73786c8a. It contains one exact initial guard and 31 fixed small perturbations: hinge standard deviation 0.005 rad with anatomical clamping, and velocity standard deviation 0.02 in the corresponding generalized-coordinate units. This tests local robustness around one body, guard, target and jab; it does not establish new targets, timings, morphologies, other punch families, or mixed sequences.

The declared attack window is 0.5–3.3888889 seconds of reference time. It excludes the explicit appended return blend but is not yet a precisely annotated outward/follow-through window. Reported contact times remain guard-clock times, not the paper's launch-relative TTC. There is no simulated forceful head impact: the accepted analytic center-distance contract supplies the contact event.

The accepted physics step is 2 ms with a 20 ms actor period. Native 5 ms tests failed; integration into G1's accepted 5 ms physics has not been achieved. First Warp initialization included several minutes of kernel compilation and Lustre I/O waits. The reported 444-second training duration excludes this cold startup and the baseline/final evaluations; it is not end-to-end wall time.

Reference v3 uses the previously conditioned, head-directed DuoBox jab at 60% of its source playback speed, with inserted quiet guard and return blend. The learner's --speed 1 means normal playback of that already slowed reference. This milestone does not establish full-speed human punching. The reference lasts 4.989 seconds and evaluation adds one second of final guard hold.

Experiment history

  • Job 5895225: eight-world synthetic-standing API smoke test; one PPO update completed on actual GPU physics, with a saved checkpoint and continuous evaluation. Both baseline and trained one-second standing trials had zero falls. This tests software plumbing and is not evidence of learned punching.
  • Job 5895242: cancelled during initialization, before training, when an additional audit found scalar-joint discontinuities in reference v2. Small Cartesian tracking error alone was insufficient to accept that reference.
  • Job 5895253: real-jab experiment on smoothed reference v3; 128 worlds, 200 PPO updates, 2 ms physics, checkpoint every 25 updates. Completed 1,638,400 transitions in 444 seconds of training after initialization and baseline evaluation. The 128-world guard-start learned evaluation had zero falls and 99.22% any-cycle contact, compared with 100% falls in its baseline. Mean continuous-evaluation reward increased from -0.474 to 0.977. These initial states are small perturbations of one morphology/guard/reference, not a broad generalization benchmark. Strict timing and guard gates are checked separately.
  • Job 5895265: checkpoint-50 guard-start evaluation; all 16 worlds still fell. This intermediate failure is retained; improved training reward alone did not establish success.
  • Job 5895277: separate 256-world balance-feedback PPO comparison. Intentionally stopped after checkpoint 100 was successfully loaded, at exactly 1,638,400 training transitions, matching the primary experiment's experience budget. Its fixed-bank evaluation is job 5895286. This is an exploratory controller comparison, not a repeated-seed statistical study. Its independent audit also passes 32/32 trials on all frozen gates, with mean continuous-evaluation reward 0.9883. The primary policy remains the original no-balance-feedback checkpoint, which demonstrates that the learned residual acquired the needed balance.
  • Jobs 5895279/5895280: paired zero-residual/learned-200 evaluations on the frozen 32-trial guard bank, recording every world and a declared attack window.

An independent native-controller diagnostic found that 2 ms inverse-statics feedforward plus reference PD initially stands, but develops slow root drift and falls after about 3.7 seconds during a long quiet trial. A 5 ms variant produces rapid joint oscillations and fails earlier. The initial PPO run therefore has a real balance problem to learn, rather than merely reproducing a successful classical whole-cycle controller. Results must be judged on guard-start physical evaluation, not training rewards alone.

The paired evaluations were independently checked against the immutable initial states, all 32 world IDs, model/reference hashes and bank hash before acceptance.

The first v3 guard-start reference-PD baseline has 100% falls over its six-second evaluation across 128 initial states. Some trajectories pass the 0.14 m glove predicate, but that does not make them valid punch-and-return demonstrations. The nominal rollout records exactly zero free-root actuator force and exactly zero generalized/Cartesian applied forces. Training rewards use episode resets; their values cannot be interpreted as whole-cycle success rates.

18 · Scalar jab pilot: independent evaluation and rendering

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Scalar jab pilot: independent evaluation and rendering

The first learned-controller milestone needs a recorded guard → jab → recovered guard physics trial. A plausible video or a successful offline reference is insufficient. src/human_punching/jab_evaluation.py independently recomputes the geometric metrics from saved simulator states and checks recorded actuation, applied forces, contact support, and reset provenance.

This is an engineering pilot acceptance contract. Its numerical tolerances are declared here and in PilotThresholds; they are not measured human population limits, an injury criterion, or a claim of 95% policy success.

Required evidence

An actual rollout NPZ contains:

  • time_s (aliases time or t), qpos, qvel, with one initial state and every subsequent physics step;
  • ctrl, qfrc_actuator, qfrc_applied, and xfrc_applied at the same cadence;
  • support_contact, two booleans per frame from actual left/right foot-floor support forces, rather than an FK proximity test;
  • state_reset_count=1, physics_step_count=number_of_saved_intervals, and physics_timestep_s if the simulator overrides the XML timestep;
  • evaluation_kind, such as reference_pd or learned_policy; model/reference/checkpoint provenance belongs in the run manifest and rollout where available;
  • reference_speed when the controller changes playback timing relative to its saved reference.

The model is loaded from that run's immutable model snapshot. Joint names, joint addresses, actuator joint transmissions and gear ratios come from the compiled XML, rather than a fixed DOF order. A model has one unactuated free root and scalar hinge joints. Glove sites are LeftHand_center and RightHand_center; sole sites are LeftFoot_sole and RightFoot_sole.

Missing evidence produces a failed or unverified gate. It is not filled in using assumptions. The replay itself never steps physics and cannot independently prove that a producer did not overwrite states; the reset counter must be checked against the rollout implementation. A strict all-pass result therefore means that the recorded trial and its declared provenance satisfy the checks, not that an arbitrary NPZ can prove physical execution on its own.

Pilot gates

Check Criterion
Physics provenance One initial reset; saved interval count equals physics-step count; every recorded interval matches the explicit runtime timestep
Starts at reference guard Root position ≤ 0.03 m, root orientation ≤ 0.10 rad, every scalar joint ≤ 0.12 rad from the reference initial state
No fall Root height always ≥ 75% of reference initial height; pelvis tilt from initial upright ≤ 0.60 rad
Foot motion Each sole's maximum 3D displacement from its initial position ≤ 0.03 m
Joint limits Maximum excursion beyond a compiled scalar joint limit ≤ 0.02 rad, allowing a small soft-limit tolerance
Bounded control and torque Recorded motor controls and actual generalized joint torques stay within compiled motor bounds, with 1e-6 numerical tolerance
No external assistance Every recorded generalized applied force and body applied wrench is zero within 1e-9
Unactuated root Every recorded root actuator-force component is zero within 1e-9
Attack-window contact At least one actual saved glove center reaches head-center distance ≤ 0.14 m during the declared attack window
Reference tracking Each glove's world-position RMS tracking error ≤ 0.10 m
Guard recovery After contact, the trial ends with ≥ 0.20 s continuous satisfaction of every return condition below

The return conditions are root position ≤ 0.03 m, root orientation ≤ 0.10 rad, scalar joint maximum error ≤ 0.12 rad, root linear speed ≤ 0.05 m/s, root angular speed ≤ 0.12 rad/s, every scalar joint speed ≤ 0.50 rad/s, both gloves within 0.03 m and 15° of their initial Chest-relative guard pose, both Chest-relative glove linear speeds ≤ 0.15 m/s, both gloves ≥ 0.16 m from the head, and recorded support at both feet. These are evaluated against the initial reference guard. Merely passing through guard once, remaining in initial guard, or ending with moving arms does not qualify. Chest-relative velocity is computed with body/site Jacobians as R_chest.T @ (v_glove - v_chest - omega_chest × (p_glove - p_chest)), so whole-torso rotation and translation are removed correctly. This pilot returns to one fixed initial guard; tracking a moving or newly selected guard is later work. The explicit glove gates were added before inspecting PPO results, not adjusted to admit a resulting controller.

Reference alignment uses linear interpolation for scalar positions and SLERP for the free-root orientation. The declared playback speed is applied. The final reference is held after its end. Glove orientation error and root tracking error are reported separately even though the initial strict pilot gates do not impose additional RMS thresholds on them.

Contact uses the established 0.06 m head / 0.08 m glove proxies. The first-contact clock is explicitly elapsed time from guard reset, not the PDF's outward-launch TTC. For this pilot, contact acceptance is restricted to reference-clock playback launch at 0.5 s through explicit return-blend start at 3.3888888888888893 s, divided by the controller playback speed. Raw any-cycle contact is reported separately, so a late return contact cannot substitute for an attack. This is a coarse window that still includes recovery present in the source clip; reviewed outward/follow-through annotations remain future work. Missing phase metadata fails the attack gate. Supply --attack-window 0.5 3.3888888888888893 for copied references whose original manifest is not adjacent. The target is fixed and has no physical impact response. The 0.16 m glove-clearance predicate is only one part of robust-SAV safety.

The current MuJoCo-Warp step saves post-integration qpos/qvel, while cached forces and contacts describe the forward pass for the preceding integration interval. Therefore all geometric contact/pose metrics are recomputed by independent FK from saved qpos. Support flags are interpreted as actual positive foot-floor forces over the preceding interval. A counted recovery interval requires recovered geometric/velocity state at both saved endpoints and positive support at its right endpoint; an isolated recovered sample contributes zero dwell. Dwell-boundary resolution is one physics step (2 ms in these trials). No extra forward pass is inserted into learned-policy observations or stepping.

A single passing nominal trial is not a perturbation benchmark. Report each recorded perturbed trial separately and state the number tested before summarizing success fractions.

Commands

From human-punching, evaluate without rendering:

.venv/bin/python -m human_punching.jab_evaluation \
  --model outputs/RUN/model/human.xml \
  --rollout outputs/RUN/learned/rollout_00.npz \
  --reference outputs/RUN/reference.npz \
  --output outputs/RUN/independent-evaluation \
  --attack-window 0.5 3.3888888888888893

If an older rollout omits its actual simulation timestep, add --physics-timestep 0.002 after verifying that value against the run's controller schema. Do not infer success from the original XML's default timestep when the simulator overrides it.

Render a kinematic reference diagnostic:

sbatch scripts/render_jab_volta.sbatch \
  --rollout outputs/scalar-reference-v2/jab.npz --kind reference

Evaluate and render an actual learned trial:

sbatch scripts/render_jab_volta.sbatch \
  --model outputs/RUN/model/human.xml \
  --rollout outputs/RUN/learned/rollout_00.npz \
  --reference outputs/RUN/reference.npz --kind learned \
  --attack-window 0.5 3.3888888888888893

--kind classical labels a reference-PD baseline. --scene-model can supply a scene containing the same human and a baked fixed G1 for rendering; the original model still controls evaluation. The scene must preserve the exact human qpos/qvel order. physical_scene.attach_static_g1 verifies that order while baking the fixture.

The video has oblique and side views, full-time and half-time replays, actual center distances, and explicit kinematic / learned / reference-PD labels. It replays recorded states without interpolation or state alteration. Renderer-only overrides enable physical geom group 3, make both gloves red against the light-blue body, and brighten the headlight. They do not modify the source XML, its hash, mass, inertia, contact geometry, or dynamics.

Initial diagnostic, 12 October 2026

The corrected V2 reference independently passed its geometric checks: minimum left-glove distance 0.1011 m, sole drift 0.131 mm / 0.254 mm, no scalar joint-limit excursion, and exact final guard return. outputs/scalar-reference-v2/independent-fk-audit/evaluation.json correctly reports pilot_pass=false: this input is a kinematic reference, with no recorded actuator forces, physical foot support, or one-reset physics provenance. Its zero tracking error is a self-comparison and provides no controller evidence.

GPU previews outputs/jab-render-5895244 and outputs/jab-render-5895248 show V2 with a target sphere and with the fixed G1, respectively. These are early kinematic diagnostics. Later reference/controller artifacts and their independent evaluations supersede them; this section does not claim a learned result.

First reference-PD baseline: run 5895253

The independent evaluator applied the strengthened Chest-relative guard gates before inspecting a PPO result. The nominal unlearned baseline outputs/jab-learning-5895253/baseline/rollout_00.npz fails the pilot:

Measured result Value
Minimum left-glove / head-center distance 0.16016 m (miss)
Minimum root height 0.07499 m (fall)

The single nominal trajectory passes the recording, bounded-actuation and declared joint-limit gates but fails balance, foot drift, target contact, reference glove tracking and guard return. Small joint-space tracking errors alone do not prevent whole-body collapse; the independent root/world and Chest-relative checks make that distinction explicit. See outputs/jab-learning-5895253/baseline/independent/evaluation.json. This is an unlearned reference-PD baseline and does not establish the outcome of the learned controller.

Paired evaluation bank

outputs/scalar-evaluation-bank/guard_trials.npz contains 32 fixed trials generated by scripts/make_jab_evaluation_bank.py, using seed 20261012 and NumPy PCG64. Trial 0 is exactly the V3 first guard pose with zero generalized velocity. Trials 1–31 add independent Gaussian hinge-position noise with 0.005 rad standard deviation, clipped to the compiled limits, and Gaussian generalized-velocity noise with standard deviation 0.02 (m/s for root translation, rad/s for root angular and joint velocities). Root positions and quaternions remain unchanged.

Random draws are interleaved per trial: joint position vector, then complete velocity vector. The first eight trials regenerated as an eight-trial bank exactly match the first eight of the 32-trial bank, making ordering independent of requested batch size. The bank records model and reference hashes; its manifest records the attack window without rewriting the frozen reference.

Both baseline and learned controllers must start from these exact arrays and record every trial. scripts/audit_jab_bank.py checks bank hash, model/reference hashes, all 32 unique trial IDs, and initial states within 3e-7 for float32 transfer before computing per-trial acceptance and aggregate counts. This is a paired small-perturbation test on the same jab motion, not held-out motion, target, body, or opponent generalization.

.venv/bin/python scripts/audit_jab_bank.py \
  --model outputs/EVALUATION/model/human.xml \
  --reference outputs/EVALUATION/reference.npz \
  --rollouts outputs/EVALUATION \
  --bank outputs/scalar-evaluation-bank/guard_trials.npz \
  --output outputs/EVALUATION/independent --workers 4

Paired learned result: 32 trials

The immutable 32-trial bank was evaluated by baseline job 5895279 and learned-policy job 5895280. Independent replay verified exact initial states (allowing float32 transfer tolerance), model/reference hashes, bank hashes and all 32 unique trial IDs before scoring. The learned controller came from PPO run 5895253, checkpoint after 200 updates. The comparison keeps the same reference-PD and offline support feedforward; the learned controller supplies policy residuals.
