The per-trial JSON files and complete summaries are in outputs/jab-learning-5895279/independent/ and outputs/jab-learning-5895280/independent/. These results establish a small-perturbation pilot on one fixed jab and one fixed head target. They do not establish performance on another punch, target, morphology, moving G1, or large disturbance distribution.

A separately recorded nominal training-run evaluation also passes all gates: minimum attack distance 0.11399 m, first sampled attack contact 1.750 s after reset, minimum root height 0.96347 m, maximum sole drift 4.95 mm, and recovered guard dwell 1.804 s. Final Chest-relative glove errors are 6.35 / 4.80 mm, 2.14° / 1.41°, and 0.00419 / 0.01206 m/s. Its video is outputs/jab-render-5895283/jab-rollout.mp4, with independent metrics in outputs/jab-learning-5895253/learned/independent/evaluation.json. The video shows actual learned-controller state replay beside the fixed G1; G1 is a visual target with no impact response.

Across all 32 accepted primary-policy trials, the closest attack distance ranged from 0.0980 to 0.12986 m and final recovered dwell from 1.800 to 1.824 s. Maximum final Chest-relative glove error was 7.54 mm / 2.31°, and relative speed was at most 0.0290 m/s. Maximum sole drift was 5.89 mm; peak joint torque was 64.72 N·m; the largest soft joint-limit excursion was 0.009714 rad, within the declared 0.02 rad tolerance. Root actuation and external applied forces remained exactly zero in every trial. Full ranges are saved in outputs/jab-learning-5895280/independent/range_summary.json.

The final reference preview (jab-render-5895255), actual learned rollout (jab-render-5895283), and corrected baseline diagnostic (jab-render-5895275) each passed a complete FFmpeg decode with exit code zero and no errors. Each render directory contains video-validation.json.

An exploratory variant with explicit online balance feedback plus learned residuals also passed all frozen gates in 32/32 trials (outputs/jab-learning-5895286/independent/summary.json). That evaluation uses checkpoint 100 of the separate 256-world training run, with the same 1,638,400 training transitions as the primary 128-world/200-update run. It is a secondary comparison; the selected milestone controller remains the original policy without that extra online balance-feedback helper. This small shared bank does not establish that either controller is generally superior.

Five-millisecond, four-family extension: acceptance contract

The October 15 extension preserves the existing physical pilot thresholds and adds explicit timing and actuator evidence. Earlier results above remain 2 ms historical experiments; adding an evaluator option does not convert those checkpoints to 5 ms.

The proposed native implicit-PD actuator uses gain=1, affine bias [0, -kp, -kd], and the original strength bounds as compiled forcerange. Its drive input is kp*q_target + kd*v_reference + feedforward. That input is not torque and can exceed the strength limit without violating it. The independent evaluator consequently requires every-step actuator_force, verifies each force against its compiled cap, transmits those forces through the XML gear/joint mapping, and compares the result with recorded qfrc_actuator. It still checks actual joint torques, zero root actuation, and zero externally applied forces. Unlimited drive is accepted only for the recognized force-limited direct-hinge affine actuator. Historical direct-torque motors retain their original control-range checks.

New releases must explicitly request the 5 ms physics and 20 ms action gates. Physics acceptance requires agreement among the frozen runtime XML, recorded timestep, every recorded state interval, and the actual simulator time saved as simulation_time_s. The latter must advance by 5 ms and agree with elapsed nominal time, allowing only dtype-specific floating-point accumulation error. Synthetic timestamps alone cannot pass. Action acceptance requires a recorded 20 ms interval and actual update indices [0, 4, 8, ...] before their corresponding physics intervals. Recording at 5 ms while using a different compiled timestep cannot pass. The end frame has no following control interval and is excluded from the update list.

.venv/bin/python scripts/audit_jab_bank.py \
  --model outputs/RUN/model/human.xml \
  --runtime-model outputs/RUN/model/human_runtime.xml \
  --reference outputs/RUN/reference.npz \
  --rollouts outputs/RUN \
  --bank outputs/BANK/guard_trials.npz \
  --require-physics-timestep 0.005 --require-policy-timestep 0.020 \
  --output outputs/RUN/independent --workers 4

--model remains the bank's immutable source-body XML for provenance. --runtime-model is the frozen compiled-controller XML; its hash must match every rollout. Exact runtime-file names follow the run manifest. These requirements make model/controller changes visible without rewriting the previous body or bank.

The first 5 ms GPU smoke run (5931602) exposed a serialization error: mj_saveLastXML preserved numerical affine coefficients but omitted changed actuator-type/limit flags. The saved XML therefore recompiled as a capped torque motor even though the live GPU controller used affine PD. Independent auditing caught this mismatch. Those files remain diagnostic artifacts, and a schema-reconstructed analysis explicitly fails runtime-file provenance. New accepted evaluations must use a correctly exported, recompiled runtime XML. The evaluator now checks the recorded controller mode against compiled actuator semantics, as well as the runtime-file hash; a matching hash alone cannot prove that a serializer captured the live controller correctly.

Annotated references identify the punch family, active hand, absolute source-speed factor, and attack-window semantics. The evaluator additionally requires contact by the designated active hand. It reports full-cycle and attack-window glove RMS, actual and reference first-contact times, their signed timing error, and linear threshold-crossing estimates at the available physics resolution. Positive timing error means the actual punch is late. Interpolation estimates a crossing between measured distance samples; it does not create substep measurements. An unreviewed or coarse window stays explicitly marked as such.

The plan's 2 cm active-glove RMS and 20 ms contact-timing error are reported separately as precision targets; they do not silently replace or relax the existing pilot gates. A passing physical pilot can still miss those tighter goals. Each family's fixed bank tests small guard perturbations of that reference only, not unseen motions, moving targets, continuous combinations, or biological realism.

19 · Scalar human and learned jab milestone

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Scalar human and learned jab milestone

12 October 2026. Implementation is confined to human-punching; sibling repositories are read-only. No Git staging, commits or pushes are part of this work. The initial milestone is achieved: a constrained scalar body, a complete jab reference, and a learned physical tracker that passes all declared pilot gates in 32 matched guard-start trials.

Watch the learned jab beside G1 · Contact still · Checkpoint · Bundle manifest · Learning curve

This is one fixed-target jab at 0.6× source speed, with small initial-state perturbations. It is not the full four-family Task 09 tracker or adversarial game.

Body choice and completed asset work

The original plan's SMPL-X model files were not available in the inspected project inputs. DuoBox fitted-motion arrays are available, but they do not supply those body-model files. We therefore used the plan's explicit fallback: the SOMA23 MuJoCo asset from ProtoMotions, pinned at 7a8417a9f55b1586b0d6e8f46578c5bc9d7ce1f1. Upstream source, hashes and notices are retained under assets/vendor/protomotions.

The user relaxed the exact-height requirement. The model retains its native bone lengths and measures 1.7664 m in its neutral standing pose. The physical mass is our declared 75 kg scenario, not a measurement of a capture actor.

The generated body has 48 actuated scalar hinges, 55 position coordinates and 54 velocity coordinates, including a free unactuated pelvis. Knees are flexion hinges; elbows separate flexion from forearm rotation; wrist rotation is retained. Head/finger/toe articulation is reduced. Effort limits, gains, coordinate maps and selected collision pairs are explicit in scalar_model.json. There are no passive joint springs/dampers. Both sole contact and 13 selected self-collision pairs are enabled.

The visible body uses plain blue primitives derived from the simulator rig. This is a SOMA23-derived physical skeleton, not an imported SOMA-X skinned mesh or a calibrated musculoskeletal model. See the body audit. The renderer explicitly colors gloves red for inspection without changing physical parameters or the frozen source XML.

Retargeting the jab

scalar_reference.py transfers the previously conditioned DuoBox jab onto the exact compiled scalar model. It uses both glove positions/orientations, stationary sole poses, root/torso posture targets, selected self-collision avoidance, and scalar joint limits. It inserts a quiet initial guard and a smooth source-pose return to the initial guard. The nominal source playback speed is 0.6× for this learning pilot; this does not establish a prescribed fast-punch TTC.

The first solver exposed an important failure: solving an unconstrained Jacobian update and clipping joint angles afterwards left knees stuck at their bounds and produced large sole errors. Bounded least squares fixes that coupling. A second audit caught about 0.14 rad of joint-coordinate change at guard boundaries despite tiny Cartesian errors; removing the initial seed-pose bias eliminated those spikes. Earlier outputs are retained as diagnostics, not accepted references.

The frozen training reference is outputs/scalar-reference-v3/jab.npz, with manifest:

Geometric diagnostic Result
Duration, including guard/return padding 4.9889 s
Closest attacking glove/head center distance 0.101116 m
Maximum glove-target position error 0.291 mm
Maximum sole drift 0.250 mm
Sole height range −0.026 to 0.590 mm
Penetration among enabled self-collision pairs None detected
Maximum scalar joint speed 3.043 rad/s

All reference poses stay within the declared scalar limits. The final held configuration equals the initial guard. Velocities use MuJoCo tangent-coordinate differentiation, including the free-root rotation. The 0.14 m contact and 0.16 m safe-clearance definitions are unchanged.

The reported reference contact is about 1.5844 s after the archive starts, or 1.0844 s after the explicit 0.5 s quiet guard. That launch convention is a declared playback annotation; it is not a reviewed source outward-launch label or a certified requested-TTC contract.

Training and evaluation implementation

jab_learning.py implements a small reference-conditioned PPO tracker using the already-tested mjlab/MuJoCo-Warp stack. Batched dynamics and policy learning execute on the allocated V100 GPU. The policy adds bounded scalar joint-target residuals to the reference; joint PD plus a documented support feedforward calculation produces capped motor torques. Computed support forces are never applied externally. Observations include proprioception, reference errors, future targets, phase, glove errors and previous action.

Training may reset to different reference phases. Acceptance evaluation always starts at guard and runs continuously through punch and recovery, with one initial reset. Checkpoints include the body/reference hashes, controller settings and observation/action contract. Runs snapshot their input body, reference and trainer source.

Independent checks live in jab_evaluation.py and the evaluation contract. They use actual recorded physics states and forces, both-glove contact/clearance, joint/torque limits, sole drift, and measured supported guard recovery. A collision during a fall is a failed attack. A reference replay cannot pass the physical-evidence checks.

The initial controller uses 2 ms physics and 20 ms policy updates. This remains separate from accepted G1 timing. No compatibility claim is made for coupling this controller to G1's 5 ms plant.

Experiments and failures

Job Purpose Finding
5895225 Eight-world standing GPU/PPO smoke Actual batched scalar physics, one PPO update, checkpoint save and continuous evaluation completed. This is an infrastructure check, not a trained jab.
5895250 Native zero-residual PD; 3 s quiet guard then v2 jab Failed at both 2 ms and 5 ms. At 2 ms, the pelvis slowly drifts despite planted feet, then falls. At 5 ms, explicit PD develops immediate joint oscillation/saturation. All actuator bounds and zero-external-force conditions remained enforced.
5895253 PPO on corrected v3 jab, 128 worlds 200 updates / 1,638,400 policy transitions. Training took 444.3 s after initialization and baseline evaluation. The learned six-second guard-start evaluation had no falls in 128 worlds; full independent acceptance was then checked on the fixed bank below.
5895279 / 5895280 Matched 32-trial baseline / learned evaluation Same body, reference, controller gains, physical clocks and exact initial-state bank. Baseline passed 0/32 complete cycles; learned policy passed 32/32.
5895270 / 5895274 Native optional balance-feedback diagnosis / matched no-feedback control Joint-only feedback greatly reduces initial guard drift, but neither native test completes the longer quiet-guard-plus-jab protocol. Late return-phase contact is rejected.
5895277 / 5895286 Secondary PPO with optional joint-only balance feedback / matched evaluation The saved 100-update checkpoint also passes 32/32 pilot trials. It used 256 worlds and the same 1,638,400-transition budget. Training was intentionally stopped after saving this checkpoint; the selected primary policy remains 5895253.

The native failure log is outputs/scalar-native-5895250. Its head-envelope entry during a fall is explicitly rejected. These tests explain why geometric reachability and bounded torques alone do not establish a balanced punching controller.

Accepted learned result

The primary checkpoint was trained without the optional classical root-error feedback. It uses reference joint PD and the offline support feedforward calculation, with learned residual actions supplying the successful balance/tracking corrections. The zero-residual baseline uses the same remaining controller and fails. This comparison demonstrates a material learned-control contribution.

The independent paired evaluations use one exact guard and 31 fixed perturbations: 0.005 rad standard-deviation scalar joint noise, clipped to limits, and 0.02-unit generalized velocity noise. Root position/orientation are not perturbed. Both rollouts are verified against the exact same bank arrays and hashes; neither resets after time zero. These are descriptive results on a small fixed bank, not a population reliability estimate.

Independent gate Reference PD baseline Learned tracker
Complete pilot: every gate passes 0/32 32/32
No fall throughout cycle 0/32 32/32
Contact during declared attack window 12/32 32/32
Supported, safe, quiet guard recovered 0/32 32/32
Bounded recorded motor torque and no external/root actuation 32/32 32/32

Across the 32 learned trials, closest attacking glove/head distance is 0.0980–0.12986 m, maximum sole drift is 5.89 mm, and the final recovered-guard dwell is at least 1.800 s. Final chest-relative glove position error is at most 7.54 mm, orientation error at most 2.31°, and relative speed at most 0.029 m/s. Peak actual joint torque is 64.72 Nm, with every joint respecting its own configured motor cap. Maximum physical joint-limit excursion is 0.00972 rad, within the declared 0.02 rad soft-constraint tolerance. Root actuator forces and external applied forces are zero.

Guard acceptance requires both gloves within 3 cm / 15° of the chest-relative initial guard, low relative velocity, quiet root/joints, both feet supporting the body, and both gloves at least 0.16 m from the head for a 0.2 s dwell. Contact alone is insufficient. The attack window is the coarse declared reference interval 0.5–3.3889 s, not yet a manually reviewed outward/follow-through interval.

The video replays actual recorded GPU physics states, rather than the reference poses. It adds the existing standing G1 as a fixed visual fixture with unchanged human coordinates and places the same analytic head target at G1's proxy center. It does not show a learned G1 policy or impact response. Reference, failed-baseline, and learned videos all decoded completely with FFmpeg.

Authoritative geometry/contact metrics come from independently recomputed FK at every recorded 2 ms state. Warp's cached body geometry and contact/force arrays describe the preceding integration interval; policy evaluation preserves that trained observation convention. Guard dwell checks use the recorded positive foot forces and consecutive recovered states, with at most one-step boundary uncertainty. This timing detail is documented rather than changed only at evaluation.

Limits of this milestone

  • This is one training seed, one morphology, one jab source, one fixed target and small guard perturbations. Other punches, broader initial states, different targets, speed requests and repeated sequences require further training/evaluation.
  • The reference runs at 0.6× source speed. The primary nominal attacking-glove tracking RMSE is about 4.14 cm, and worst per-trial glove RMSE is 4.41 cm. These pass the declared preliminary 10 cm pilot gate but do not meet the PDF's proposed 2 cm tracker gate.
  • Nominal physical first contact is 1.750 s after guard reset versus reference contact at 1.5844 s, about 166 ms later. This is not the PDF's proposed 20 ms TTC accuracy. Full outward-launch annotation and timing training remain open.
  • The accepted controller uses 2 ms physics; tested 5 ms classical variants are unstable. G1 integration must resolve the shared physics clock explicitly. The current checkpoint must not be silently run under different gains or timing.
  • The model has engineering joint limits, masses, strengths and selected self-collision pairs. This is a more constrained training surrogate than the original all-ball rig, not a calibrated prediction of human biomechanics.

The next useful experiment is faster and more accurate jab tracking with broader guard perturbations, followed by additional punch families on this same frozen body. Mixed sequences, camera/perception integration and adversarial plan selection remain later milestones.

Reproduction

From human-punching, with the existing pinned physics environment and conditioned source jab available:

# Export the fixed scalar body (do not overwrite assets used by an active job).
.venv/bin/python -B -m human_punching.scalar_body --output outputs/scalar-body

# CPU reference preparation; run substantial batches on an allocated CPU node.
.venv/bin/python -B -m human_punching.scalar_reference \
  --source-dir outputs/physical-5890952 --output outputs/scalar-reference-v3

# One allocated GPU; source snapshots and run artifacts stay under outputs/.
sbatch scripts/train_jab_volta.sbatch \
  --reference outputs/scalar-reference-v3/jab.npz \
  --num-envs 128 --updates 200 --save-every 25

The source jab is a derived artifact from the earlier physical pilot, rather than a new independent motion recording. Its path and hash are saved with the reference. Regenerating that source follows the earlier pilot commands; exact retargeting results depend on those conditioning settings.

The review bundle contains the selected checkpoint, reference, body, controller schema, training log/curve, matched evaluation summaries, nominal actual rollout, video, and upstream source notices. Its file hashes and source jobs are recorded in the manifest. The bundle README provides evaluation commands using only its frozen controller assets and the installed project environment. See the training guide and evaluation contract for detailed commands.

Final verification

The full preview-environment suite passed 94 tests and four subtests; one Torch-dependent module was skipped there. Its seven learning tests passed separately using Torch from the pinned physics environment and pytest from the preview environment. Ruff passed across src, tests and scripts. All three comparison videos passed complete FFmpeg decoding. The bundle's 29 files were checked against their SHA256 manifest entries, and all local links in the README and milestone reports resolve.

The primary training, matched evaluations, final rendering, and secondary evaluation jobs completed. The secondary training job was deliberately cancelled after its saved 100-update checkpoint reached the comparison budget. No GPU jobs remain running. validation.json retains the test/media evidence. Nothing was staged, committed or pushed.

20 · Genuine 5 ms human physics: controller investigation

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Genuine 5 ms human physics: controller investigation

Date: 2026-10-15. This report concerns numerical control and is separate from learned-policy acceptance. The body, 75 kg mass, segment inertias, 48 scalar joints, joint limits and original strength limits are held fixed.

Why the old controller struggled at 5 ms

The accepted early jab checkpoint used 2 ms physics and 20 ms policy updates. Its proportional/derivative (PD) calculation happened in Python/Torch, and the result was sent to plain torque motors. Although the simulator selected implicitfast, it could not see the velocity derivative of this externally computed force. The PD damping therefore behaved explicitly.

The accepted initial guard has a largest generalized damping eigenvalue lambda_max(M^-1 D) = 778.5152409958 /s, where M is the compiled full mass matrix and D contains the existing joint derivative gains, with zero free-root entries. For the frozen, unconstrained pure-damping linearization, explicit integration requires h * lambda < 2:

Physics step h * lambda_max Local explicit-damping result
2 ms 1.5570 Below the bound
5 ms 3.8926 Above the bound

The corresponding bound is about 2.569 ms. This is a diagnosis of an unstable local numerical mode, not a proof that every 2 ms motion is stable or that every 5 ms motion must fall; contact, stiffness and torque saturation alter the full system. It explains why simply changing the old physics clock is not a credible solution.

Native, bounded PD actuation

The new helper scalar_control.py expresses exactly the same continuous PD force using a native MuJoCo affine actuator:

drive = kp * desired_joint_angle + kd * desired_joint_velocity + feedforward
actual_torque = clip(drive - kp * joint_angle - kd * joint_velocity,
                     original_minimum_torque, original_maximum_torque)

The native actuator has gainprm[0] = 1, biasprm[:3] = [0, -kp, -kd], unit gear and no activation dynamics. Its output is limited with forcerange, after all feedback and feedforward terms. Its input drive is no longer itself an applied joint torque, so checking the input against the old motor torque range would be incorrect. Independent evaluation must check the actual actuator force and generalized joint force against the unchanged output limits.

This exposes the damping derivative to implicitfast. It does not add passive damping, increase armature, relax strength or joint limits, actuate the root, add external support, reset a falling state, or use hidden 2 ms substeps. A 20 ms action hold contains exactly four 5 ms physics steps.

This approach is already supported by the pinned software: mjlab's BuiltinPdActuator documents native position/velocity actuation for stiff PD, and the installed MuJoCo-Warp derivative kernel includes affine actuator velocity bias. Warp suppresses that derivative when the output force is saturated and clamps the actual output with forcerange. MuJoCo's force generation and numerical integration documentation describes these native force laws and implicit velocity integration.

The conversion creates a distinct runtime model; the earlier body XML and checkpoints remain immutable. Old checkpoints must not silently be evaluated under the changed controller semantics.

Focused verification

test_scalar_control.py checks:

  1. Native output torque equals the original clipped PD law at representative positive/negative states, including both saturation directions; root actuator forces remain zero.
  2. An isolated low-inertia hinge converges with native damping at a real 5 ms timestep, while explicit damping at the same gains and timestep diverges.
  3. Exporting the full human changes no masses, inertias, body positions, joint ranges, damping, armature, dimensions or original output strength limits.
  4. An actuator without a provable output-force cap is rejected.

All four tests passed. These tests establish implementation properties and numerical mechanism; they do not substitute for free-standing, learned GPU rollout evaluation.

Two additional runtime-export round-trip tests were subsequently added after the serialization issue below: six controller tests now pass, covering both explicit motors and native PD.

Native full-body probe

control_5ms_probe.py compares explicit versus native PD at 2 ms and 5 ms, with the previously available bounded joint balance feedback both disabled and enabled. Each deterministic trial starts once, holds guard for three seconds, executes the entire accepted jab reference, then holds guard for one additional second. The probe saves every physics state, actuator force, generalized actuator force and applied external force. It checks the MuJoCo clock on every step to reject resets or an unexpected timestep.

The first CPU submission, Slurm 5931585, failed during Torch import because the worker could not resolve libnccl.so.2; no simulation ran. It ran on the same worker d-19-14-4 that produced filesystem errors in another concurrent task. The retry, 5931597, ran on d-6-4-4 and explicitly added the already installed project-local NCCL directory to the dynamic-library search path. It completed all eight cases. Avoid the problematic worker for follow-up CPU jobs; the evidence does not isolate the first failure to a package deficiency.

The saved numerical results show the following at 5 ms, without learning:

Actuator Joint balance feedback First fall Three-second guard: peak joint speed Guard: maximum root drift Attack-window glove/head minimum
Explicit PD Off 1.225 s 31.67 rad/s 1.419 m 1.373 m
Explicit PD On 1.505 s 26.90 rad/s 1.765 m 1.081 m
Native PD Off 3.245 s 1.32 rad/s 0.471 m 1.643 m
Native PD On 7.785 s 1.32 rad/s 0.00641 m 0.1735 m

The native controller with joint balance feedback also limits initial sole drift to 3.15 mm. The numerical oscillation is removed, but the jab still misses the 0.14 m contact criterion during its attack window. It later reaches 0.1265 m on recovery and then falls; that late contact is not a successful attack. Native PD by itself does not solve free-standing balance. This makes the distinction between a numerically usable actuator and a learned punching controller concrete.

Across all eight cases, actual output torque respects every original strength cap; applied external forces and root actuator forces are exactly zero. The 5 ms records contain 1,798 steps over the full quiet-guard, jab and final-hold sequence. These long deterministic probes deliberately continue after a fall to show what happens; there are no hidden recovery resets.

The initial gray interval is the three-second guard hold. Native damping removes the fast oscillation, while the later loss of balance shows why numerical stability alone is insufficient. Vector figure.

A runtime XML export bug found by independent audit

The first GPU smoke run, 5931602, and training jobs 5931617/5931618 used the correct native actuator arrays in memory. Their initial runtime XML export, however, used mj_saveLastXML after mutating a loaded MjModel. That export copied the numeric biasprm and output force range but retained the original ctrllimited=true and omitted biastype=affine. Recompiling the saved file therefore reconstructed a different actuator: a plain motor that also clipped the drive input. A downstream evaluator using that file would audit the wrong controller. The automatic save also rounded body mass/inertia and joint limits; the recompiled total mass was 75.000012 kg instead of the original 75 kg.

The forensic runtime audit records this discrepancy without rewriting those historical files or their hashes. It is a serialization failure, not evidence of unbounded forces in the actual training simulation. An independent GPU force audit checked all nine saved smoke-run rollouts, each containing 1,199 samples. The recorded actual forces satisfy the native clipped PD law to 3.03e-5 Nm maximum numerical error. The recorded generalized hinge force equals the actuator force exactly, every original torque cap is respected, and root actuation/applied external forces are exactly zero. Reconstruction correctly uses the preceding physics state, since these cached force values describe the interval that produced the newly recorded state.

The fix is export_runtime_model(source, destination, model) in scalar_control.py. It copies the exact source XML body, explicitly writes the actual actuator enum/boolean fields and all gains/biases/limits, and saves the actual timestep, solver settings, gravity and flags. It then recompiles the result and requires exact equality of relevant body, actuator and solver arrays before accepting the export. It refuses a changed body it cannot faithfully serialize. This avoids both missing actuator semantics and unnecessary mass/inertia rounding.

The ongoing training weights remain usable because the actual GPU dynamics were correct. Accepted results must come from new evaluation jobs with the correct export and matching runtime hash; the old exports remain diagnostic evidence. New evaluations should also record the simulator's own per-world clock, in addition to the nominal sampling timestamps, to provide direct evidence of the physical step count.

Early full-speed jab precision diagnostic

The independent checkpoint-100 evaluation 5931645 exposed two specific remaining problems before further training. These are intermediate findings, not the final status of the subsequent checkpoints.

The worst joint-limit excursion was 0.022247 rad at LeftHand_z at 1.230 s, against the unchanged 0.020 rad gate. The reference reaches the 0.610865 rad upper limit while rapidly decelerating the wrist: its velocity drops from roughly 3.8 rad/s near 1.16 s to zero by 1.24 s. The actual wrist lags this braking and briefly reaches 0.633112 rad. Its actuator is already applying a braking torque at the peak. Clamping the desired angle to the joint limit therefore does not guarantee that inertia and a compliant physical stop cannot produce a small transient excursion.

If later checkpoints do not resolve it, a direct joint-limit penalty is a meaningful first change: penalize the worst actual excursion across joints and all four 5 ms steps, rather than averaging it over 48 joints or checking only 20 ms action boundaries. Here the worst sample lies between action boundaries. A small declared inset of commanded target angles is another legitimate controller option, but it changes the controller contract and requires new training/evaluation. Relaxing the physical joint limits or the acceptance gate would not address the problem.

The nominal first contact was 40 ms late. Full-cycle active-glove RMS error was only 1.494 cm, but attack-window active-glove RMS was 2.952 cm. At reference contact, the actual glove was approximately 2.51 cm behind in x, 2.37 cm offset in y and 5.59 cm low in z. A diagnostic temporal alignment over 0.9–1.4 s finds roughly 20 ms lag, but still leaves 2.78 cm position RMS. Consequently the 40 ms threshold-crossing delay is not simply an actuator latency that can be cancelled by advancing the reference 40 ms.

The current precision reward averages both glove errors before applying its exponential reward and also scores the long guard interval. If further tuning is needed, separate active-hand tracking during the attack from inactive-hand guard tracking, and give the attack position/velocity errors direct weight. Keep the intended reference clock and timing evaluation fixed. The policy already observes future reference states, so it can learn physical anticipation. More complete acceleration feedforward is a later option: the current offline feedforward compensates gravity and velocity effects but omits the reference mass-times-acceleration term. That requires careful reference-acceleration and support-force validation and is a larger change than targeted reward tuning.

Acceptance still required

The relevant downstream milestone is a learned actor validated on MuJoCo-Warp at 5 ms physics / 20 ms actions. Native CPU diagnostics alone cannot establish that. Evaluation must use the saved runtime actuator model, actual force caps, one initial reset, all physics samples, no external/root assistance, and the same independent contact, balance and supported-guard checks used for the earlier milestone. Punch speed and tracking accuracy must then be improved at that exact clock, followed by the remaining punch families.

21 · Scalar-body references for the 5 ms learning milestone

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Scalar-body references for the 5 ms learning milestone

15 October 2026. Work remains confined to human-punching; source repositories and the accepted historical artifacts are read only.

Purpose and scope

The earlier learned milestone used one scalar-body jab at 0.6× source speed. This stage extends the same bounded full-body retargeting pipeline to the jab, cross, left hook and right hook, and initially attempts each at 1.0× source speed. These are offline geometric targets, not evidence that a policy can physically execute them. The independent 5 ms rollout evaluations determine physical acceptance.

The original approximately 1.77 m, 75 kg, 48-hinge SOMA23-derived body supplies exact forward kinematics and joint limits. Changing actuator implementation for 5 ms integration does not require changing the reference coordinates when articulation, geometry and inertial properties remain identical. The reference manifest records its original XML hash; the training record must separately identify the actual simulation XML and controller.

Sources and declared conditioning

Family Source run Active hand Existing source modifications
Jab physical-5890952 Left Smoothed, planted soles, root/leg support IK, re-aimed at the head
Cross physical-5890924 Right Smoothed, planted soles, support IK, re-aimed
Left hook physical-5890924 Left Smoothed, planted soles, support IK, re-aimed
Right hook physical-5891006 Right Same conditioning plus a 12 cm forward stance offset

The prior cross/hook controllers used 0.3× playback at runtime. Their saved source timestamps retain the source clock, so --speed 1 here does not perpetuate that slowdown. These are already conditioned source motions; “source speed” describes the timestamp multiplier and does not mean untouched motion capture or independently verified boxing realism. Per-reference manifests preserve the conditioning fields and the original capture-candidate identity.

The four families currently have different initial guards and stance positions, inherited from their source clips. They are isolated training tasks, not a sequence that can switch freely without a transition controller. No claim of continuous mixed-family combinations follows from this reference bank.

Retargeting and timing

scalar_reference.py solves bounded least squares using compiled MuJoCo position/orientation Jacobians. It preserves both glove targets, plants both soles, applies torso/root and elbow priors, softly centers the COM, and discourages penetration of selected self-collision pairs. The initial guard receives a longer solve; quiet holds reuse the exact same solved pose rather than allowing null-space drift.

The default complete cycle consists of a 0.5 s initial guard, the source crop at the requested speed, a 1.0 s quintic return blend, and a 0.6 s final guard. Increasing punch playback speed does not shorten the inserted recovery or quiet holds. The final configuration exactly equals the initial configuration. Velocities use MuJoCo tangent-space differences, including the free-root quaternion.

Each archive exports the family, active hand, source speed, explicit phase samples, launch time, return start, guard-hold start, both glove centers, first contact and a declared attack window. The stricter attack window starts at playback launch and ends 150 ms after closest active-glove approach, capped at source playback end. This is a geometry-derived follow-through allowance, not a reviewed outward-phase label from capture; attack_window_reviewed=False records that limitation. A coarse source-playback window is retained separately for provenance. Recovery-only contact must not satisfy the attack gate.

The manifests audit joint limits, maximum step/velocity/acceleration, guard-boundary steps, endpoint equality, sole drift/height, glove target error, contact and selected self-penetration. These diagnostics expose geometric solver discontinuities; they are not acceleration/torque feasibility certificates.

Execution and reproduction

From the project directory:

# Four CPU jobs, one per family; native MuJoCo IK, no GPU needed.
sbatch scripts/retarget_scalar_punches.sbatch

# A single reference with a different playback factor and fixed recovery time.
.venv/bin/python -B -m human_punching.scalar_reference \
  --punch cross --speed 1 --return-duration 1 \
  --output outputs/my-cross-reference

.venv/bin/python -B -m pytest tests/test_scalar_reference.py -q

The first CPU array, 5931576, failed before Python because node d-19-14-4 returned No such device while creating a shared output directory. Array 5931593 completed its IK work but exposed a metadata bug: the shared first-contact API requires two glove channels; computing active-only contact must supply the active channel twice. That annotation was corrected without changing the physical contact contract. A later array, 5931623, stalled similarly on node d-19-13-3 and was deliberately cancelled. Successful replacement arrays ran on d-18-4-4. These are infrastructure/annotation failures, not evidence of dynamic infeasibility.

Conditioning failures and fixes

The unmodified scalar transfer, array 5931598, produced a clean source-speed jab. Cross and hook transfers had larger angular speed and acceleration demands. The right hook also overlapped its own gloves by 6.96 mm during its preparatory motion. The source body had not enforced this hand/hand collision constraint.

A stronger leg-posture prior was tried in array 5931614. Although it reduced some support motion, it worsened the cross's peak joint velocity from 13.64 to 26.41 rad/s by changing the redundant IK solution. That candidate was rejected. Increasing a regularization weight is not automatically a smoother trajectory.

The accepted right-hook correction changes only the inactive glove target, pushing it smoothly away from the active glove when the two targets approach within 0.17 m. Its largest target change is 28.63 mm. The active punch target, model radii, strength and joint limits are unchanged. The 0.17 m self-clearance target is distinct from the glove/G1 head contact contract of 0.14 m.

Cross and both hooks then received Gaussian pose smoothing with a 25 ms standard deviation, without changing timestamps. Scalar angles are averaged within their existing bounds; root rotation is smoothed in a local rotation-vector chart and reconstructed as a unit quaternion. The inserted quiet guards remain exactly fixed. This reduced angular accelerations, but direct coordinate smoothing introduced 8–13 mm of sole motion because forward kinematics is nonlinear. Array 5931630 records that intermediate result.

The final pass, array 5931641, re-solves the sole constraints while retaining the smoothed glove path and a strong local posture prior. Sole drift returned below 0.25 mm while preserving the acceleration improvement. This does not pull the hands back to the original unsmoothed path. Maximum glove-path changes from the earlier conditioned targets are 25.60 mm for cross, 49.24 mm for left hook, and 36.29 mm for right hook. These changes are declared motion conditioning, not tracking error by the learned policy.

Accepted geometric bank

The immutable bank is scalar-four-reference-v1. The jab is copied byte-for-byte from 5931598; the other families are copied from 5931641. Each includes the exact reference-generator snapshot, source hashes, conditioning parameters and metadata. All four use 1.0× source playback, a 0.5 s initial quiet guard, a 1.0 s inserted return and a 0.6 s final quiet guard.

Family Complete reference duration First contact after reset Contact after playback launch Closest active glove/head centers Peak joint speed Peak joint acceleration Maximum sole drift
Jab 3.833 s 1.1506 s 0.6506 s 0.10118 m 5.07 rad/s 102.1 rad/s² 0.250 mm
Cross 3.800 s 1.0953 s 0.5953 s 0.12763 m 10.48 rad/s 168.6 rad/s² 0.084 mm
Left hook 3.950 s 1.3527 s 0.8527 s 0.10537 m 13.20 rad/s 236.2 rad/s² 0.069 mm
Right hook 3.908 s 1.2239 s 0.7239 s 0.07121 m 10.19 rad/s 293.7 rad/s² 0.210 mm

All four pass finite-state/unit-quaternion checks, scalar joint limits, selected self-collision checks, exact initial/final configuration equality, zero endpoint velocity, planted-sole checks, and sampled active-glove contact within the declared attack window. The inactive glove remains outside the head contact envelope. The cross has the shallowest contact margin and may require especially accurate physical tracking.

Compared with unsmoothed transfer, peak acceleration decreases from 403.7 to 168.6 rad/s² for cross, 480.2 to 236.2 for left hook, and 464.6 to 293.7 for right hook. These remain kinematic derivative measurements, not evidence that the chosen strength bounds can realize those accelerations. Four targeted regression tests pass, including tangent-space free-root derivatives, limit-aware IK recovery, timing independence of return duration, and quaternion/bounds/quiet-guard preservation during smoothing. Ruff passes for the changed source and test files.

No physical execution is asserted by this reference-generation report. Separate 5 ms learned rollouts must pass balance, effort, active attack contact, final supported guard, tracking and timing checks. The existing 2 ms jab checkpoint and these references alone do not satisfy that requirement.

Why the source-speed hooks needed another timing pass

Subsequent learning attempts and an independent inverse-dynamics audit exposed a limitation that planted feet and COM geometry had not detected. The source-speed hook trajectories asked the weak proximal shoulder joints to transmit large inertial loads. The full-frame audit found peak upper-body torque demands of approximately 2.78× the declared limit for left hook and 2.21× for right hook. Static COM projections remained approximately 10 cm inside the support polygon. Static balance was therefore not the principal issue.

The largest loads were not simply spikes in the shoulder joint's own acceleration. For the left hook's shoulder-Y overload, gravity alone contributed roughly 9.9 Nm, whereas gravity plus velocity-dependent terms contributed roughly 74.8 Nm before acceleration raised total demand to approximately 90.9 Nm. The existing shoulder cap is 35 Nm. Changing the redundant shoulder angle can shift some demand but cannot eliminate the transmitted momentum of the entire moving arm. These numbers are model-based reference requirements, not measured forces from a trained policy.

Two stronger shoulder-posture regularizers were tried in arrays 5931729 and 5931733. They reduced the left-hook peak by roughly 15% but still required about 2.37× the limit; the right hook remained about 2.27×. Array 5931749 additionally anchored pelvis orientation near guard and relaxed source wrist-orientation tracking. Its peaks remained about 2.38×/2.30×. These candidates were not adopted. All model masses, inertias, torque caps and joint limits remained unchanged.

The next candidate changes when the same configuration path is traversed. retime_punch_reference.py applies an explicit monotone PCHIP phase warp. It borrows 250 ms from the inserted initial guard, reducing that quiet hold from 0.5 to 0.25 s, and spends more time on the high-load swing. Complete cycle durations, contact poses and the inserted final recovery/guard interval are retained. The first contact after reset remains near the prior time. Velocities and accelerations are recomputed on the new clock, including the timing transition effects.

This is not uniform source-speed 1.0× playback. The NPZ deliberately omits the scalar source_speed, records the nominal pre-warp multiplier separately, and exposes the complete phase-warp knots and local speed range. Runtime --speed 1 means playing this modified archive at its own recorded clock. It does not restore the original capture timing.

The immutable retimed hook bank contains:

Reference Local source-rate range during playback First contact after reset Maximum joint speed / acceleration Sole drift Inverse-dynamics peak fraction of original torque cap
Left hook 0.471–1.377× 1.3544 s 8.17 rad/s / 102.6 rad/s² 0.80 mm 1.045×
Right hook 0.491–1.374× 1.2014 s 5.56 rad/s / 85.1 rad/s² 1.25 mm 0.762×

The full-frame inverse-dynamics screen finds all 236 right-hook reference frames compatible with its ideal foot-force constraints and original torque limits. Left hook is substantially improved but retains three frames exceeding the shoulder cap by at most 4.48%, plus three frames for which the exact prescribed root wrench is infeasible in the screening contact model. A learned controller may alter the pose and timing slightly, but it must demonstrate success under the unchanged physical gates; the screen is not a reason to relax those gates.

The retimed references retain exact final/initial guard equality, legal joint coordinates, selected self-collision clearance and head contact. Small extra sole drift comes from configuration-space interpolation between samples and is reported explicitly. Left-hook reference preview and right-hook reference preview are kinematic previews, not learned-motion evidence. Independent learned evaluations are reported separately.

Generation jobs were 5931790 and 5931791; diagnostic job 5931826 screened the resulting references. Focused reference, timing and release-provenance tests cover the retained changes. Release packaging instructions distinguish physical pilot acceptance, precision targets and explicitly admitted nonuniform timing.

Independent review of the retimed attack windows

The frozen archives retain attack_window_reviewed=false: their windows were derived from launch and closest approach, not manually annotated against the original capture. A separate derived-window review now checks their intended geometric meaning. It recomputes forward kinematics at 5 ms sample spacing along the frozen configuration trajectory and examines active/inactive glove distances, radial velocity, all contact episodes and the later inserted return. These are kinematic samples, not a physics simulation.

Reference Derived attack window Only active contact episode Inbound radial speed at first contact Minimum active distance during inserted return
Left hook 0.2500–1.5333 s 1.35436–1.40043 s −2.35 m/s 0.564 m
Right hook 0.2500–1.3807 s 1.20140–1.26517 s −4.03 m/s 0.490 m

Both derived windows pass this scoped review. Each contains a single approach/contact/withdrawal episode by the declared active hand; the inactive glove never enters the 0.14 m contact envelope. By the window end, the active glove is withdrawing, and it remains beyond the 0.16 m safety distance for the rest of the cycle. Thus the accepted reference contact belongs to the intended strike, not a later guard-recovery pass. Dense versus archived first-contact calculations differ by only 0.068 ms for left hook and 0.010 ms for right hook.

Blue shading denotes the derived attack window; gray shading denotes the inserted return to guard. The red vertical line marks first contact. These plots and the review script support derived_window_review_pass=true in a separate report without changing the frozen reference hashes or claiming source-capture annotation review. They do not establish learned rollout acceptance, impact-response physics or the tighter learned contact-timing target.

The two linked kinematic preview videos were also completely decoded by FFmpeg without errors; the per-video video-validation.json files preserve those results. Closest-approach stills were visually inspected. Review job 5931877 initially stopped because the rendering environment lacked Matplotlib; replacement CPU job 5931886 used the existing physics environment and completed the numerical review and video decoding.

22 · Five-millisecond physical tracking: experiment log

Historical snapshot. Read this in its original stage. Later reports supersede earlier inventory and milestone claims. Original Markdown file.

Five-millisecond physical tracking: experiment log

Started 15 October 2026. Completed objective: establish genuine 5 ms physics with 20 ms policy updates, then improve the jab and learn cross, left hook and right hook on the same constrained scalar body. All writes are confined to human-punching inside Azizan_Lab/HumanBox. Sibling repositories are read-only. No Git staging, commits or pushes.

Completed milestone: four learned punches at 5 ms

All four selected policies pass 64/64 fresh trials each, all 18 physical gates, and all three tighter targets: full-cycle active-glove RMS ≤2 cm, attack-window active-glove RMS ≤2 cm, and interpolated contact-time error ≤20 ms. Each matched zero-residual controller passes 0/64. These are 256 accepted isolated cycles, not 256 independent recordings or trained policies.

Family Learned passes Zero-residual passes Attack-glove RMS (mm) Maximum contact-time error (ms) Minimum guard dwell (s) Reference timing
Jab 64/64 0/64 4.98–7.32 4.43 1.835 Uniform 1.0×
Cross 64/64 0/64 6.92–9.06 9.87 1.735 Uniform 1.0×
Left Hook 64/64 0/64 8.53–13.01 8.38 1.735 Nonuniform retiming
Right Hook 64/64 0/64 6.00–10.08 0.93 1.755 Nonuniform retiming

Each test bank was generated after selecting its checkpoint using the existing 32-trial validation bank. A test bank contains one shared nominal anchor and 63 fresh small perturbations (joint-position σ=0.005 rad, joint-velocity σ=0.02 rad/s; unchanged root pose). Contact-time estimates interpolate distances on a 5 ms clock; their sub-step decimals do not imply sub-millisecond physical measurements. Both gloves must be safely clear during recovered guard, with recorded foot support and quiet posture/velocity. The 0.14 m contact and 0.16 m safe-clearance definitions remain unchanged.

The frozen four-family bundle and hash manifest preserve checkpoints, controller/body/reference snapshots, all learned and paired-baseline rollouts, trial audits, source snapshots, plots and videos. The validation log records exact job IDs and selection/test-bank provenance; the controller interface explains execution and remaining G1 integration differences.

Required contract

  • Exactly 0.005 s per integrated physics step; four steps per 0.02 s policy period. No hidden 2 ms integration or changed reporting clock.
  • Original 48-hinge articulation, free unactuated pelvis, chosen 75 kg mass scenario and original motor output-strength limits. No external root assistance, pose replacement or mid-cycle resets.
  • Every evaluation starts at guard and records every physics step through attack and supported recovery. Fixed initial-state banks distinguish actual control success from random-phase training diagnostics.
  • Existing 12 pilot gates remain, plus explicit physics/policy cadence and actual actuator-force checks. The proposed stronger targets are active-glove RMSE ≤2 cm and reference-contact timing error ≤20 ms; results against these are reported separately and must not be silently substituted for the pilot gate.
  • Faster references aim first at 1.0× source playback speed. Quiet guard and return padding are declared separately. This is not a promise of arbitrary requested punch frequency.

Diagnosis and implementation

The prior explicit PD law computes velocity damping outside MuJoCo. Consequently, implicitfast cannot include that derivative in its implicit damping solve. The accepted guard has a coupled leg/ankle damping mode whose local explicit-integration bound is about 2.57 ms; a 5 ms step crosses that bound even though the continuous torque law is reasonable.

The implemented replacement uses a native affine actuator:

force = clip(drive - kp*q - kd*v, original torque bounds)

drive = kp*q_target + kd*v_reference + support_feedforward

This exposes damping to implicitfast while preserving the bounded force law, articulation, mass and strength. Drive input is not applied torque: the evaluator must audit actual actuator_force and generalized actuator force. Runtime XML and controller schema are frozen, rather than claiming the original source XML fully describes the altered actuator implementation.

The new launcher is scripts/train_punch_5ms_volta.sbatch. It enforces 5 ms / 20 ms, snapshots the full local Python package, and records control/evaluation provenance. Learning can explicitly initialize from previous policy weights, but that is documented as transfer with a fresh optimizer, never as an unchanged-checkpoint evaluation or exact resume.

Per-policy-boundary FK refresh computes derived geometry without changing qpos/qvel or advancing time. This removes the old observation's previous-substep geometry lag in the new experiment; old checkpoints retain their original observation convention unless explicitly used for new training.

Experiments

Controller diagnosis is in report 20; reference construction is in report 21. This report records the learned experiments and final acceptance evidence.

5931870 Cross with worst-joint guard-return reward Targets the neck-posture failure without changing physics, gains, allowed joint/guard errors or punch timing.
Runtime provenance failure and repair

The first smoke/training jobs had a serialization error: mj_saveLastXML did not preserve the in-memory native actuator enum/limit changes. Those saved XML files cannot establish correct downstream replay. Actual GPU forces were independently checked against the native PD equation and respect the original limits; the trained weights remain usable. The corrected exporter writes the actuator semantics explicitly while preserving exact source-body properties, recompiles, and checks equality. New acceptance jobs freeze this corrected runtime XML and verify its hash. Historical diagnostic artifacts are retained unchanged. Report 20 contains the forensic evidence.

First full-speed independent jab result

Checkpoint-100 evidence uses one nominal guard and 31 frozen small perturbations, seed 20261015. All 32 trials have zero applied external/root forces, no fall, active-hand attack contact, and at least 1.845 s of supported recovered guard. Maximum sole drift is 10.85 mm.

Full-cycle active-glove RMSE is 1.44–1.60 cm; attack-only RMSE is 2.77–3.14 cm. Contact is 34–44 ms later than the reference. The wrist transient reaches 0.02225 rad beyond its soft upper stop, compared with the pre-existing 0.02 rad allowance. Thus this checkpoint is strong evidence that genuine 5 ms control works, but it is not an accepted complete release and does not meet the stronger contact-timing target. The limit threshold and reference clock are not adjusted to make it pass.

The simulator's own clock is recorded at every step. Independent checks require 5 ms increments, matching nominal timestamps, and policy-update indices every four physics steps. A 20 ms policy period does not imply an extra artificial 20 ms sensor delay.

Precision improves before joint-stop behavior

The checkpoint-400 audit greatly improves glove tracking and contact timing, yet increases the transient wrist-stop excursion. Longer training is not automatically better: the 600-update final checkpoint's online tracking reward later degrades. Checkpoints are selected by independent whole-cycle measurements rather than their update number.

The first corrective experiment adds a cost on the worst actual joint-limit excursion, taking a maximum across all joints and every one of the four 5 ms steps. This catches a wrist excursion between policy observations. It does not move the physical joint stop, strengthen the motor, change the allowed excursion, or advance the reference clock. A lower learning rate limits disruption of the already accurate punch.

The initial left-hook transfer also exhibited large policy changes and repeated training falls. An optional PPO target-KL early stop limits further optimizer steps when the sampled divergence grows beyond 1.5 times its configured target. It is a training safeguard, not a physics change or a guarantee that no individual update can exceed the target.

The penalty-only jab illustrates why world-space fist tracking is insufficient for guard acceptance. It retains millimetre-level world-space glove errors while slightly changing torso/arm posture, leaving the gloves about 3.1 cm from their initial Chest-relative guard. A new training term therefore targets the same relative geometry as the existing acceptance check. Neither the 3 cm threshold nor the 0.2 s supported dwell is relaxed.

The chosen jab is checkpoint 100 from run 5931736, SHA256 bafa080cb9025e8b70eeb7697e7efb4e4eb9981b104cdd25044a5b58c28978c0. After the 32-trial selection pass, a fresh seed-20261016 test bank was generated: one shared nominal anchor and 63 previously uninspected perturbations. The learned controller passes all 64 trials and all tighter precision targets; the identical zero-residual controller passes none. This is one trained motion and small initial perturbations, not a general robustness claim.

Fresh learned evidence · Matched baseline · Recorded-physics video beside G1.

Additional families require attack shaping and dynamic references

Initial cross and hook transfer runs show that high average imitation reward can coexist with a missed attack. The revised reward explicitly weighs the active glove during the geometrically derived attack interval. A rational distance score retains a useful learning signal when a hook initially misses by tens of centimetres, where a sharp exponential tracking reward is nearly zero. This is separate from the inactive-hand and final-guard objectives.

The first full-speed left-hook run (5931665) was intentionally stopped after checkpoint 400; the conservative variant (5931683) was stopped after checkpoint 200. Their retained outputs record why they were superseded. They are failed/diagnostic branches, not successful policies.

Offline inverse-dynamics diagnostics show a further obstacle: exact full-speed hook tracking can require more than twice a configured shoulder effort limit. Static center-of-mass margins are healthy; fast coupled motion is the issue. A slower curriculum followed by explicitly conditioned timing resolves the largest dynamic loads while the original strength limits remain fixed. These calculations assume exact reference tracking and simplified support-force allocation; they guide reference design rather than proving that all nearby punching motions are impossible. See the diagnostic evidence.

Learning and scope

Each family currently has its own reference-conditioned residual PPO network. The four networks share the body, actuator mechanism, observation/action layout, 5 ms physics and 20 ms decision period. They are specialized trackers, not yet one network conditioned on a punch-type command. Their guards/stances differ, so selecting a family at a fresh guard reset is distinct from switching families during continuous motion.

Jab and cross preserve 1.0× source playback, with explicit 0.5 s initial guard, 1 s return blend and 0.6 s final guard. Hooks use declared nonuniform phase retiming: 0.25 s of initial quiet guard is reassigned to the demanding swing, approximately preserving contact time and preserving total cycle duration. Their scalar source speed is intentionally null; archive playback is 1.0×. Evaluation adds a 1 s hold for every family. They target the same fixed G1 head center analytically. No learned G1 policy, impact response, arbitrary requested TTC, or variable-frequency sequence has been validated by these isolated-cycle experiments.

Guard refinement and final checkpoint selection

The cross needed a maximum-joint guard-posture reward in addition to glove geometry: a small neck error failed the unchanged 0.12 rad return allowance. Run 5931870 checkpoint 100 passes all 32 selection trials, then all 64 fresh trials in 5931915. Its matched zero-residual baseline, 5931916, passes none.

Both retimed hooks pass all 32 selection trials at checkpoint 300 (right 5931833, left 5931834). Further right-hook guard-posture training in 5931884 produces a much larger supported recovery margin: checkpoint 100 raises the minimum guard dwell from 0.23 s to 1.755 s. It passes all 64 fresh trials in 5931918; its matched baseline 5931919 passes none.

Extra left-hook refinement in 5931885 improves joint-limit margin but increases foot drift. Checkpoint 100 passes with a smaller foot-drift margin; checkpoint 200 fails the unchanged 3 cm sole-drift gate in all 32 trials (33.38–38.29 mm). The earlier 5931834 checkpoint 300 is retained. Training is intentionally stopped once a selected policy has sufficient evidence; stop records preserve the reason rather than presenting superseded branches as successes.

The first right-hook fresh jobs 5931892/5931893 were cancelled before any test outcomes were inspected, because guard refinement was still being considered. Final right-hook testing uses a new seed-20261017 bank created after selecting 5931884 checkpoint 100. Cancelled jobs are not acceptance evidence.

Final verification

The complete preview-environment suite passes 133 tests and four subtests, with the Torch-dependent module skipped there. The pinned physics environment separately passes nine learning tests. Ruff and shell syntax checks pass. Packaging has its own evidence-schema tests and real four-family preflight; videos are fully decoded and selected contact/guard/fall stills are reviewed before inclusion. HTML validation is recorded separately by the master-report builder.

All four actors remain specialized reference trackers. This milestone completes the requested clock repair, jab improvement and three additional learned punches. Continuous mixed sequences, requested punch rates, arbitrary targets and the shared dynamic G1 scene remain subsequent work; clock compatibility alone does not resolve the global solver/cone difference.

An independent final audit of nominal and last test worlds per family verifies unchanged body arrays, exact original strength caps, native force-law agreement within 3.01×10⁻⁵ N·m, genuine simulator clock increments and bit-for-bit zero-residual drive reconstruction. See audit evidence. Final packaging also caught and repaired a nested retiming-metadata lookup; this affected evidence ingestion, not the accepted dynamics. All four real evaluation banks subsequently passed strict precision preflight.

Independent validation of the 5 ms learned punches

This log records candidate evaluations as they are completed. An evaluated checkpoint is not automatically a released policy. Every family uses 32 deterministic initial states generated with NumPy PCG64 seed 20261015: an exact guard trial and 31 trials with 0.005 rad hinge-position Gaussian perturbations and 0.02 generalized-velocity Gaussian perturbations. Root positions/orientations are unchanged. These exact arrays are not fed to gradient-update training. They are validation banks, because their results inform checkpoint/controller selection; they are not untouched final tests or unseen-motion generalization.

Early export fault: diagnostic only

Smoke job 5931602 used actual native affine PD at 5 ms, but mj_saveLastXML incorrectly serialized its actuator type/limit flags. The resulting runtime file could not reproduce the live actuator. Independent auditing exposed the problem; those files were retained, not repaired silently. A diagnostic reconstruction from the saved body and controller schema confirms bounded recorded motor output and zero root/external assistance, but deliberately fails the runtime-file provenance gate. Both the unlearned controller and the one-update PPO smoke controller fell. No accepted policy claim comes from this run.

The corrected exporter writes explicit actuator attributes and checks the recompiled model's geometry, dynamics, actuator fields, and solver settings against the live model. Subsequent validation jobs use that verified runtime XML and record its hash. See outputs/punch-5ms-5931602/schema-reconstruction-diagnostic.json and the controller report for details.

Full-speed jab: precise tracking exposed a wrist-limit problem

Both candidates below come from training job 5931617. They use the same body, 1× jab reference, 5 ms physics, 20 ms actor interval, and frozen 32-trial bank. Both passed all actual timing, no-fall, sole-drift, active-hand contact, supported guard recovery, bounded-force, and no-assistance gates in 32/32 trials. Neither passed the complete pilot, because of a soft joint-limit excursion.

Candidate Evaluation job All physical gates Full-cycle active-glove RMS Attack-window active-glove RMS Contact delay versus reference Maximum joint excursion
Update 100 5931645 18/32 14.37–15.99 mm 27.74–31.41 mm 33.97–43.95 ms 0.02225 rad
Update 400 5931667 0/32 4.03–4.73 mm 5.96–7.29 mm 1.14–3.61 ms 0.03210 rad

The timing estimates linearly interpolate the 0.14 m distance crossing between 5 ms FK samples. The second row is therefore best read as within approximately one physics tick, not as sub-millisecond ground truth. The 2 cm full-cycle RMS and 20 ms timing targets pass in all 32 update-400 trials; the attack-window RMS also lies well below 2 cm.

The failed joint is consistently LeftHand_z: it reaches the upper 35° wrist limit and overshoots during contact. At update 400, the nominal trial reaches 0.64127 rad at t = 1.210 s, exceeding the 0.610865 rad limit by 0.03040 rad; the declared tolerance remains 0.02 rad. At that time, the reference angle is already 0.60724 rad but its velocity reference is still +0.972 rad/s. Positive velocity feedforward near the stop is one plausible contributor. The remedy is being investigated through controller/training changes; the acceptance threshold is not relaxed.

This distinction matters: a high imitation reward and excellent glove tracking can coexist with a failed physical constraint. Update 600 later had worse online imitation reward than update 400, so training duration alone does not determine checkpoint selection.

Joint-limit penalty fine tuning

Run 5931681 fine-tunes the accurate update-400 jab policy with a penalty on actual joint-limit excursions sampled across the four physics steps per actor update. Its update-100 checkpoint was evaluated in job 5931694 with verified checkpoint, body, reference, and runtime hashes.

The result improves to 28/32 full pilot passes, with 32/32 joint-limit passes. Maximum joint excursion is 0.00621 rad; active-glove RMS is 3.55–4.21 mm over the complete cycle and 4.85–6.15 mm during the attack window. Interpolated contact error is within 3.31 ms in every trial. All trials remain upright, contact with the active hand, respect force bounds, and meet the actual 5 ms / 20 ms clocks.

The remaining four failures are specific: Chest-relative glove position enters and stays within the 3 cm return tolerance only 165–190 ms before the recording ends, short of the unchanged 200 ms dwell requirement. They are not falls, missed punches, torque violations, or an unquiet root. The evaluator now reports dwell for each guard component so this issue is visible independently of the aggregate gate. See the independent summary and guard-component diagnosis.

The update-200 checkpoint (evaluation 5931711) retains excellent attack precision and joint-limit compliance, but 0/32 complete guard recoveries. In the nominal trial, final Chest-relative glove errors are 30.44 / 30.94 mm, just beyond the 30 mm gate; the root itself returns within 3.23 mm and 0.020 rad. Full-cycle glove RMS remains only 3.17–3.91 mm across trials. This exposes a distinct reward gap: matching world-space glove positions does not ensure that the hands return to the correct position relative to the chest. The controller can redistribute torso and arm posture while preserving the fist's world position. An explicit guard-return objective is being considered; the gate and recording duration remain unchanged. See the update-200 summary.
